- 1.7.7, now "An extension that a node depends on stays active": a node outside the extension depends on it through a kind, a property or a type of link that the extension adds, or through a link to one of its nodes, and the procedure that disables the extension refuses to disable it. The extension's own nodes no longer keep it active, as in PostgreSQL.
The page that shows the base specification is now published at https://claude.ai/artifact/2oWUUA5dNyNiFdwmsmHfBF, replacing SRXVNo8oChYAhyHDHFJAVF, which the Artifact tool would not update until the agent had read all 647 KB of it.

Node 1.7.5 is now shown to the owner. The question still to be put to the owner when 1.7.6 is shown is what is to serve as the record of which owner changed the list of active extensions. The commit's author cannot serve as that record, since a participant who is not an owner and who merges an owner's branch would then have the merge refused. The reviewer recommends an entry that names the owner, as is done for the specification's approval status (10.6) and the strict flag (10.7), and adds a row to table 10.1 after row 10.1.27a. Follow-up changes to agreed nodes, to be proposed once 1.7.5 through 1.7.7 are agreed: 5.1 (the schema includes the schema of each active extension), 10.3 (`sheriff guard` also covers the list of active extensions), row 10.1.24 ("or of an active extension"), new rows of table 10.1 for the second paragraph of 1.7.5 and for the entries of 1.7.6, and "chosen" replaced with "active" in 2.3 and in the reason of 1.7.2. To be checked as well: 10.1.8, 1.3.7, 6.4 and 5.2, which treat the nodes of the SpecSheriff guidelines as a special case. The nodes of an active extension probably need the same treatment.

Before agreeing to 1.7.5, the owner asked (2026-11-18) why only the active extensions are listed, and how an extension works technically: whether it is a plugin of its own, with its own rules and scripts, loaded and stored somewhere, or a part of the SpecSheriff plugin that is not yet active. The agent gave the answer as a diagram on a page, extensions-runtime.html, published at https://claude.ai/artifact/MmfKFHwpwFQTyDuEX271Cs. An extension is part of the SpecSheriff plugin, as the agreed 1.7.1 says: it comes with every release of the plugin and stays inactive until an owner enables it in a project. In the plugin, each extension has a file of its own alongside the SpecSheriff guidelines, holding its rules, its part of the schema and its part of the appendix of the tools; the code of its checks is part of the SpecSheriff tools and runs only while the extension is active. The project's repository holds only the list of active extensions in `sheriff.config.json`, and no copy of the guidelines or of any extension (2.5). On every run, the tools read the list, compose the schema, run the checks of the guidelines and of the active extensions, and `sheriff guide` prints the rules of the active extensions with an index of the others. The available extensions are not listed in the project, since that list would become out of date after each update of the plugin; `sheriff settings` shows every extension of the release and marks the active ones. Node 1.7.5 is waiting for the owner's answer.

The owner then commented on the Russian text of 1.7.5 on the page of the base (2026-11-18): «доступные» on the word «активные» in its first sentence, and «только отмеченные как активные» on «только активные» in its second. Read together, the two comments ask that the configuration list the extensions available in the project and mark which of them are active, which is the owner's first proposal. The agent replied in the thread on the words «только активные» ("only the active ones") with one question: what makes an extension available in a project. If every extension the release delivers is available, the list duplicates what the plugin already contains, has to be rewritten after each update, and flips back and forth as participants on different releases rewrite it. If only those an owner has added are available, a third state appears, added but disabled, which no rule yet needs. The agent recommends keeping only the active extensions in the configuration, and will write the owner's wording as given if the owner identifies a use for that third state.

The owner answered (2026-11-18): while configuring, the user sees every available extension and marks those needed, which makes them active; that is what configuring means («При конфигурации пользователь должен видеть все доступные расширения. И среди них он отмечает те, которые ему нужны, и делает их таким образом активными»). This matches the agent's design: the plugin's release defines the available extensions, and the configuration stores the marks. The agent proposes a new wording of 1.7.5, "Available and active extensions", which keeps the owner's words «доступные» and «только отмеченные как активные»: the extensions that the installed release delivers are available to the project; the configuration marks as active those the project uses; only those marked as active apply in the project; the configuration marks only available extensions as active. The proposal is in the scratchpad file n175b.json, and it is written into newform.json only once the owner agrees to it.

The owner then dictated a text for 1.7.5 in place of the proposed one (2026-11-18): «При помощи утилиты, входящей в состав плагина, пользователь может произвести его конфигурирование, а именно отметить те расширения, которые он хочет использовать в своем проекте. В процессе конфигурации он видит все доступные расширения из текущей версии плагина. Среди которых отмечены те, которые он уже выбрал ранее. Пользователь в любой момент может отменить свой выбор и сделать расширение снова неактивным.» The agent's assessment, put to the owner before anything is written:
- the text describes how a tool of the plugin lets an owner make the choice, which belongs in the appendix of the tools, as a rule implementing 1.7.5;
- "the user" may change the choice, while the procedure on which the owner's text is based and the draft of 1.7.6 let only an owner of the specification do so, as 10.6 and 10.7 do for the approval status and the strict flag;
- "at any time" contradicts the draft of 1.7.7, under which an extension that a node depends on stays active;
- the text does not say that only the extensions marked as active apply in the project, nor that the marks are kept in the repository.
The agent recommends keeping in 1.7.5 the proposed wording of what applies in the project, and making the owner's text a rule of the appendix of the tools, with "the user" replaced by "an owner of the specification", the fragment «Среди которых» joined to the sentence before it, and the exception of 1.7.7 added.

The owner then judged that the first sentence of 1.7.5 said nothing beyond what 1.7.1 already says, and that the node should say who makes the choice and how, under the title "Configuring extensions" («Настройка расширений»). The agent merged the draft of 1.7.6 into 1.7.5 and asked whether the choice is made by any user or by an owner of the specification. The owner agreed to 1.7.5 "Configuring extensions" on 2026-11-18 («Окей, в таком виде согласую этот пункт»): an owner of the specification chooses which extensions the project uses, in person with a utility of the SpecSheriff plugin or through an agent working for that owner; the choice is recorded in the repository's configuration; an agent changes the choice only when that owner asks it to. fix-newform-470.mjs wrote the agreed text of 1.7.5 into newform.json and marked it as agreed, and remove-node.mjs removed the draft of 1.7.6 and renumbered 1.7.7 as 1.7.6. The page that shows the base specification is at version 2, and the owner's two comment threads on it are resolved. EngStickler's reader has read the English text of 1.7.5 (the file agreed-1-7-5-en.md in the scratchpad), and fix-newform-471.mjs and fix-newform-472.mjs corrected that English without changing its meaning.

The agent decided, treating it as a question of where a rule belongs, that the text the owner dictated about the utility (it shows every available extension, marks the ones chosen earlier, and lets an owner withdraw a choice except for an extension that a node depends on) is to become a rule of the appendix of the tools that implements 1.7.5, in the T25 step that changes the SpecSheriff tools. The record of who made the choice is to be an entry naming the owner, as is done for the approval status (10.6) and the strict flag (10.7), since a check on the commit's author would reject a merge made by a participant who is not an owner.

The owner is now shown 1.7.6 "An extension that a node depends on stays chosen" (s-j8y62pa9hzz6nwx), the former 1.7.7, which now refers to a chosen extension instead of an active one, as the agreed 1.7.5 and 2.3 do. Follow-up changes to agreed nodes, to be proposed once 1.7.6 is agreed: 5.1 (the schema includes the schema of each chosen extension), 10.3 (`sheriff guard` also covers the record of the choice), row 10.1.24 ("or of a chosen extension"), and new rows of table 10.1: each chosen extension is delivered by the installed release; each entry recording a choice names one of the specification's owners. Since 1.7.5 and 2.3 both refer to a choice, no renaming in 2.3 or 1.7.2 is needed. Still to be checked, whether any change is needed: 10.1.8, 1.3.7, 6.4 and 5.2.

On 2026-11-18 the owner then asked the agent to replace «узел вне этого расширения» in 1.7.6 with «узел спецификации». The agent pointed out that, under 1.1.2 and 2.3, the specification includes the guidelines and the extensions the project has chosen, so the extension's own nodes are nodes of the specification too, and, with that wording, the choice of an extension whose nodes link to one another could never be withdrawn. The agent proposed «узел спецификации, который не входит в это расширение», which keeps the owner's words and avoids «вне», a word the owner once read as "in breach of".

On 2026-11-18 the owner agreed to that wording («ок»), and so to 1.7.6; fix-newform-473.mjs wrote it and marked 1.7.6 as agreed. EngStickler's reader will then read the English text of 1.7.6.

fix-newform-474.mjs then wrote the follow-up proposals, now shown to the owner together as proposals of the same kind: 5.1 (the schema also includes the schema of each extension the project has chosen), 10.3 (an agent's edit is refused where it would change the record of the chosen extensions other than through the procedures that record the choice; the reason mentions choosing an extension), row 10.1.24 ("or of an extension that the project has chosen", as the agreed 2.3 says), new row 10.1.27b (each new entry recording a choice or its withdrawal names an owner of the specification; no commit removes or changes such an entry) and new row 10.1.27c (the installed release delivers every chosen extension; otherwise the check refuses the commit and names the extension). The table of checks is now published at https://claude.ai/artifact/Hku1cffdcWxNGnTUaqpJXH, replacing the page RjJ1KAjj2pvHxiPDmXppKF, which the Artifact tool refused to update until the agent had read the whole 467 KB page. The page that shows the base specification is at version 4.

The owner accepted the five proposals on 2026-11-18 («ок»): accept-proposal.mjs wrote the proposed text of 5.1 and 10.3 into their drafts, and the table now holds row 10.1.24 in its new wording and the agreed rows 10.1.27b and 10.1.27c. fix-newform-475.mjs then corrected the English of the agreed 1.7.6 after EngStickler's reader had read it ("withdraw" stays, as 1.3.7 uses it for approvals).

Next, the agent asked the owner about the rules that treat the nodes of the SpecSheriff guidelines as a special case, a treatment that the nodes of a chosen extension also need, since they also come approved with the plugin and are not the project's to change. They are 1.3.7 (nobody in a project withdraws the approval of those nodes), 2.5 (a project keeps no copy of them), 5.2 (a project's extension of the schema contradicts no schema that comes with them), 6.4 (no link from the project's nodes is written into them), 9.2 (no implementation is required of them), 9.7 (they list no code or tests of the project) and row 10.1.8 (their approval is not checked in the project). The agent recommends adding "or of an extension that the project has chosen" to each of them, rather than writing a single rule in 1.7 on which every such rule would depend, so that whoever implements one of them reads the exception in that rule.

On 2026-11-18 the owner chose the option of adding the clause to each rule («1»). fix-newform-476.mjs wrote the seven proposals: 1.3.7, 2.5 (with the title "How a project uses the SpecSheriff guidelines and their extensions"), 5.2, 6.4 (a node of a chosen extension holds only the links to it from that extension, since 1.7.3 and 1.7.4 rule out links from the guidelines or other extensions), 9.2, 9.7 ("or of an extension of the guidelines", since every extension is written where the guidelines are written) and row 10.1.8. They are shown together on the page chosen-extensions-proposals.html, published at https://claude.ai/artifact/36QfCBnEzayTC89x5iy2RS, each beside its current wording.

The owner accepted the seven proposals on 2026-11-18 («ок»): accept-proposal.mjs wrote 1.3.7, 2.5, 5.2, 6.4, 9.2 and 9.7 into their drafts, and row 10.1.8 holds its new wording; An EngStickler reader agent has read the English text of the seven changes (the file agreed-seven-en.md in the scratchpad). It found nothing to correct in the parts that changed. The work on how a project chooses an extension (1.7.5, 1.7.6 and their follow-up changes) is done.

The owner was then shown the proposal for the agreed 1.7.4 that specsheriff-rule-reviewer had left in the queue. fix-newform-477.mjs wrote it: the links that a rule of an extension declares are limited to the types defined by the guidelines or by that extension, since 6.1 lets an extension define types of link of its own. Next in the queue after it: the rewording of section 12 (RecordType) so that its rules name no concept of another extension, as the ruling of 2026-11-16 requires.

The owner accepted the proposal for 1.7.4 on 2026-11-18 («ок»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft. Subsection 1.7 is now done.

Next: the rewording of section 12 (RecordType) in domain-neutral wording, so that its rules name no domain, as the owner ruled on 2026-11-16 (the RecordType extension stands on its own; its rules mention no domain). The agreed nodes that name domains are 12.1, 12.4, 12.5, 12.6 and 12.12; 12.2 and 12.3 name none. The owner's rulings of 2026-11-14 apply: a RecordType governs only the data that a part of an application outputs and passes along; each format has exactly one part that outputs it. Each rewording is a proposal for an agreed node, shown to the owner one node at a time, starting with 12.1.

fix-newform-478.mjs wrote the proposal for 12.1, now shown to the owner. The owner accepted the proposal for 12.1 on 2026-11-18 («ок»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft. fix-newform-479.mjs then wrote the proposal for 12.4, now shown to the owner: the word "domain" becomes "a part of the system", with the same wording as in 12.1, and the example keeps its agreed text and opens with the same words as the example in 12.1.

draft-12-3-8.mjs then wrote the draft of 12.7, "One source for the values of a field of type `one of`", from the existing 1.27 (s-xfu2gpzddjnmgks), which the owner has not yet reviewed. On 2026-11-18 the owner chose to generalize "the names of the domains" («1»). fix-newform-485.mjs then wrote the list of 12.7: its own list, the names of the parts of the base specification, and the sids of the nodes of one kind ("NodeKind" was renamed, as the owner ruled on 2026-11-10); its text and reason are unchanged. 12.7 is to be submitted for the owner's agreement once the wording checker (the agent specsheriff-wording-checker) has read it. The owner agreed to 12.8 on 2026-11-18 («ок») after fix-newform-486.mjs had applied the wording checker's findings, and agree.mjs marked it as agreed.

Next came 12.9 (the existing 1.32, "Where a value is declared", s-xfwuvd3funutmsw): "A value of a field of type `one of` is declared in the part of the specification where the list, the domain or the node it comes from stands", with the reason that whoever is responsible for a list, a domain or a node declares the values coming from it and adds the RecordType variant for each of those values without changing another part. The agent proposes removing it. Under the agreed 12.7 the values come from the field's own list, which is declared with the field, or from the names of the parts (12.13) or the sids of the nodes of a project's own kind (12.8), which are read from the specification and not declared anywhere. So the text says nothing that 12.7 does not already say. Its reason contradicts the agreed 12.6 and the owner's ruling of 2026-11-16 that a RecordType variant is always declared in the same part as its RecordType. The owner agreed on 2026-11-18 («ок») to remove 12.9 (the existing 1.32), and remove-node.mjs removed it and renumbered the nodes after it. draft-12-9.mjs then wrote 12.9, "Gathered values are written beside their field" (the existing 1.33, s-ju5vcanbjph7mgh): the title says "are written beside" instead of "stand beside", and the reason no longer cites 11.1.2, a node of the Domains extension, which 1.7.4 forbids. The owner agreed to 12.9 on 2026-11-18 («ок»), and agree.mjs marked it as agreed.

Next came 12.10, the row reserved for the part of the existing 5.10 that concerns a RecordType. That part says three things: the reviewer is given `RecordType.fields`; the types and RuntimeChecks generated from the RecordTypes are reported to the reviewer as files left out of the change; and what a RecordType requires is treated as carried out by them. The agent proposed removing the row, because the agreed 10.4 already states all three in wording that applies to every kind of node. Under 10.4, the reviewer is given the wording of each rule, and the agreed 1.3.5 counts the fields that a node's kind adds, such as `RecordType.fields`, as part of its wording. 10.4 also requires the tools to tell the reviewer that each file left out of the change is generated from the specification. Under 10.4, the reviewer also treats what a rule requires of generated code as carried out by that code. A rule in the RecordType extension would only repeat this. The note in the list of changes in 10.4 that refers this part to "the extension for domains (12.3.15)" is then corrected to say that the general wording of 10.4 covers it.

The owner agreed on 2026-11-18 («ок») to remove row 12.10, and remove-node.mjs removed it and renumbered the rows after it: 12.11 and 12.12 are now 12.10 (What a RecordType may refer to) and 12.11 (Names of the parts of the base specification). fix-newform-489.mjs corrected the note in the list of changes in 10.4. It also replaced "considers what a rule requires of generated code to be carried out by that code" in the English of 10.4 with "treats what a rule requires of generated code as carried out by that code", as EngStickler's reader recommended. The meaning is unchanged, and so is the Russian text of 10.4. Every node of section 12 has now been agreed.

Next comes section 14, "Adoption of an existing project". Its only row, 14.1, was reserved for the part of the agreed 1.3.2 that concerned a project adopting the method: the clause of its first item that cites 9.5, and a paragraph of its reason about code and tests written before the project adopted the method. Neither remains to be moved:
- on 2026-11-17 the owner ruled that the rules on code without a SpecTag also apply to a new project, so 9.5 stays in the base and 1.3.2 keeps its clause;
- the owner's own rewording of the reason of 1.3.2 removed that paragraph.

The other rows planned for section 14 (from 5.2, 5.4 and 5.1) were removed on 2026-11-17 for the same reason. The agent proposed removing section 14, for two reasons. No rule applies only while an existing project adopts the method. Under the principles, an extension contains what prescribes how the system is organised, and adoption prescribes nothing of the kind.

The owner's adoption procedure is carried out by an agent, so it goes into the spec-adopt skill as part of the T25 step that changes the skills:
- only the legacy documentation becomes nodes;
- the legacy code is deleted or used as documentation;
- where the documentation and the code disagree, the owner is asked which logic the rule should state.

Step 5 of that skill, which has the agent add SpecTags to the existing code, is then to be brought into line with that procedure and with the agreed 9.5, whose reason names both ways as acceptable: code regenerated from the rules, or code given SpecTags. The last paragraph of step 5, on the rule "The specification is approved", is to be brought into line with the ruling on finding A.

The owner agreed on 2026-11-18 («ок») to remove section 14 and to describe the adoption procedure in the spec-adopt skill. remove-section-14.mjs removed section 14 and its row 14.1 from the page that shows the base specification. It also corrected the notes that still said a part of a node was to move to the Adoption extension: two items in the list of changes in 10.6, and row 49 of table 10.1, whose cost while the specification is a draft is now stated in 9.5. It took Adoption out of the extensions shown in extensions-runtime.html and in settings-prototype.mjs. The extensions of the guidelines are now 11 Domains, 12 RecordType and 13 Views of the specification.

The T25 step that changes the skills now includes changes to spec-adopt: adding the owner's adoption procedure and revising step 5 of that skill and its last paragraph, as described above.

Every draft on the page that shows the base specification has now been agreed. gen-table-10-1.mjs no longer offers Adoption as the destination of a row of table 10.1.

Next come the 20 rows of table 10.1 whose checks leave the table for an extension: 15 for RecordType and 5 for Domains. None of them has been agreed. Some of them check what the agreed drafts no longer require:

- row 26 mentions domains, and a rule of the RecordType extension must not mention them (1.7.4);
- the agreed 12.10 now says, without mentioning domains, what a RecordType may refer to.

Before the rows are shown one at a time, the owner is asked where an extension keeps its checks. The agent recommends that each extension keep its checks in one rule of its own: a table with the same columns as 10.1, which refines 10.1. Under the agreed 10.8, a project's own checks are already listed this way, in a rule of the project's own guidelines. The first sentence of 10.1 would then begin "Every check of the SpecSheriff guidelines", which also removes its conflict with 10.8. The other options are:
- the rows stay in 10.1 with a condition that names the extension, which 1.7.3 forbids;
- each check goes into the rule that it guards, in the extension, so a person asking whether a commit will pass the checks no longer finds the whole answer in one place, although 10.1's reason calls for exactly that.

The owner agreed on 2026-11-18 («ок») that each extension keeps its checks in one rule of its own, a table with the same columns as 10.1, which refines 10.1. The agent had quoted the first sentence of 10.1 from the draft of 10.1 in newform.json. The head of 10.1 that the owner agreed to on 2026-11-11 is in table-10-1.json, in the owner's words: "This table contains the complete list of the checks run after an edit to the specification or to the code, including its tests." The agent therefore proposes the same change to that sentence: "the complete list of the checks of the SpecSheriff guidelines that run after an edit…". Under the owner's rule, a change that only carries out a decision already made is shown as a proposal without a check by specsheriff-rule-reviewer.

The draft of 10.1 in newform.json and the head in table-10-1.json are two versions of the same node, s-jau82aq9juv9zzh, and they differ. The draft's reason keeps paragraphs that are omitted from the head the owner agreed to on 2026-11-11: drift is only reported, and code or a test without a SpecTag that a commit leaves unchanged is reported. The owner agreed to changes in those paragraphs on 2026-11-17. The two versions are to be reconciled when the drafts are moved into the specification files, and the result is to be shown to the owner.

The owner accepted the new wording of the head of 10.1 on 2026-11-18 («ок»): "This table contains the complete list of the checks of the SpecSheriff guidelines that run after an edit to the specification or to the code, including its tests." The head remains agreed (accept-head-10-1.mjs).

draft-11-3.mjs drafted 11.3, "The checks of the Domains extension" (s-rp2nxe3cga2p26x), in table-11-3.json, the only place where the node is recorded. specsheriff-rule-reviewer passed 11.3 subject to fixes, and revise-11-3.mjs applied them:
- the head cites 11.1.1 for the Domains extension, and its reason says "names no particular extension", as 1.7.3 does;
- the head's second sentence ("These checks run together with those listed in the table of checks") is cut, on the condition that 10.2 is changed to name the tables that refine 10.1;
- row 11.3.1 is split in two: "Every domain has a header", and a new row 11.3.2, "The index in each domain's header is the one built from the domain's sections", in the words of 11.1.4;
- the cycle row, now 11.3.4, is stated positively: "The domains can be ordered by the `consumes` links of their headers so that each domain comes after every domain it uses";
- row 11.3.5 is a proposal, not an unchanged wording: the approved row is "Every domain's header names a level the project's own guidelines list".

The rows whose wording changes are shown beside their approved wordings, as the owner's rule on changes to an approved wording requires. The reviewer found that no rule of the Domains extension no longer requires what row 40 of table 10.1 checks. The agreed 4.1 now says that an appendix section bears the title of the section of the base specification it implements, not a domain's name. Row 40, as worded, also rejects an appendix section implementing the contracts section. The agent therefore proposed keeping row 40 in table 10.1, as 10.1.22a, in more general wording: "Every section of the technical appendix other than the section on the stack and the technical debt section stands under the title of a top-level section of the base specification". This departs from the owner's ruling of 2026-11-11 that row 40 goes to the Domains extension, and the departure was put to the owner together with 11.3.

The reviewer's other findings, each to be put to the owner after 11.3:
- follow-up proposals for agreed nodes that name only the table of checks: 1.6.1 ("that the table of checks, or a table refining it, lists", and "unless the rule that lists the check says otherwise", the words of 10.8), 10.2 ("or in a table refining it" in the bullet on agents' edits, and "those tables" in the bullet on commits), and 10.6 ("or in a table refining it");
- a new node in 1.7, "The checks of an extension": "An extension of the guidelines (§s-mbmnsnxenmavu3z) lists all its checks in one `TableRule` of its own. That table refines the table of checks (§s-jau82aq9juv9zzh) and has the same columns." The reviewer wrote this wording, so another reviewer checks it before it is shown.

table-11-3.html is published at https://claude.ai/artifact/B8WiUDxxYsNkCfAoyYH15D.

Noted for later: the agreed 11.1.3 says "Domains do not form a cycle in which they use one another", a negation, like the old row; no row checks that a domain divided into several documents has one section per document (11.1.2); at the step that changes the SpecSheriff tools, the node of the appendix of the tools that checks that every domain has a header is to be named, since 7.1.9 only rebuilds the index.

The owner rejected table 11.3 as shown on 2026-11-18 («Нет, не принимаю»). Some of the agent's proposals did not keep the meaning of the approved rows they changed, and the owner asked for the rows to be agreed one at a time. The owner's rule "A table whose rows change is agreed one row at a time" was added to .claude/vt/rules.md the same day. revert-11-3.mjs restored the five approved rows as the rows of 11.3, numbered 11.3.1 to 11.3.5 (rows 20, 23, 25, 40 and 22 of table 10.1), each with its approved wording. Row 22 keeps, as a proposal, the change that follows the owner's decision of 2026-11-08 that a level is optional. Row 40 is back in 11.3, as the owner ruled on 2026-11-11, and the proposed row 10.1.22a is dropped. The head keeps the reviewer's fixes.

Now the head of 11.3 is shown to the owner on its own. Then each row is shown in its own message: first what the approved row means, worked out from its wording, from the rules it checks and from the owner's decisions on them, and only then the proposed wording, if any. Before a row is shown, the agent reads the approved nodes of the current spec.json that the row rests on, with their `meta.decisions`.

The owner agreed to the head of 11.3 on 2026-11-18 («шапку я, по-моему, уже согласовывал. Там банальная информация. Давай дальше»). The wording of the head merely followed from the owner's decision about the tables of checks, so the agent should have marked it as agreed and reported that in one line. The owner's rule on tables whose rows change now says so.

Row 11.3.1 is shown now. The approved row reads "Every domain has a header, and the index in it is what the domain's sections produce | refused". It rests on the approved 1.38 (s-yggv9myedprnaa7): a domain's specification opens with its header, and the header carries the index of the domain's sections, built from them and never written by hand. The owner ruled on 2026-10-30 that every domain opens with its header, which alone gives the domain its level and its place on the map. The proposal splits the row in two without changing its meaning: 11.3.1 "Every domain has a header", and a new 11.3.2 "The index in each domain's header is the one computed from the domain's sections". "Computed from" follows the owner's wording of the agreed 10.1.16 («вычисляются из исходящих»).

The owner agreed to row 11.3.1, "Every domain has a header", on 2026-11-18 («я согласен с тем названием, которое ты предлагаешь… Коротко, так как оно есть»). The owner also said what a header is: an auxiliary part of the domain that holds information about the domain itself, such as a brief description, the level assigned to the domain and a list of the titles of its sections. agree-11-3-1.mjs recorded the row as agreed and kept that explanation with the row, so that it can be recorded later in the `meta.decisions` field of the row's node. It added the new row 11.3.2, the second half of the approved row, and renumbered the rows after it: 11.3.3 is the row on the `consumes` link, 11.3.4 is the row on domains that use one another in a cycle, 11.3.5 is the row on the sections of the technical appendix (row 40 of table 10.1), and 11.3.6 is the row on a domain's level.

Row 11.3.2 is shown now: "The index in each domain's header is the one computed from the domain's sections". The agreed 11.1.3 says that the header always carries the index, while the owner said that the header may hold a list of the titles of its sections. The message showing 11.3.2 states this, so that the owner can say whether the index is required.

The owner ruled on 2026-11-18 that the index is required where a domain has sections, since a small domain may have none («Указатель обязателен, если есть разделы. Теоретически у нас может быть домен, который очень маленький и который не содержит разделов»). No agreed rule requires a domain to have sections. propose-11-3-2.mjs narrowed row 11.3.2 to "The header of each domain that has sections carries the index computed from those sections". It also recorded a proposal for the agreed 11.1.3, which follows from the same ruling: "Where the domain has sections, the header also carries their index" instead of "The header also carries the index of the domain's sections". Row 11.3.2 and the proposal for 11.1.3 are shown together, as one question.

The owner then asked why two nodes are needed, since row 11.3.2 and the proposed sentence of 11.1.3 say practically the same thing («обе строки говорят практически одно и то же. Зачем нам их две?»). The agent answered that the two are nodes of different kinds. The sentence in 11.1.3 states the requirement. Row 11.3.2 is the check in the table of checks: it says that the SpecSheriff tools verify that requirement and what a failure costs. Table 10.1 is built the same way, and the owner agreed to such pairs on 2026-11-13: row 10.1.18 "Every section contains at least one rule" checks 8.1, and row 10.1.16 "The incoming links are those computed from the outgoing ones" checks 6.4. The agent asked whether to keep both nodes, as in table 10.1.

The owner agreed to keep both nodes on 2026-11-18 («Окей, оставляем - согласованно. Давай следующую пару»). agree-11-3-2.mjs marked row 11.3.2 agreed and wrote the accepted sentence into 11.1.3, which remains agreed.

Row 11.3.3 was shown next: "A `consumes` link goes from a domain's header to a section of another domain | refused". It checks the ends of every `consumes` link: the link starts at a domain's header and names a section, not a rule, of a different domain. It rests on the agreed 11.1.3, whose sentence "The header's `consumes` links name the sections of other domains the domain uses" is the requirement. The owner's rulings on the link: links between rules cross domain boundaries freely and are checked only for whether they name a node (2026-10-26, in the approved 2.3); a cycle of domains is detected from the `consumes` links alone (2026-10-30, in the approved 1.41). The approved 2.2 defined the `consumes` link type, and the agreed 6.1 no longer defines it, as 1.7.3 requires, so the sentence in 11.1.3 is now its only definition. The agent proposes keeping the approved wording unchanged.

The owner agreed to row 11.3.3 unchanged on 2026-11-18 («ок»), and step-11-3-4.mjs marked it agreed.

Row 11.3.4 is being shown now. The approved row reads "The domains do not use one another in a cycle | refused". It rests on the agreed 11.1.3, which says "Domains do not form a cycle in which they use one another". It also says that a notification sent through an event does not make the sender a user of the receiving domain. Its reason: a domain cannot work without the domains it uses, so domains that use one another cyclically cannot be built one after another. Under the owner's ruling of 2026-10-30 (in the approved 1.41), a cycle is still refused, and it is detected from the `consumes` links of the domains' headers alone. The agent proposes adding that clarification: "The domains do not use one another in a cycle through the `consumes` links of their headers". The negative wording is kept, as in the agreed 11.1.3. The positive equivalent, that the domains can be put in order, was part of the table the owner rejected.

The owner accepted row 11.3.4 with that clarification on 2026-11-18 («ок»).

Row 11.3.5 is being shown now. The approved row reads "Every section of the technical appendix but those of the stack and of the technical debt stands under the name of a domain | refused". Its meaning, taken by the agent from the approved 1.19 (s-vj7ercem526zd6v) and its decision of 2026-10-31, was this: the technical appendix is divided as the base specification is, with a section, named after the domain, for each domain it implements, a section for the stack and a section for the technical debt. The node of the appendix of the tools that implements the row (tools 7.1.22) checks that the name of each file of the technical appendix, other than `stack.json` and `debt.json`, is among the names of the domains. On 2026-11-11 the owner restored this meaning and sent the row to the Domains extension, the agreed 1.1.2 stating the general rule. 4.1, agreed on 2026-11-17, gives the appendix a section for each top-level section of the base specification that it implements, and in a specification divided into domains the top-level sections are the domains and the contracts section (11.1.1). As approved, the row makes the check refuse an appendix section named after the contracts section. The agent proposed "Every section of the technical appendix other than the section on the stack and the technical debt section bears the name of a domain or of the contracts section", and told the owner that adding the contracts section to the permitted names changes the meaning of the row.

The owner rejected that proposal on 2026-11-18: the agent had misunderstood the row, and it has nothing to do with the contracts section. The owner explained what the row means. The technical appendix refines how the rules of the base specification are implemented: it answers the question "how". So its structure matches that of the base specification, including its domains and their sections. The match is not complete: it goes only as far as needed:
- a base rule that needs no refinement gets no counterpart in the appendix, so no empty rule is added there;
- the stack and the technical debt have no counterpart in the base.

step-11-3-5b.mjs replaced the proposal for row 11.3.5 with one that expresses that meaning: "Every section of the technical appendix other than the stack section and the technical debt section has the same name and the same place in the structure as a domain or a section of the base specification". The check runs in one direction only: every section of the appendix has a counterpart in the base, and not the other way round. The owner's explanation is kept with the row so that it can later be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of its node. Without the Domains extension, no row of table 10.1 checks that the appendix matches the structure of the base, which the agreed 1.1.2 requires; this gap is noted and will be raised with the owner after table 11.3 is agreed.

The owner agreed to row 11.3.5 as worded in the replacement proposal on 2026-11-18 («ок»).

Row 11.3.6, the last row of 11.3, is being shown now. The approved row reads "Every domain's header names a level the project's own guidelines list | refused". It rests on the approved 1.40, under which every domain is at one of the levels that the owners of the specification list in the project's own guidelines. On 2026-11-08 the owner made it optional for a domain to have a level, and the agreed 11.2.1 says that a domain's header may name one of those levels. The proposal: "A domain's header that names a level names one of the levels that the project's own guidelines list". A header that names no level passes the check.

The owner then corrected the wording on 2026-11-18. A domain's header contains a level rather than naming it («Заголовок домена уровень не называет, а содержит»). The owner proposed that the row be worded as "the header of a domain may contain one of the levels, if the project's own guidelines define levels", since a level is optional. The owner also recalled that levels appear only when the corresponding extension is active, and asked that the information on levels be tied to that extension. Levels belong to 11.2 "Hierarchy", which is part of the Domains extension, so the check of the row, which is kept in table 11.3 of the Domains extension, runs only while that extension is active.

step-11-3-6b.mjs replaced the proposal for row 11.3.6 with the owner's wording: "A domain's header may contain one of the levels, if the project's own guidelines define levels". The check refuses a header that contains a level absent from the list in the project's own guidelines, or a header that contains a level when no list is defined. Because the owner's remark changes the verb, the script also replaced "names" with "contains" where the verb applies to a level, in the agreed 11.2.1 and 11.2.2, which remain agreed, and this is reported to the owner in one line.

The owner agreed to row 11.3.6 in the owner's wording on 2026-11-18 («ок»). Every node of 11.3 has now been agreed: its head and its six rows. agree-11-3-6.mjs recorded this, and gave row 11.3.5 the English wording that EngStickler's reader recommended, with its meaning and its Russian version unchanged. Its English wording now reads: "Every section of the technical appendix other than the stack section and the technical debt section has the same name and the same place in the structure as a domain or a section of the base specification".

Next come the follow-up proposals for the agreed 1.6.1, 10.2 and 10.6, which concern only the table of checks. They follow from the owner's decision that each extension lists its checks in a table of its own that refines 10.1, so they are shown to the owner together, without a check by specsheriff-rule-reviewer:
- 1.6.1: "that the table of checks, or a table refining it, lists", and "unless the table that lists the check says otherwise";
- 10.2: "every check in the table of checks or in a table refining it that needs neither…", and "every check in those tables except…";
- 10.6: "the checks listed in the table of checks or in a table refining it".
agree-11-3-6.mjs recorded them in the `proposals` of those drafts. The sentence "These checks run together with those listed in the table of checks" was removed from the head of 11.3 in advance, since 10.2 was to be changed to name the tables that refine 10.1.

After these proposals, in this order: the new node in 1.7, "The checks of an extension", which another reviewer is to check, since the first reviewer wrote it; then table 12.12 of the RecordType extension, with its head marked agreed and reported in one line, and its 15 rows taken one at a time; then the missing row for 11.1.2 (one section per document). No row of table 10.1 is to be proposed for 1.1.2: on 2026-11-11 the owner moved the check of the structure of the appendix to the Domains extension, because the agreed 1.1.2 already states the rule in the guidelines.

The owner accepted the proposals for 1.6.1, 10.2 and 10.6 on 2026-11-18 («ок»), and accept-tables-followups.mjs wrote them into the drafts, which remain agreed.

draft-1-7-7-and-12-12.mjs then drafted two nodes:
- 1.7.7, "The checks of an extension" (s-gpzs5fcxvp6jkrp): "An extension of the guidelines that adds checks lists all of them in one `TableRule` of its own. That table refines the table of checks and has the same columns as the table of checks." specsheriff-rule-reviewer is checking it, working from the brief brief-1-7-7.md in the scratchpad.
- 12.12, "The checks of the RecordType extension" (s-3k8mvhq2xbn5wpz), in table-12-12.json. Its head repeats the head of 11.3, with the RecordType extension in place of the Domains extension, so it is marked as agreed under the owner's rule, and this is reported in one line. Its 15 rows are rows 8, 38, 35, 27, 28, 29, 30, 31, 32, 36, 33, 34, 37, 21 and 26 of table 10.1, numbered 12.12.1 to 12.12.15 in the order of the rules of section 12, each in its approved wording.

Row 12.12.1 is being shown now. The approved row reads "No two RecordTypes share a name, whatever the case of its letters | refused" (quoted as approved). It checks the agreed 12.1, "RecordType names are unique across the specification"; the owner ruled on 2026-10-30 (in the approved 5.1) that terms and RecordTypes each have a name that no other has, regardless of letter case, and 7.1.3 of the appendix of the tools says that the names are compared in lower case. The proposal states it positively, in the words of 12.1: "RecordType names are unique across the specification, regardless of letter case". A matching proposal adds "regardless of letter case" to the sentence in 12.1, which does not yet say so. Both are shown as one question.

The owner agreed to row 12.12.1 and to the change to 12.1 on 2026-11-18 («ок»). step-12-12-2.mjs wrote them, with "regardless of letter case" in the English wording, since "whatever the case" can also be read as the idiom "in any event"; the Russian wording is unchanged.

Row 12.12.2 is being shown now. The approved row reads "A field of type `identifier of` names a RecordType that names the field identifying it, one of its fields, and the identifying fields it leads to, followed from one identifier to the next, end in a field of another type | refused". It rests on two rulings the owner made on 2026-10-31. An identifier has the type of the field that identifies the data records it refers to, and the RecordType of those data records names that field (in the agreed 12.2). Identifiers that form a chain, from one identifying field to the next, leading back to a RecordType already visited have no type that can be assigned to them in the implementation, so such a chain is refused. The approved row contains two requirements, each of which can be met without the other, so the proposal is to split it, as row 11.3.1 was split:
- 12.12.2: "A field of type `identifier of` refers to a RecordType that names one of its own fields as the field identifying its data records";
- 12.12.3, new: "A chain of identifiers, followed from each identifying field to the next, ends in a field of a type other than `identifier of`".
The rows after it are renumbered 12.12.4 to 12.12.16.

The second reviewer passed 1.7.7 subject to fixes, and fix-1-7-7-and-12-12-3.mjs applied them to the draft, which is not yet agreed: the text cites 8.6, "A rule may be a table", for `TableRule` and states the columns in a separate sentence, and the reason's second sentence reuses, word for word, the agreed wording of the reasons of 10.1 and 11.3. The reviewer's doubts are kept with the draft, to be shown with it: no row of the table of checks verifies 1.7.7, and the reviewer proposes an example. The reviewer also made two recommendations for later. When the two versions of 10.1 are reconciled, the head's reason is to be kept rather than the draft's. When the heads of 11.3 and 12.12 next change, their reasons are to cite 1.7.7 instead of restating it. 1.7.7 is shown after rows 12.12.2 and 12.12.3.

The owner then asked what values a field of type `identifier of` can hold («Нужно понять, какие значения может содержать это поле»). The agent answered on the basis of node 20 of the appendix of the tools. The row of its table for `identifier of` says that such a field has the same type as the field that identifies the data records of that RecordType. A field of type `identifier of` holds the identifier of one data record of the RecordType it refers to. Its values have the type of that RecordType's identifying field, which the RecordType names in `identifiedBy`. Where that field is itself of type `identifier of`, the type is taken from the next RecordType, and so on along the chain. Whether a data record with that identifier exists is a matter for the running system, not for the checks of the specification.

The owner still did not see why such a field is needed, and asked for a JSON object that makes its meaning clear («Попробуй показать мне некий JSON-объект…»). The agent showed the worked example in the saloon repository: the RecordType "Tab" is identified by its field `regular`, of type text, and the RecordType "Journal entry" has a field `regular` of type `identifier of` "Tab". It also showed a data record of each. The agent explained what the type is for. The type declares that the field refers to a data record of another RecordType rather than holding just any text. The type of the field's values is taken from that RecordType's identifying field, so the type of the field and the type of the identifying field cannot drift apart. The SpecSheriff tools can check that the reference is well-formed in the specification.

The owner agreed to the split of row 12.12.2 on 2026-11-19 («ок»), and step-1-7-7.mjs marked rows 12.12.2 and 12.12.3 as agreed.

1.7.7, "The checks of an extension", is being shown now, in the wording that the second reviewer accepted, with the example the reviewer proposed. The example is reworded so that each sentence states one thought. The reviewer's other concern, that no row of the main table of checks verifies 1.7.7, is put to the owner after 1.7.7, as a possible row of table 10.1: "Every `TableRule` that refines the table of checks has the same columns as the table of checks". After that comes row 12.12.4.

Instead of answering the question about 1.7.7, the owner proposed new names on 2026-11-19: "the main table of checks" («основная таблица проверок») for 10.1, and an additional table of checks of an extension, or an extension's own table of checks («дополнительная или собственная таблица проверок расширения»), for an extension's table of checks, since "the table of checks" had come to mean either of the two. The term «основная таблица проверок» was added to terms-ru.md as the owner's. The agent recommended "the table of checks of an extension" («таблица проверок расширения») without either adjective: «собственная» would be confused with the word «собственные» in a project's own checks (10.8) and in a project's own guidelines, and «дополнительная» would also fit the checks that a project's own guidelines add. Once the owner has settled on the second name, the agent renames the term in every agreed node that uses it and reports the change in one line: the title of 10.1, 1.6.1, 10.2, 10.6, 10.8, the heads (title, text and reason) of tables 11.3 and 12.12, and the draft of 1.7.7, which will then define the name of an extension's table. Then the agent asks the owner about 1.7.7 again.

The owner accepted both names on 2026-11-19 («ок»): "the main table of checks" («основная таблица проверок») and "the table of checks of an extension" («таблица проверок расширения»). Both are in terms-ru.md as the owner's terms. rename-tables-of-checks.mjs renamed the term in the agreed nodes, which keep their agreed status: the title of 10.1 and the wording of 1.6.1, 10.2, 10.6 and 10.8, and the titles and reasons of the heads of 11.3 and 12.12, whose titles now read "The table of checks of the Domains extension" and "The table of checks of the RecordType extension". In the draft of 1.7.7 the title is now "The table of checks of an extension", and the node introduces the name. 1.7.7 is shown to the owner again.

The owner agreed to 1.7.7 with its example on 2026-11-19 («ок»). The agent does not propose a row of the main table of checks that would verify 1.7.7. The tables of checks of the extensions ship with the plugin, and no project can edit them, so such a check would always pass in a project. The tests of the SpecSheriff tools verify the columns of those tables instead.

Row 12.12.4 is being shown now. The approved row reads "A RecordType holds at most one field of type `variant`, the ones it inherits counted | refused". A field of type `variant` is the field in which a data record holds the fields of its RecordType variant. Node 20 of the appendix of the tools defines it as an object holding those fields. The schema allows at most one such field in a RecordType, and only in one that names the fields that select its variant (`chosenBy`). The owner ruled on 2026-10-31 (in the approved 1.26) that a data record holds at most one field of type `variant`; with two such fields, it is undetermined which of them holds the fields of the chosen variant. No agreed node of section 12 mentions a field of type `variant`. The agent proposes rewording the row as "A RecordType holds at most one field of type `variant`, including the fields it inherits from the RecordTypes it refines", and adding to the agreed 12.6 a sentence that records the ruling: "A data record holds the fields of its RecordType variant in the single field of type `variant` of its RecordType". Both are shown as one question, with an example in JSON, since the owner asked for one for row 12.12.2.

The owner then asked on 2026-11-19 whether the inheritance in the proposed row violates a principle. As the owner remembered, every RecordType stands on its own, two RecordTypes never link to or refer to each other, and the only link allowed is a RecordType's inclusion of a fragment of a format from the contracts section. The agent answered on the basis of the agreed nodes. 12.10 (agreed 2026-11-18) lets a RecordType refer, in the types of its fields and through `refines`, to the RecordTypes of the part of the system that outputs its data, and to fragments in the contracts section. The owner's ruling of 2026-11-14 forbids links between the RecordTypes of different parts of the system. 12.6 (agreed 2026-11-18) makes a RecordType variant a RecordType of the same section with a `refines` link to the RecordType it is a variant of. The inheritance in row 12.12.4 is nothing more than that: a RecordType variant takes the fields of the RecordType it refines, within one part of the system. The agent asked whether the owner's principle is meant to apply only between parts of the system, as 12.10 states it, or also within one part, which would require reworking 12.6 and 12.10.

The owner asked what the agent means by "a part of the system". No agreed node defines "a part of the system", although 12.1, 12.4, 12.5 and 12.10 use it. The agent answered with its reading. A part of the system is anything that outputs data, keeps it in its own store with its own API (12.1), and is described by one top-level section of the base specification. In a specification divided into domains, it is a domain. In a specification without domains, it can be, for example, one stage of a pipeline, as the owner described on 2026-11-16. Under 12.1, a RecordType is declared in the section that describes where its data first appears. The agent asked whether this is what the owner meant, and noted that the term is to be defined in 12.1 without naming domains, as 1.7.4 requires of a rule of the RecordType extension.

The owner confirmed this reading on 2026-11-19: in an implementation, a part of the system is most often a module; in the specification, it is a section or a domain («в минимальном исполнении чаще всего модуль, а в спецификации это, соответственно, раздел, или домен»). So the owner's principle applies only between parts of the system, as 12.10 states it, and RecordTypes within one part may refer to one another. propose-12-1-part.mjs recorded a proposal for the agreed 12.1: a new paragraph after its first paragraph: "A part of the system is what one top-level section of the base specification describes. Most often it is a module of the system." It names no domain, as 1.7.4 requires. In a specification with domains, the top-level sections of the base specification are the domains and the contracts section, as in the agreed 4.1. The owner's explanation is kept in the draft's `decisions`. Once the owner has decided on this proposal, the agent will ask again about row 12.12.4 and the sentence for 12.6.

The owner refined the definition on 2026-11-19: at the level of the documentation, that is, the specification, a part of the system may be a section or a domain; at the level of the code, a module or a component. propose-12-1-part-b.mjs rewrote the proposal for 12.1: "In the specification, a part of the system is described by one top-level section of the base specification. In the code, a part of the system is a module or a component." The word "domain" was left out of the text, because 1.7.4 allows a rule of the RecordType extension to use only the words that the SpecSheriff guidelines or that extension define. A domain still falls under the definition because, as in the agreed 4.1, it is a top-level section of the base specification. The agent explained to the owner why the word was left out and asked the question about 12.1 again.

The owner answered on 2026-11-19 that the definition may use general words: "section", "domain", "module" and "component" are commonly used words, used here in their general sense and not as entities that the specification defines. 1.7.4 applies only to the words that take their meaning from the specification, so it does not forbid the word "domain". propose-12-1-part-c.mjs rewrote the proposal for 12.1 in the owner's words: "In the specification, a part of the system is described by a top-level section of the base specification or by a domain. In the code, a part of the system is a module or a component." The owner's ruling is kept in the draft's `decisions`, so that a later review does not flag the word under 1.7.4.

The owner accepted the definition on 2026-11-19 («ок»), and accept-12-1-part.mjs wrote it into 12.1, which remains agreed. The owner's question about links between RecordTypes is thereby settled: they are allowed within one part of the system, as 12.10 says, and the inheritance in row 12.12.4 stays within one part. Row 12.12.4 and the sentence for 12.6 are put to the owner again, unchanged.

The owner then noted that the proposed row 12.12.4 contains the word "RecordType" twice, which is redundant. Instead, the agent proposed the Russian wording that the owner had approved, «RecordType содержит не более одного поля типа `variant`, считая унаследованные», and the English wording "A RecordType holds at most one field of type `variant`, including inherited ones". Which RecordType the fields are inherited from is clear from 12.6, under which a RecordType variant has a `refines` link to the RecordType it is a variant of.

The owner agreed to row 12.12.4 and to the paragraph for 12.6 on 2026-11-19 («ок»). step-12-12-5.mjs marked the row agreed and wrote the paragraph into 12.6, which remains agreed.

Row 12.12.5 is being shown now. The approved row reads "A RecordType variant refines the RecordType it is a variant of | refused". It enforces the agreed 12.6, under which a RecordType variant has a `refines` link to the RecordType it is a variant of. Through that link the variant inherits the fields of its RecordType, and the SpecSheriff tools find which RecordType it is a variant of. The agent proposes keeping the approved wording. The next row, 12.12.6, still states the old rule that a variant is in the part of the specification that declares its value; the agreed 12.6 replaced that rule, so the row will need a new wording.

The owner found row 12.12.5 tautological: a variant refines the RecordType it is a variant of by definition («масло масляное»). What the SpecSheriff tools actually check is that a RecordType that names the values it corresponds to (`answersTo` in the schema), that is, one declared as a variant, has a `refines` link: validate.ts refuses "a RecordType variant that refines no RecordType". The schema does not require that link. The agent proposed "A RecordType that names the values it corresponds to has a `refines` link"; which RecordType the link points to is checked by row 12.12.8 (the agent first said 12.12.9 by mistake).

The owner could not understand that wording: the variant had disappeared from it, and a `refines` link had appeared without it being clear how it related to the variant. The owner asked for the purpose of a variant to be stated first, and the checks to be derived from it («нужно написать, для чего предназначен вариант. И, исходя из этого, уже делать проверки»). The agent took the purpose from the agreed 12.6: a RecordType variant declares the fields that a data record holds because of the values of some fields of its RecordType. It then listed the requirements that follow from that purpose, with the row of table 12.12 that checks each:
- the variant adds fields of its own; otherwise it is not needed (12.6; row 12.12.7);
- the variant names the RecordType whose fields it extends, through a `refines` link to that RecordType (12.6; row 12.12.5, now proposed as "A RecordType variant has a `refines` link to the RecordType whose fields it extends");
- the variant names the values it applies to, one value of each field that its RecordType names as selecting the variant (12.6; row 12.12.8);
- no two variants of one RecordType apply to the same values (12.6; row 12.12.9);
- the variant is declared where its RecordType is declared: in the same section as its RecordType, or in the contracts section when its RecordType is declared there (12.6; row 12.12.6, which still states the old rule and needs a new wording);
- a data record holds the fields of its variant in its single field of type `variant` (12.6; row 12.12.4, agreed).

The agent asked whether the owner agrees with this set of checks, before their wordings are shown one at a time.

The owner agreed to that set of checks on 2026-11-19 («Окей, согласен») and ended the session.

Once the owner agrees to the procedure, the agent drafts the change: a sentence in 1.7.1 or a new node. The agent specsheriff-rule-reviewer checks the draft before it is shown to the owner. The draft is assessed against 2.3 and 2.4. Under 2.4, a value that may differ between projects is specified only in a project's own guidelines.

**NOW (2026-11-17):** finding A is settled, and the nodes that record the owner's decisions are being shown to the owner one at a time. The owner's decisions of 2026-11-17:
- A section has no approval status that its owners set by hand. Its approval status stays derived from its rules.
- The specification's approval status determines whether the tests run on a push («Да, нужно развязать»).
- The specification may be approved while some code has no SpecTag. The approval procedure shows the approving owner what the checks report, and records the approval only after that owner confirms it.
- A commit that adds or changes code without a SpecTag is refused, as it is now.
- A commit is accepted even while code outside the commit has no SpecTag. While the specification is approved, the checks issue a single warning about all such code, with no list. The warning names a command of the SpecSheriff tools that lists that code by file and symbol («Коммит проходит, а проверка выводит предупреждение - без списка»).
- While the specification is a draft, that check for SpecTags does not run, so there is no warning. Once the owners have approved the specification despite the code's incomplete conformance to the rules, the warning reminds them that code remains to be brought into line with the rules: regenerated from the rules, or given SpecTags («Проверка имеет смысл только если мы перевели спецификацию в статус аппрув несмотря на предупреждение о неполном соответствии»).

How the owner reached these decisions. The agent's first draft of 9.5 required the checks to report every symbol outside the commit that has no SpecTag, regardless of the specification's approval status, and the owner rejected it as a gap in the method («дыру размером в галактику»). The agent then showed, from the rules and from the code in skills/tools/src/binding.ts, that while the specification is approved the check for SpecTags reads the whole repository, so one old file without SpecTags causes every commit to be refused. The owner chose a single warning instead, issued only while the specification is approved.

The owner's reasoning, which is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 10.6 and 9.5 when the drafts are moved into the specification files, is as follows. The owners set the specification's approval status on the basis of what they see. Old code without a SpecTag may work correctly, and so may old tests. Binding old code to rules requires that every rule be approved first and that a dedicated tool exist, both outside the current tasks.

**The owner's proposal, now under discussion (2026-11-17):** the specification's `strict` flag, false by default. While it is true and the specification is approved, the check refuses a commit if code outside the commit does not conform to the specification; the flag sets how far the ban on non-conformance reaches («добавить спецификации флаг strict (по умолчанию false)… Тоесть он дейстувует на размер области запрета несоответсвия спецификации»). The owner asked to be questioned with the skill `grilling`: one question at a time, each with the agent's recommended answer.

- Question 1: where the flag is set and who specifies its default. The agent recommended setting the flag in a rule of the project's own guidelines, since node 2.4 lets only a project's own guidelines specify a value that varies between projects, or a default for it. The owner answered that the flag belongs at the same level as the specification's approval status, with its default in the schema («флаг по идее должен быть на том же уровне где флаг статуса спецификации. Его значение по умолчанию можно указать в схеме»). The agent's interpretation: only an owner changes the flag, in an entry that names that owner, as with the specification's approval status, and the flag is false until the first such entry.
- The owner's ruling on defaults (2026-11-17): the rules of the SpecSheriff guidelines state no default approval status; only the schema holds it («я бы статусы по умолчанию вообще не фиксировал в правилах гайдлайна… а только в схеме»). So 2.4, which is about rules, needs no clarification. fix-newform-422.mjs removed "`draft` is the default" from 1.3.1, which remains agreed; 1.3.2 still says that whatever is not approved is a draft. In the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, the schema is to declare `draft` as the default value of an approval status and `false` as the default value of the `strict` flag.
- Question 2: what the flag makes the check refuse. The owner agreed to the agent's recommendation («согласен с рекомендацией»): while the flag is true and the specification is approved, the check refuses a commit only because symbols or tests outside it carry no SpecTag, which is what 9.5 warns about. The other reports of the checks stay reports, since a rule is approved first and implemented afterwards, and refusing a commit because of an approved rule that has no code yet would refuse every commit until that code is written.
- Question 3: what happens when an owner turns the flag on while code outside the commit still carries no SpecTag. The owner chose to have the procedure refuse («Отказ»), as the agent recommended: the procedure does not turn the flag on, and the flag can be turned on once no code without a SpecTag is left. The flag then keeps a project that has no code without a SpecTag from getting such code.
- Question 4: what the refusal of a commit shows while the flag is on. The agent recommended listing the files and symbols. The owner ruled otherwise: the refusal, like the warning in 9.5, names the command that lists that code, so that the command stays independent of the checks and can be extended on its own; the flag changes only the scope of the check: the commit alone or the whole repository («Разница должна быть только в области проверке между только коммит и все репо»). By the same ruling, the refusal to turn the flag on names that command too.
- The summary, which the owner confirmed on 2026-11-17 («все верно»): (1) only an owner changes the flag, in an entry that names that owner, and the latest entry sets it; its default `false` is in the schema; (2) while the specification is approved, the check for SpecTags refuses a commit only because of code without a SpecTag that is in the commit when the flag is off, and because of such code anywhere in the repository when it is on; when the flag is off, such code outside the commit gets the single warning of 9.5; (3) only symbols and tests without a SpecTag count toward a refusal, and the other reports cause no refusal; (4) the flag cannot be turned on while any code without a SpecTag remains; (5) every message about such code names the command that lists it, and no message lists the code itself; (6) while the specification is a draft, the flag has no effect.

Finding 11 (node 1.4.2) proposed that a test of technical code, which implements none of the system's logic (1.2.7), may carry a SpecTag that names no node. The owner rejected the proposal on 2026-11-17, replying "ok" to the agent's recommendation to reject it, and 1.4.2 stays as it is. The proposal contradicts two principles: "Rule, test and code bound by the sid", under which every test names the rule it is written from, and "The specification first, in one direction". Where the behaviour of technical code is worth testing, it can be stated as a rule of the technical appendix, and the test names that rule. Otherwise the technical code is tested by the tests of the rules whose code uses it. This ruling is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 1.4.2 (s-nrjxfvpkq3m8hzm) when the drafts are moved into the specification files. fix-newform-436.mjs and fix-table-135.mjs then brought the English wordings agreed on 2026-11-17 into line with their Russian text, after EngStickler's readers had read the English wordings; in 1.3.6 "in an owner's name" became "on an owner's behalf", as in 1.3.4. Finding 3 (node 1.5.2, theme E of strictness-audit.md) says that 1.5.2 gives an exhaustive list of the kinds of symbol that carry a SpecTag: a function, a class or a member of one, a type, a constant or a component. A table or a view in SQL and a resource in Terraform are not on that list, and the SpecSheriff tools read only files in languages for which they have a description (TypeScript, JavaScript, Python); they silently skip a file in any other language. On 2026-11-17 the owner agreed («согласен») to the approach the agent recommended: the list in 1.5.2 becomes a list of examples, and what counts as a symbol in a language is defined by the description of that language, which the SpecSheriff tools provide for those three languages and a project adds for any other. The next question to the owner: what the checks do with a file of the system's code in a language that has no description. The agent recommends treating such a file as code without a SpecTag: a commit that changes it is refused, and one that leaves it unchanged is handled as 9.5 says. The project then adds a description of the language, or removes the file from the globs that name the system's code. The owner did not accept that such a case exists (2026-11-17): all code, even a configuration file, is written from the rules, so whoever writes it can always add a reference to the rule it implements, whether or not the file has symbols; the exception for technical code already exists. The agent agreed that such code can always be tagged, and explained that the gap lies in the check: without a description of the language, the tools cannot tell where a symbol starts and ends, so they cannot read its SpecTags or tell which symbol a changed line belongs to. The agent's new proposal, now put to the owner: where there is no description of the file's language, the whole file counts as one symbol; it carries its SpecTags as text in a comment at the top of the file; a change anywhere in the file is a change to that symbol. So no file of the system's code escapes the check, and a description of the language only makes the binding finer-grained, symbol by symbol. A file format that does not support comments, such as JSON, cannot carry a SpecTag. The owner agreed on 2026-11-17 («Ну, в принципе, да») and ruled: a SpecTag is placed directly before a symbol where the symbol can be identified; in every other case it is placed in a comment at the start of the file, in the comment syntax of the file's format; where the file's format does not support comments, the link is one-way: it goes from the rule that the file was written from, and that rule lists the file among the files that implement it. The owner accepted that the one-way link violates the protocol, since the owner took this to be unavoidable for technical reasons, and wants the wording to name no language or technology. fix-newform-437.mjs to fix-newform-439.mjs drafted a new text of 1.5.2 under the temporary key "1.5.2~" and as a proposal for the agreed text of 1.5.2; the wording checker ran on it twice; specsheriff-rule-reviewer reviewed it, with the brief written by make-brief-1-5-2.mjs in the scratchpad folder. The reviewer found that the one-way link has consequences for far more nodes than 9.7 alone: the file is bound to no rule on the file's side, so 9.2 and row 10.1.41 would count its rule as not implemented, 9.6 and row 10.1.42 would not require the commit message to name that rule, 10.4 would not give it to the reviewer, and 9.5 would count the file as code without a SpecTag. The principle "Rule, test and code bound by the sid" says every symbol in the code carries a SpecTag, so docs/principles.md would have to change first. A file of technical code whose format does not support comments would have nowhere to say that it implements no rule. The agent therefore asked the owner to choose between keeping the owner's one-way link and keeping the SpecTags of such a file outside it, in a place that the appendix of the tools names, read by the tools as the file's own SpecTags. The second option keeps the link two-way and leaves every other check and the principle as they are; the agent recommended it, in line with the owner's global rule that a technical limitation that can be overcome must be overcome. The owner answered on 2026-11-17 («храним», "we keep them"): the SpecTags of a file whose format allows no comments are kept outside the file, in a place that the appendix of the SpecSheriff tools names, and the tools read them as the file's own SpecTags. fix-newform-440.mjs and fix-newform-441.mjs then rewrote the draft, now shown to the owner, with the reviewer's other findings applied: a sentence after the list says which files count as one symbol; the SpecTags are placed in the first comment of the file, since some formats require a declaration on the first line; paragraphs 4 and 5 of the reason were merged into one sentence, the owner's argument that any file of the system's code can be bound to the rules it implements; the agreed sentences cite 1.2.7 for technical code and 9.1 for the system's code, and "monitor coverage" becomes "monitor which rules the code implements"; the examples name no format. Changes to other nodes, to be proposed once 1.5.2 is agreed: row 10.1.26, which says a SpecTag immediately precedes a symbol. In the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, the nodes s-5z5gnu7pahr82xp, s-9rtrj24myzjvshu and s-t383grkn3q68dpf in the appendix of the tools are to say which formats allow no comments, where the SpecTags of such a file are kept, and that the tools read the first comment of a file in a language for which they have no description. 9.2, 9.5, 9.6, 9.7, 10.4 and the principle need no change, since a SpecTag kept outside a file is still that file's SpecTag. The owner then asked (2026-11-17) what the point is of counting a file as a symbol when no symbols can be found in it, since a symbol is only a means of binding a rule to code more precisely. The agent agreed, and explained that the sentence is needed only by the other rules: nodes 9.2, 9.4, 9.5, 9.6, 10.4 and rows 10.1.26, 10.1.28a, 10.1.41 and 10.1.42 speak of a symbol as the unit that a SpecTag binds and a commit changes, so without the sentence they would not apply to a file bound as a whole. The agent asked the owner whether to keep that one sentence in the node, reworded to say that the rules on symbols apply to such a file as a whole, or to replace "symbol" in all those rules with a term for any part of the code that a SpecTag binds; the agent recommended the first option. The owner chose the second (2026-11-17): the rules are to say that rules are bound to code, and 1.5.2 is to describe only the ways of binding, of which there are three: directly before a symbol, where the symbol can be identified, which is the main way; in a comment at the start of the file, where its format allows comments; and, for a file whose format allows none, a third way. For the third way the owner asked the agent to weigh two options and give an opinion: one registry file that lists every such file with the rules it implements, or a file beside each such file, with the same base name and the extension `.spec`, listing the rules that file implements. The owner named the strength of the second option, which is that one can see at a glance whether a file is bound, and its weakness: auxiliary files that clutter the repository, though there are few of them and they appear only beside files such as JSON files. The agent recommended a file beside each such file: the binding stays next to the file it binds, a move or a rename that leaves one of the pair behind is caught by the checks, and parallel branches never conflict in one shared file. The agent proposed naming it after the whole name of the file it binds, plus a dedicated extension, as in the full name `texts.json.spectag`, since `.spec` already marks test files in many JavaScript projects and two files with the same base name would collide. Once the owner has decided, the agent will draft 1.5.2 so that it describes the ways of binding, and will propose a term for the part of the code that a SpecTag binds, to replace "symbol" in 9.2, 9.4, 9.5, 9.6, 10.4 and rows 10.1.26, 10.1.28a, 10.1.41 and 10.1.42. The owner then asked (2026-11-17) for the drawbacks of one registry file to be listed, with a note of how critical each one is, since auxiliary files in a project's folders interfere with how the project is organised and will not suit every project. The agent listed them and found none critical: every one is either caught by the checks or rare and trivial to resolve. A registry with one line per file, sorted by path, produces a merge conflict only when two branches add entries for neighbouring paths. The binding is not visible in the file itself, but the rule's list of code shows it, and a command of the SpecSheriff tools can show it for any path. A file that is moved or renamed leaves a stale entry, which the checks catch in both directions, as they would with a companion file beside each such file. The agent therefore recommended one registry file, at a location that the appendix of the SpecSheriff tools specifies. The owner ruled on 2026-11-17: one registry file; it is located where the configuration file written by `sheriff init` is located, so that its location is always the same and the project's own folders are not touched; at every commit the checks verify, for every entry in the registry, that the file it names exists, which catches a file that was moved or renamed; the guidelines themselves list JSON among the formats that allow no comments, and a project may extend that list. The owner said that the configuration file is "in the plugin's own folder", but `sheriff init` writes `sheriff.config.json` at the root of the repository (the appendix of the tools, s-7unxytshhc4md54), and the plugin's own folder is outside the repository, where git does not keep it. It describes the ways of binding code to rules: by a SpecTag directly before a symbol, which binds that symbol and the symbols nested in it; by SpecTags in the first comment of a file, which bind the whole file; by an entry in the file of bindings, which holds the file's SpecTags and binds the whole file. The formats that allow no comments are JSON and those that a project's own guidelines name. Each entry in the file of bindings names a file that exists.
