The agent recommends rejecting it: the single appendix can hold the nodes of each implementation in subsections of its own, under the section of the base specification that each of them implements, and the stack section can be divided in the same way. Several appendices would also contradict the principle "Three documents, changing at three rates". The owner rejected finding 9 on 2026-11-17, replying "ok" to that recommendation; 4.1 stays as it is, and the ruling is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 4.1 when the drafts are moved into the specification files. Every implementation of such a payment service, on any stack, must accept that format; without the clause such a project would either leave the format unnamed in the base, which makes the rule ambiguous, or move the requirement into the technical appendix, which changes with the stack while the format does not. The owner kept the clause on 2026-11-17: «упоминание технологии само по себе не криминально если базовый пункт по прежнему отвечает на вопрос что» (naming a technology is not wrong in itself, as long as the rule of the base still answers the question "what"). The agent recommends rejecting it: an EARS sentence ("When X, the system shall Y") states one relation, namely a condition, which 7.3.2 counts as one thought; a "shall" statement states one requirement; the benefit clause of a user story belongs in the reason of a node. Next comes finding 8 of sections 6 to 10 (node 9.6): a commit message names, by sid, every node to which a SpecTag binds code that the commit adds or changes, which is a burden in a large-scale refactoring, and the names can be lost in a squash merge. The audit proposed that the history should contain a record of which nodes each change implements, in a place the project chooses. The agent recommends rejecting it: the reviewer is given the rules that the message names (10.4), and the history of a repository is its commits; a squash merge on a hosting service keeps the messages of the squashed commits in its body by default. To ease the burden of a large-scale refactoring, the agent proposed that, in the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, a git hook pre-fill the commit message with the sids, while the check still refuses a message that lacks one of them. On 2026-11-17 the owner saw merit in the finding: the method relies on git alone for the history of changes; a squash merge can corrupt that data, and the git hosting service is outside the project's control. The owner proposed keeping rule 9.6 as a formality («для порядка») and adding a journal of the project's own that is always at hand and that records which nodes each change implements, and asked to be questioned with the skill `grilling`. The grilling session proceeds one question at a time. Q1, where the journal is kept: the owner chose a file in the repository (2026-11-17, «файл в репозитории»), an append-only file modelled on specs/_approvals.jsonl; a squash merge mangles commit messages but preserves file contents, and the file comes with every clone. Q2, who writes an entry in the journal: the agent recommended that the SpecSheriff tools write an entry on every commit, before the checks run, as the pre-commit hook already writes the files that `sheriff export` and `sheriff render` generate, so that the entry is derived from the commit and nobody has to remember it; the owner agreed (2026-11-17, «инструменты SpecSheriff»). The owner then returned to Q1 and asked whether the history should rather be kept in the specification itself, as a property `history` of each node, since a node holds everything that bears on it in one place; that property could also hold the earlier wordings, while the archive holds only removed nodes. The agent weighed a list of history entries in each node against a separate journal file and presented the comparison to the owner, recommending the separate file with the history shown beside each node in what the tools generate. The owner then said (2026-11-17) that code is not worth keeping anywhere but in the repository, since it can be generated from the specification at any time; what might be worth keeping is the wording of the rules, and the owner was no longer sure even of that. The agent explained that the journal would not hold code, only a record of which nodes each change touched, and gave its view: since the code is secondary to the specification, the link from each change to its nodes matters little beyond what the commit messages already record; what matters in the history of a rule is why it changed, which its `meta.decisions` field already records, while git keeps its earlier texts. The agent therefore recommended not adding a journal and rejecting finding 8, keeping rule 9.6 as it is and adding, in the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, a git hook that pre-fills the commit message with the sids. The owner rejected finding 8 on 2026-11-17 («отклоняем»): no journal is added, 9.6 stays as it is, and the ruling goes into its `meta.decisions` field when the drafts are moved into the specification files; the hook that pre-fills the sids is to be added in the step of T25 that changes the tools. The owner then proposed (2026-11-17) reversing the order in which the tools and checks run, from the most specific to the most general: first every script that can modify the files in the commit, such as linters and formatters; then the checks that the project's own rules define; and last, the checks that the SpecSheriff guidelines define, so that they check only what can no longer change and has passed the checks in the earlier steps. The agent agreed and proposed to the owner an order of three steps: first the tools that rewrite files, that is, the project's own tools that its guidelines list, and then the generators of the SpecSheriff tools, so that the files those generators write are left in their final form; then the project's own checks and its other tools, which change nothing; and last the checks of the guidelines. Nothing changes what the commit contains after the first step; every check and tool still runs regardless of what the earlier ones found, and the commit goes through only if all of them succeed. The owner agreed to 10.11 on 2026-11-17 («ок»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft; EngStickler's readers are reading its English text. In the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, the pre-commit hook is to run the three steps in that order, and the appendix of the tools is to say where a project lists its tools that rewrite files. Next came finding 12 on sections 6 to 10, the last of the findings on those sections: 10.6 and sections 9 and 10 depend on git, while some teams use another version control system. The agent recommends rejecting it, since the git hooks are the gate that the principle "Deterministic checks hold every participant to the contract" names, and the SpecSheriff tools are built on git; supporting another version control system would be a new feature of the tools rather than a change to the guidelines. The owner rejected finding 12 on 2026-11-17, replying "ok" to that recommendation; the ruling is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 10.6 when the drafts are moved into the specification files. The English text of the agreed 10.11 was then brought into line with its Russian text by fix-newform-461.mjs, after EngStickler's readers had read it. The findings on sections 1 to 10 of strictness-audit.md are now all settled. Next come the findings on the extensions, sections 11 to 14, in the order of the sections; the first is finding 2 about them, which concerns node 11.1.3, which prohibits domains from forming a cycle in which they use one another. The agent recommended rejecting the finding, since two domains that need something from each other put what they share into contracts (3.1). The owner observed (2026-11-17) that the audit and the rule concern different things: the audit concerns event-driven systems, where domains notify each other of changes to their state, and the rule concerns a cycle in which domains use one another. A signal bus may connect domains in any direction, but it must not affect how domains use one another, and probably must not affect the flow of data either: a loop in the data flow would feed an output back into an input and could make the system hang, and that is the problem the rule was meant to prevent. The agent agreed, and proposed keeping the ban on a cycle in which domains use one another and adding one sentence to 11.1.3, so that no reader mistakes notifications for use: a notification that one domain sends to another through an event does not make the sending domain a user of the receiving one, and notifications may go in either direction. The owner agreed to that proposal on 2026-11-17 («ок»), and fix-newform-462.mjs added the sentence to 11.1.3, which remains agreed. Next comes finding 8 about the extensions, also on 11.1.3: a domain's header names one directory for its code. The text of 11.1.3 says only "where its code is", so the limit to one directory exists only in the schema of a header, which allows one directory or null, and no SpecSheriff tool reads the header's `code` field. The agent recommended leaving 11.1.3 as it is and, in the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, letting the header name several paths or patterns. The owner ruled instead (2026-11-17) that the header should no longer say where the domain's code is: code is bound to rules by SpecTags, not to sections or domains. fix-newform-463.mjs removed that item from 11.1.3 and corrected the errors that EngStickler's readers had found in the English of the node; the ruling is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 11.1.3 when the drafts are moved into the specification files. In the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, the `code` field, which the schema of a header currently requires, is to be removed from the schema and from the headers of the saloon's domains. Next came finding 4 about the extensions, on node 12.1. The agent recommended widening 12.1 to cover the data that a domain receives from outside the system, and declaring in contracts a format in which more than one domain outputs data. That recommendation contradicted the owner's earlier rulings, which the agent had not checked first, and the owner pointed this out on 2026-11-17:
- the RecordType extension stands on its own: it is written in general terms, and its rules mention no domain (2026-11-16);
- a RecordType governs only the data that a part of an application outputs and passes along the chain, and no rule governs the data a part receives (2026-11-14);
- each part writes its data into a store of its own, which the owner calls a journal and which no other part writes to, so each format has exactly one part that outputs it (2026-11-14).

The owner's rule "A proposal is based on the owner's earlier rulings on its subject" was added to .claude/vt/rules.md the same day. The agent then checked every remaining finding about the extensions against the earlier rulings, and proposed rejecting these findings as already settled:
- finding 1, on 12.12, that a RecordType may refer to any RecordType: the owner ruled on 2026-11-14 that a RecordType refers only to RecordTypes of its own part and to fragments of a format in contracts;
- finding 3, on 12.4, that a project names where conformance is enforced: the owner ruled on 2026-11-14 that a part never outputs or stores data that violates its own format, and the generated RuntimeCheck refuses such data;
- finding 4, on 12.1, for the reasons above;
- finding 6, on 14.1 and 14.2, which concerns the single approval status of the whole specification: settled by the ruling on finding A on 2026-11-17;
- finding 9, on 12.1, which proposes that RecordType names need to be unique only within a generated file: the owner ruled on 2026-11-14 that one format has one name.

Findings 5 (12.3: the generator writes only TypeScript, Python and JSON Schema) and 7 (13.1: one draft RecordType freezes every generated type) remain open. The rewording of section 12 in general terms, which the ruling of 2026-11-16 requires, stays in the queue. The owner rejected findings 1, 3, 4, 6 and 9 on 2026-11-17 («ок»). Each rejection is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of the node it concerns (12.12, 12.4 and 12.1) when the drafts are moved into the specification files; finding 6 needs no record of its own, since the ruling on finding A settled it.

Next comes finding 5, on 12.3. It has two parts:
- A project may write a type by hand and have a check compare it with its RecordType. The agent recommends rejecting this part: the owner agreed to 12.3 on 2026-11-14, and 12.3 says that types and RuntimeChecks are generated from the RecordTypes and are never written by hand.
- A project for whose language the SpecSheriff tools do not generate types needs a generator of its own. The guidelines already allow that (s-km4x4s26kv7pjze, "A project's own generators join the shared one"), but on 2026-11-11 the owner put that rule, together with 13.1, into the Generation extension, and on 2026-11-16 the owner ruled that an extension relies only on the SpecSheriff guidelines (1.7.4). Therefore the RecordType extension may not rely on those rules. This is the open question recorded as item 4 of the list of consequences of 1.7.4 below, and the reviewer of 1.7.4 recommended moving 13.1 into the SpecSheriff guidelines.

The agent recommends moving both rules of the Generation extension, 13.1 and s-km4x4s26kv7pjze, into the SpecSheriff guidelines, reworded to name no extension. Neither prescribes how a system is organised: each applies only where a project generates code. The SpecSheriff guidelines already hold 1.2.7, on generated copies, and the rule on a project's own checks (s-2fau85dfyyeq66m). The owner agreed to the recommendation on 2026-11-17 and explained what the ruling of 2026-11-11 had meant. The rules on generating code from the RecordTypes for the running application, and on a project's own code generators, belong in the SpecSheriff guidelines. What the owner had meant to move into an extension was the generation of additional kinds of documentation, such as a diagram of the specification, a knowledge map or a mind map. The move of 2026-11-11 moved the rules on generated code into the extension instead, and the owner asked the agent to work out how to undo that move properly.

The agent's plan, put to the owner on 2026-11-17:

2. Section 13 becomes the extension for the views of the specification: the kinds of additional documentation generated from the nodes, such as the readable export, the mind map and diagrams. Its rules say what each view shows. How the SpecSheriff tools build each view stays in the appendix of the tools, which already has "Building the map" (s-rpv4fkdqqguptqd) and "What the export, the guide and `sheriff show` write of a node" (s-k4nqjr96ux7a5m7).

3. Adoption stays section 14, so no section is renumbered.

Finding 7, on 13.1, is to be put to the owner once the plan is settled.

**NOW:** the extensions are being restructured in accordance with the owner's decisions of 2026-11-16, before the review of the RecordType nodes resumes. The owner agreed («ок принимаю») to this structure: the rules that apply to all extensions form subsection 1.7 of section 1; each extension is a section of its own, numbered from 11 onward. The sections of the extensions are 11 Domains (11.1 Decomposition, 11.2 Hierarchy), 12 RecordType (12.1 RecordType, which needs no domains; 12.2 RecordType in a specification divided into domains, which applies only when a project also uses Domains), 13 Generation of code from the nodes, and 14 Adoption of an existing project. restructure-extensions.mjs renamed the drafts' keys and rewrote the structure of the page that shows the base specification; no node's wording changed. Earlier that day the owner had agreed to split the Domains extension into two extensions, Domains and RecordType («Да, я тоже думаю, что нужно сделать два расширения»). The agent had confirmed that RecordType was required of every project that divides its base specification into domains: section 12.3 was titled "Domains: RecordType", and the node now numbered 12.1.4 lets a domain output and store only data records that conform to the RecordType that describes them. The owner also observed that RecordType need not depend on domains: it describes the data that the parts of an application pass to one another, so it can also be used in a single domain built as a pipeline of stages. The split is consistent with four established practices: domain-driven design (DDD), where Published Language is one of the patterns chosen for a relation between bounded contexts; ISO/IEC/IEEE 42010, whose viewpoints are linked by correspondences; the Reference Model of Open Distributed Processing (RM-ODP), whose information viewpoint is separate from its computational viewpoint; and the OGC ModSpec, whose requirements classes name the classes they depend on.

The owner then split the matter into two questions (2026-11-16). First, how extensions relate to one another: any combination of extensions must work together. Second, RecordType itself: either it is rewritten to work on its own, with the word "domain" replaced by a general word for a part of an application, or it becomes an extension that depends on Domains and cannot be used without Domains. The owner preferred the first option, and added that an extension that depends on no other extension uses the general terms and names no other extension. The agent proposed that every extension rely only on the SpecSheriff guidelines and name no other extension, so that any combination of extensions works by design. On 2026-11-16 the owner agreed («ок принимаю») to this proposal, and to the two consequences the agent derived from it: 1.7.4 is rewritten to say so, and the draft that gave such rules a section of their own is withdrawn; RecordType is written using general terms, and section 12.2 is removed. Examples in an extension may mention another extension's concepts, such as a domain, as an example of a part of an application: 1.7.3 forbids naming an extension only in the text and the reason of a rule.

Before agreeing to the proposal for node 1.7.2, on 2026-11-16 the owner asked whether an extension needs to change a rule of the SpecSheriff guidelines at all, since the owner considers such a change unsafe, or whether adding rules and refining existing ones would be enough. The owner asked the agent to look at every extension and at the owner's first project that is to use SpecSheriff in production. The agent found the following. Domains and RecordType only add rules. "Generation of code from the nodes" would have had to change 10.12, since 10.12 says that whatever is generated from the nodes is regenerated after every change, while 13.1 holds generated code back until its nodes are approved; the contradiction disappears if 10.12 refers only to the views it lists, or if 13.1 is moved into the SpecSheriff guidelines. "Adoption of an existing project" relaxes rules of the guidelines in three of its four nodes (14.1, 14.3 and 14.4, which come from the current nodes 5.2, 5.1 and 1.8), while 14.2 only adds a requirement; without changes to rules, if extensions cannot change rules, those relaxations go back into the SpecSheriff guidelines as rules about a draft specification, where they are today. The owner's first project that is to go into production using SpecSheriff needs Domains, RecordType and probably the generation of code; its own rules only add rules to the method's, and every symbol in its code already carries a SpecTag. The agent recommended dropping the possibility of an extension changing a rule: an extension only adds rules to the guidelines and refines rules of the guidelines, as a project's own guidelines already do; the `overrides` link type, its check and the limit proposed for 1.7.2 are then dropped. On 2026-11-16 the owner agreed («Да, тогда я думаю, стоит отказаться от оверрайда и остаться только на уточнениях, расширениях, которые безопасны»), and asked for a review of every rule of the guidelines: a rule that sets a requirement some other project could not meet and could get around only by changing the rule is to be made general enough, so that a project's own guidelines can add what that project needs, just as a shared component is kept general so that it can be reused.

In accordance with that ruling, the script fix-newform-404.mjs replaced the earlier proposal for node 1.7.2 with a new one, now shown to the owner: a rule of an extension of the guidelines either adds a rule to the SpecSheriff guidelines or refines one of their rules, and is consistent with every rule of the SpecSheriff guidelines, as node 2.3 already requires of a project's own guidelines. On 2026-11-16 the owner agreed to the new wording of node 1.7.2 («ок»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft; EngStickler's readers have read its English text, and only the English text was fixed. fix-newform-405.mjs removed the `overrides` link type from node 6.1, which keeps its agreed status. The owner said that the owner must not be asked about such a change again («ну если уже решили что убираем overrides зачем меня спрашивать сто раз - убираем»), and `.claude/vt/rules.md` now says so: what a decision removes or renames is removed or renamed without asking the owner, and reported in one line. fix-table-119.mjs marked row 67 of table 10.1, the check on the `overrides` link, as deleted, and renamed the sections that 23 other rows move to. It mistakenly overwrote an earlier version of fix-table-119.mjs, written on 2026-11-13, whose change had already been applied. The review for undue strictness has finished; its findings, condensed, are in strictness-audit.md in the t25-pass folder, with the themes that recur and the remedy most of them share. The review of every rule for undue strictness has started: three general-purpose agents are reading sections 1 to 5, sections 6 to 10, and the extensions in sections 11 to 14. Each reports the rules that some legitimate project could not meet and could not get around by refining or extending them, with a loosening for each; the agent then presents the findings to the owner one by one.

On 2026-11-16 the owner asked whether letting a project's own guidelines choose a value other than a shared rule's default is not itself an override. The agent agreed that it is, and restated the principle: a rule of the SpecSheriff guidelines, or of an extension, requires only the minimum that every project needs; a project's own guidelines may only make it stricter or more specific, never weaker; where a choice cannot be avoided, such as the language of a document, the shared rule says outright that the project makes it, and sets no value for the project to replace. The owner agreed («Да, я согласен»), and proposed that examples in a rule of the SpecSheriff guidelines point to stricter rules that a project's own guidelines could add, as hints for an agent that writes those guidelines. The agent proposed writing both down first: the principle in a new node beside 2.3, and a third kind of example, besides the typical case and the closest mistake, in a proposal for 7.6.3; and then to work through the 14 main findings of strictness-audit.md in section order. The owner agreed («ок»). draft-2-4.mjs drafted the new node 2.4, "The minimum a rule of the guidelines requires", and renumbered the former 2.4 and 2.5 to 2.5 and 2.6; after the wording checker had run, specsheriff-rule-reviewer reviewed the new node and found it not ready. fix-2-4b.mjs and fix-2-4c.mjs, in the scratchpad folder, applied its findings: the title became "The minimum the shared rules require"; the minimum is determined by the purpose that the rule's reason states; where projects may differ in a value, only a project's own guidelines name that value or any default for it, since a default set by the shared rule would be replaced, and so changed, by a project; the sentence that repeated what 2.3 already states was cut. The example of a choice is now the choice of who owns the specification, which 2.3 already leaves to a project. The language of a project's specification is no longer used as an example: the reviewer found that it contradicts 7.6.1 and the owner's decision of 2026-10-31 that everything SpecSheriff governs is in English. That question will be put to the owner when the review reaches 7.6.1, finding 9 of strictness-audit.md. The reviewer also said that the remedy written at the top of strictness-audit.md, a shared rule that sets a default, contradicts the owner's ruling; the remedy agreed instead is the minimum that 2.4 states. In 7.6.3, "stricter or more specific" was replaced with "refine or extend", the words of 2.3, without asking the owner, since the change gives the concept the name it already has in 2.3. On 2026-11-16 the owner agreed to node 2.4 («согласовано»), and agree.mjs marked it as agreed. A configurable value of the SpecSheriff tools still has a default (.claude/vt/rules.md): `sheriff init` can write it into a project's own guidelines, where 2.4 places it. fix-newform-406.mjs wrote the proposal for 7.6.3; the wording checker found no problems in the sentences that the proposal adds. On 2026-11-16 the owner agreed to it («ок»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft; EngStickler's readers then read its English text, and only the English text was fixed.

Work on the findings of strictness-audit.md, one by one, has started with finding 2, node 1.1.4 (fix-newform-407.mjs, then rewordings in the scratchpad folder after the wording checker and specsheriff-rule-reviewer had run). The proposal keeps out of the base specification only the technologies the system is built with, and lets a rule of the base specification name a technology that is part of what the system does, as it names anything else the system does, even where the system is also built with it. On 2026-11-17 the owner agreed to it («согласовано»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft. Following that agreement, these must change: the reason given in 1.1.5, which says the base names no technology; the second sentence of the reason in 4.1; the note on 2.1 that says 1.1.4 does not change; CLAUDE.md line 95, docs/README.md line 34 and _promo/articles/no-code-without-a-rule.md line 105, which state the old ban on naming a technology in the base specification. The owner's decision must also be added to the `meta.decisions` field of 1.1.4 (s-5x737t9nee4zgaa). 4.3 stays valid but is stricter than the new 1.1.4 (finding 10). fix-newform-408.mjs wrote the proposals for the wording of the reasons in 1.1.5 and 4.1, shown to the owner at the same time, and brought the note on 2.1 up to date; CLAUDE.md, docs/README.md and the article in _promo will be brought into line with the new 1.1.4 when the drafts are moved into the specification files. On 2026-11-17 the owner agreed to both proposals («согласовано»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote them into the drafts of 1.1.5 and 4.1.

Finding 6, node 1.2.6: the owner ruled on 2026-11-17 that the rule may be relaxed for the technical appendix only («Думаю, можно ослабить, но для технического приложения»). fix-newform-409.mjs and two rewording scripts in the scratchpad directory wrote the proposal. It says that a node of the technical appendix may name an artifact outside the specification as the place where part of that node's content is kept, such as an interface contract that another team owns. The node names the artifact's version, the checks compare the code with that version, and adopting a new version of the artifact is a change to the node. On 2026-11-17 the owner agreed to it («согласовано»), and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft of 1.2.6. Since the node was being changed anyway, the proposal also addressed the findings of the wording checker about the node's agreed wording. The proposal carried out the ruling the owner gave on 2026-11-17, so it was not sent to the reviewer. Still to be done: the owner's ruling is to go into the `meta.decisions` field of 1.2.6 (s-xqs2e24mx5253ye) when the drafts are moved into the specification files, and the appendix of the tools must say how the checks compare the code with an external artifact in the version that a node names.

Finding A of strictness-audit.md concerns the single approval status for the whole specification. It touches 1.3.3, 10.6 and the future 14.1 and 14.2, which come from 5.2 and part of 5.4 of spec.json. On 2026-11-17 it was put to the owner as a question about which direction to take, and the owner ended the session before answering. The proposed direction follows the pattern that the principles establish for owners: any section may have an approval status of its own, which its owners set by a separate decision, as the owners of the specification set the specification's status; a rule's code and tests are checked as strictly as the status nearest above the rule requires; a section that has its own status lists the paths of its code, since code without a SpecTag names no rule. The cost of this direction: a section would then have two statuses, the one derived from its rules (1.3.3) and the one its owners set, and the two need different names. The SpecSheriff tools would then also have to determine how strictly to check the code of each section. The alternatives are a separate specification for each part of a repository, an option that does not help a project that adopts the method part by part within one service, or a single status for the whole specification, as now.

The steps of the restructuring, in order:

1. The owner agreed to node 1.7.4, "Independence of extensions", on 2026-11-16 (the owner's reply: «ок»); agree.mjs marked it as agreed. Node 1.7.4 was written by rewrite-1-7-4.mjs and then by fix-1-7-4c.mjs, fix-1-7-4d.mjs and fix-1-7-4e.mjs, the last three in the session's scratchpad folder. Its text says that a rule of an extension of the guidelines uses only the terms that the SpecSheriff guidelines and the extension it belongs to define, cites only nodes of these two, and declares links only to nodes of these two. EngStickler's readers have read the English text of node 1.7.4 and of node 12.6, which the owner agreed to earlier that day. english-1-7-4-12-6.mjs in the scratchpad folder corrected only the English wording of both nodes; their meaning and their Russian text remain as agreed. Node 12.6 now says "select" instead of "choose", "adds fields" instead of "brings fields", and "has a `refines` link" instead of "refers through a `refines` link". The reviewer raised two concerns. First, two extensions could each change the same rule of the guidelines, or define a type of link with the same name, and the two extensions combined would then contradict each other; the reviewer recommends a check that rejects the later of two such changes. The wording checker found that this is a separate requirement, so it has been drafted as node 1.7.5, "Changes to one rule by several extensions" (draft-1-7-5.mjs, then english-1-7-4-12-6.mjs): each rule of the SpecSheriff guidelines may be changed by at most one extension of the guidelines. Link types to which two extensions give the same name are left out of node 1.7.5, since 7.2.4 already requires a name to mean the same thing in every node. specsheriff-rule-reviewer then found that the requirement of node 1.7.5 narrows the permission of node 1.7.2, and under the owner's working rule in `.claude/vt/rules.md` an exception is written into the rule it narrows. So fix-newform-403.mjs deleted the draft of node 1.7.5 and wrote that requirement into a proposal for node 1.7.2, a node the owner had agreed to. Since node 1.7.2 is being changed anyway, the proposal also addresses the findings of the wording checker on its agreed wording without changing its meaning, and adds an example; the proposal is being shown to the owner. The reviewer also proposed adding to node 1.7.4 an item stating that a rule declares links only of types that are defined by the guidelines or by the rule's own extension; this goes to the owner after node 1.7.2. Second, node 13.1 must stop citing node 12.3, which belongs to another extension. Without that citation, in a project that does not use the extension "Generation of code from the nodes", nothing would keep the types generated from RecordTypes from being regenerated from draft nodes. This will be settled when the review reaches section 13.
2. flatten-recordtype.mjs removed section 12.2 and moved the rows of the only remaining subsection directly into section 12: 12.1 to 12.11 are the rules of RecordType, and 12.12 to 12.15 (the former 12.3.5, 1.30, 1.28 and 1.29) are the four rules that still mention domains, each to be settled when the review reaches that rule.
3. The following nodes, which the owner has agreed to, must now be reworded using general terms, and each will be shown to the owner as a proposal: 12.1, 12.4, 12.5 and 12.6, which mention domains; the reason in 11.1.1, which cites RecordType; 7.2.7, which cites 12.1 as an example; 13.1, which cites 12.3.
4. A question to settle when the work reaches the section on generation: 12.3 requires types to be generated from RecordTypes, while the rule that generated code is not released until the nodes it is generated from are approved is 13.1. The reviewer of the earlier draft of 1.7.4 recommended moving 13.1 into the SpecSheriff guidelines, outside any extension, reworded to name no extension.
5. The review of the RecordType nodes resumes at 12.7, the node currently numbered 1.27, "One source for the values of a field of type `one of`" (s-xfu2gpzddjnmgks), which the pass of 2026-11-07 approved on the owner's behalf and which the owner has not yet reviewed. draft-12-3-8.mjs, written but not yet run, now writes the key 12.7; it will produce a draft that contains the current wording of 1.27 and its Russian rendering, both with `TextRule.list`. The page that shows the base specification now shows a draft's `TextRule.list`.

The owner agreed to node 12.3.7, "RecordType variants", on 2026-11-16 («согласовано»). It is the node currently numbered 1.26, "A RecordType variant exists only for a value carrying fields of its own" (s-z3dnru6qqdf9m6j), which the pass of 2026-11-07 approved on the owner's behalf and which the owner has not yet reviewed. draft-12-3-7.mjs produced a draft that contains its current wording and a Russian rendering, since full-ru.json has none for 1.26. specsheriff-rule-reviewer found that 1.26 contradicts 12.3.5, which the owner has agreed to. 1.26 places a RecordType variant in the part of the specification that declares the value of the field that selects the variant. According to 1.30 to 1.32, that part can be a domain other than the domain that declares the RecordType, and a variant there would refine a RecordType of another domain. On 2026-11-16 the owner ruled that a RecordType variant is always in the same part of the specification as its RecordType («делай как рекомендуешь»), after reading the page that explains the conflict (variants-explainer.html in the t25-pass folder, https://claude.ai/artifact/5emLVo4Ce69nrN9Rjx1Kqr). The owner's reasoning behind the ruling: the contracts section holds only fragments of a format; a RecordType is declared in the domain where its data first appear; another domain only mentions that RecordType or its fields in its rules, and never links two RecordTypes. The ruling is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 12.3.7 when the drafts are moved into the specification files. It also changes the reason of 12.3.13, the current 1.32, which says that the part declaring a value adds the variant for that value. It changes the SpecSheriff tools as well, whose check on variants in validate.ts currently requires a variant to be in the part that declares its value. A new proposal, written by fix-newform-402.mjs, carries out the ruling and addresses the reviewer's other findings. It uses the wording "the fields that choose its variant", as 1.26 does, instead of the coined name "choosing field", which is not idiomatic English. The proposal passed the wording checker and specsheriff-rule-reviewer, and accept-proposal.mjs wrote it into the draft once the owner agreed. The script accept-proposal.mjs now preserves all the examples that a proposal contains, not only the first. The reviewer found that the ruling also requires changes to the following, each change to be made in its own step: the reasons given in 1.32 and 1.28, which say that the part of the specification that declares a value adds the variant for that value; item 3 of node 7.1.16 of the appendix of the tools, and the row of table 5.1 that places a variant in the part declaring its value, with the two tests written from that row; the check on variants at validate.ts:404; the words "choosing fields" in node 19 of the appendix of the tools and "answers to" in node 7.1.16 and in the schema of the node files, to be brought into line with the wording of 12.3.7. When the drafts are moved into the specification files, the links of 12.3.7 change: its `cites` link to s-xfwuvd3funutmsw is removed; `cites` links to s-7vm323mhry2enx3, s-sn6au3vcr9uy5pm and s-h6h25ctd3yryudh are added; its `refines` link to the node s-e877bnfa559tvk9, which is now 12.3.3, is replaced by a `refines` link to 12.3.2. The reviewer also asked whether 1.28 and 1.29 are to be archived now, since a value that a domain adds to a field declared in the contracts section now gets its variant only in that section; the question is deferred to node 12.3.9.

The owner agreed to node 12.3.6, "Guaranteed fields", on 2026-11-16, after choosing "computes or observes" («вычисляет или наблюдает») for its second condition; agree.mjs marked it as agreed. Before the draft was shown again, fix-newform-399.mjs had brought it into line with the rules of section 7: RecordType, the domain and RuntimeCheck are cited at their first use, the first condition no longer says "never removes", the second and third conditions say "the field's value", and the reason no longer reads as a requirement. EngStickler's readers then read its English text, and only the English text was fixed, in two places.

On 2026-11-16 the owner ruled that the gap a reviewer had found in node 6.5 is not one («Пока не вижу чем бы эта связь была обоснована»): a word used in its ordinary sense is not a term and needs no citation; a term used in its special sense in another term's definition is related to that term by a `term-relation` or a `uses` link; and a `cites` link between terms would link arbitrary nouns and be fragile. The text of 6.5 therefore stays unchanged, and this ruling is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 6.5 when the drafts are moved into the specification files. On the same day, the owner agreed to the new reason in 6.5 (fix-newform-398.mjs), written by accept-proposal.mjs: "Only a term names an entity" was false under the broad meaning of "entity", so the reason is now one sentence that cites 7.2.4. EngStickler's readers then read the English text of the new reason, and only the English text was fixed, in two places.

The three nodes of section 7 that the owner agreed on 2026-11-16 to take up («да только эти три») are agreed:

The promotional card _promo/images/html/card-6-2-name-the-subject.html still uses the old wording of 7.2.1; it is to be updated now that the nodes of section 7 are agreed.

On 2026-11-16 the owner decided to keep node 1.2.3 in section 1 («оставить в разделе 1»), although the agreed structure listed it under subsection 7.3. Nodes 7.2.5 and 7.2.6 need no change: the wording checker's only finding on 7.2.5 was that its old title stated the rule, and its finding on the reason in 7.2.6 was a false positive, since "those two mistakes" refers to the two mistakes named in that reason.

The owner agreed to all five cell nodes of the TableRule 7.2.4 together with the table («я уже согласовал все узлы внутри таблицы»), and that is now a rule in `.claude/vt/rules.md`: agreeing to a table is agreeing to the nodes in its cells. The five nodes are marked as agreed. On 2026-11-16 the owner agreed to the head of the TableRule 7.2.4 together with its table («7.2.4 согласовано вместе с таблицей»); accept-proposal.mjs wrote the wording into the draft in newform.json, whose field `table` holds the columns and the rows. The head of the TableRule keeps the sid s-9w3u3wn37wksyxd of the agreed node 7.2.4. The five reviewers have reported. Two of them found that "entity", read as in node 3.3 and in the reason in 6.5 ("Only a term names an entity"), would keep the rules on names from applying to any of the method's own names, since the SpecSheriff guidelines contain no term node. On 2026-11-16 the owner chose the broad meaning («широкое значение»): an entity is anything the specification defines. The definition of an entity in the current node 1.37 ("An entity is what a term names") had not been carried over into the new form, so 7.2.4.1 now defines it. When the work reaches node 6.5, its reason is to be made more precise; when the drafts are moved into the files, this ruling is recorded in the `meta.decisions` of 7.2.4.1.

fix-newform-391.mjs applied that ruling and the reviewers' findings:

- 7.2.4.1 defines a name (what the specification gives to an entity) and an entity (anything the specification defines, with a list of examples), and requires that a name mean the same thing in every rule that uses it. A rule's title gives no name to an entity, so it is not a name, while a term's title is a name; the paragraphs on scope were therefore dropped from every node of the subsection. A new example shows the two kinds of title.
- 7.2.4.2 was retitled "Coined names" («Придуманные имена»): the specification coins a name only when the ordinary word also appears in the specification in another sense.
- 7.2.4.3 was retitled "What a name is made of" («Из чего состоит имя»); its second example is billingAddress, with userData as the wrong name.
- 7.2.4.4 applies to a node that writes a property's key. Its example `meta.comments` was removed.
- 7.2.4.5 was retitled "Relations to other entities" («Отношения к другим сущностям»).
- The other nodes cite 7.2.4.1 where they first use "name" or "entity". The subsection's purpose became «Как спецификация даёт имена тому, что определяет, и как узлы ими пользуются» ("How the specification gives names to what it defines, and how its nodes use those names").

The wording checker has been run again on all five, and a second specsheriff-rule-reviewer agent read 7.2.4.1; fix-newform-392.mjs applied their findings. A name by itself refers to an entity, so a property's key written with its object is not a name. "Every node" replaces "every rule", since a term node is not a rule. The forms of a property's key in 7.2.4.4 differ in how the object's name is written. 7.2.4.2 binds whoever writes a node, and 7.2.4.5 opens with its main thought. The page that shows the base specification is at version 529, and the nodes are being shown to the owner one at a time, 7.2.4.1 first, together with the subsection's purpose. To do when the work returns to node 7.3.1: it calls every title "a short name", while 7.2.4.1 defines a name as what refers to an entity, so 7.3.1 should instead say that a title says in a few words what the node is about. Note for the documentation to be written later: "entity" here is wider than the Entity of domain-driven design, which a developer might take the word to mean.

restructure-naming.mjs made 7.2.4 the subsection "Naming" («Образование имён»), whose purpose on the page reads «Как спецификация даёт имена тому, что определяет.» (in English, for when the drafts are moved into the specification files: "How the specification names what it defines."). Its nodes are drafts in newform.json:

- 7.2.4.2 "When a thing gets a special name", from item 1 of the skill;
- 7.2.4.3 "What a name says", from item 3 of the skill and the paragraph of 7.2.4 on general words;

Nodes 7.2.5 to 7.2.7 keep their numbers. The page shows the subsection indented under 7.2. The wording checker's group "words" lists 7.2.4.1 to 7.2.4.5, and writing-rules.mjs now sorts node numbers with any number of levels. The owner then ruled that what the drafts called a "thing" («вещь») is to be called an entity («сущность»), the word that node 3.3 uses for what a term introduces; fix-newform-390.mjs made the change, and the term has an entry in terms-ru.md of the skill `vt`. A field is not an entity in that sense, so 7.2.4.1 defines a name as referring to an entity or to a field used apart from the object it belongs to, and 7.2.4.2 had its first condition, which listed kinds of things, removed. The wording checker has been run on all five, and on the changed ones again; five specsheriff-rule-reviewer agents, one for each node, are reading them, briefed with the file naming-brief.md from the session's scratchpad. Next: apply the reviewers' findings that are valid, remove the temporary key, rebuild and republish the page, and show the nodes to the owner one at a time, 7.2.4.1 first, together with the subsection's purpose. Once the subsection is agreed, the section "Naming" of the skill spec-review is to cite these nodes instead of stating the principles, at the step of T25 that changes the skills; item 5 of that section, on writing values as the schema holds them, is not about names and stays where it is.

Items found while 7.2.4 was reviewed, each to be taken up when the review reaches the node it concerns:

- TextRule is used without an explanation or a citation in 1.3.5, 7.2.4, 8.4 and 8.6, and no draft node defines it, although node 7.2.7 requires each such name to be explained or cited. A node that defines the kinds of node, or a citation of one, is needed; raise it with the owner when the work reaches section 8, or at the review of the base specification as a whole.

- The decision of 2026-10-28 on node 6.10, about the schema's keys `type` and `kind`, still says "carries its object", which the owner withdrew on 2026-11-10; it is to be reworded when the drafts are moved into the files.

The owner agreed to node 7.2.3, "General words", on 2026-11-16; accept-proposal.mjs wrote the proposal into the draft. The owner corrected one phrase of its reason before agreeing: «нет разговора вокруг» became «нет контекста». After the agreement, EngStickler's readers read the English text of node 7.2.3, and fix-newform-386.mjs applied their fixes to the English text only, without changing the meaning: "A general word appears in the same phrase", "Where a rule refers to a specific thing, it uses the name of that thing", "binds each of them to the meaning they chose". The rule "A word of general meaning is used with what it applies to, in a message as in a file" in the rules.md of the skill `vt` says in different words what 7.2.3 now says, so the rule in rules.md needs no change.

The script accept-proposal.mjs writes the proposal that the owner accepted into its draft and marks the draft as agreed: `node accept-proposal.mjs <key> <date> <note>`.

When the drafts are moved into the files, the ruling of 2026-11-15 that 7.2.3 and 7.2.4 do not apply to a title is recorded in the `meta.decisions` of both nodes.

For each remaining node of section 7: draft changes to it based on the findings of the wording checker (below), run `node .claude/vt/check-node.mjs <key> <folder>`, send the draft to specsheriff-rule-reviewer if the changes are substantial, rebuild and republish the page that shows the base specification, and show the node to the owner. Then continue with the other nodes of section 7 in the same way, one node at a time. Each run of check-node.mjs is given an output folder in the current session's scratchpad, since its default folder is in the scratchpad of an earlier session. To check a proposed change to an agreed node, copy the draft under a temporary key such as "7.2.2~" with the proposed wording, run the checker on that key, and remove the key before the page is built.

The new titles: 7.2.1 "The subject of a rule", 7.2.2 "The order of the text", 7.2.3 "General words", 7.2.4 "Naming", 7.2.5 "Definition by negation", 7.2.6 "Lists that restrict" (originally "Restricting lists", which could be read as the act of restricting lists), 7.3.2 "Thoughts and sentences", 7.3.3 "The contents of a node", 7.4.1 "Counts in prose", 7.4.2 "The reason", 7.5.1 "Superfluous rules", 7.5.2 "The length of a sentence", 7.5.3 "Rules as tables", 7.6.1 "The language of the specification", 7.6.2 "Capital letters" (originally "Capitals", which can also mean capital cities), 7.6.3 "Examples".

The wording checker ran on every remaining node of section 7 on 2026-11-16. What it found, as the agent judged it:

- 7.2.1 (title "A rule names its subject and states what must hold"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; "the same way" refers to the previous paragraph; "in the technical debt" can attach to two words; the reason may go through the text.
- 7.2.2 ("A node's text opens with its main thought"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; the reason justifies the text's sentences one at a time, which rule 7.4.2 forbids.
- 7.2.3 ("The use of general words"): "is always used" names no one who acts; "So a rule never names just a structure … but …" states two relations; "the ambiguity" in the reason refers to nothing named in its paragraph.
- 7.2.4 ("Rules for naming"): two cases of the same kind are written as paragraphs rather than as a list, which rule 7.2.2 asks for; "is never a general word alone" is phrased as a negation.
- 7.2.5 ("No definition by negation"): the title is a prohibition rather than a name.
- 7.2.6 ("Whether a restricting list names what is allowed or what is forbidden"): "those two mistakes" in the reason refers to a paragraph of the text.
- 7.3.2 ("One thought to a sentence"): no finding the checker was sure of; the text still has to state the owner's ruling that one relation between two facts is one thought.
- 7.3.3 ("A node contains what is applied together, and nothing applied separately"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; "SpecTag" in the reason is neither explained nor cited, which node 7.2.7 now requires; EngStickler's list of calques treats "carry out", said of a rule, as a calque of «выполнять».
- 7.4.1 ("No count in prose"): the title is a prohibition; "states no count" is phrased as a negation; "those things" refers to the previous paragraph.
- 7.4.2 ("A reason says why the rule exists"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; the sentence "It does not go through the text's paragraphs one by one and does not restate them" states two prohibitions by negation and repeats the sentence before it.
- 7.5.1 ("Which rules have no place in the specification"): the reason justifies the text's list items one at a time; "does neither" is phrased as a negation.
- 7.5.2 ("A sentence is as short as its meaning allows"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name.
- 7.5.3 ("A rule is made into a table only where it is needed"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; "a table is written" names no one who acts; "Elsewhere" refers to the previous paragraph; "TableRule" is neither explained nor cited.
- 7.6.1 ("The specification is written in English"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; the reason states a requirement ("A quotation must match what it quotes letter for letter").
- 7.6.2 ("No phrase or sentence is set in capitals"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; three exceptions of the same kind stand in one sentence rather than a list; "is never set in capitals" is phrased as a negation; "such a name" refers to the previous paragraph; the second paragraph of the reason justifies the text's sentences one at a time.
- 7.6.3 ("A node may show examples"): the title is a statement of the rule rather than a name; "An example requires nothing" is phrased as a negation.

The script `.claude/vt/check-node.mjs <draft key>` runs every group of the checker and wording-lint.mjs on one draft of newform.json and prints the findings, the sure ones first. v3.2 differs from v3.1 in the following ways:

- The script wording-lint.mjs now checks texts against the rule `paragraphs`, which the group "statement" no longer checks: it flags a paragraph of more than three sentences, and marks the finding as unsure.
- The group "one-place" has a ruling that a sentence that ties a requirement to its cause states a relation and repeats nothing, and that a title is not a sentence of the text.
- The group "one-thing" has a ruling that a reason may state, as premises, what other rules require.
- The owner's layer has a new rule, `function-words`, taken from the "Writing" section of the global CLAUDE.md, and the group "words" checks texts against it.
- The group "brevity" also reports a sentence that states what the reader already knows from the rest of the node, or what obviously follows from it.
- The examples of a node are now removed only up to the start of its reason; the old code removed the reason that followed the examples as well. The code that removes the examples is in `.claude/vt/cut-examples.mjs`, which both wording-check-v3.mjs and wording-lint.mjs use.
- The prompt says that everything under the heading "The node" is the node, labelled or not, because the checker did not recognise the node in case C125, which has no "Title:" label.

The two runs of v3.2 wrote their results to `.ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/cases/haiku-high-v3.2a/` ($2.18) and `.ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/cases/haiku-high-v3.2b/` ($2.16). The table compares them with the run of v3.1 (`.ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/cases/haiku-high-v3.1/`, $2.07).
