The numbers of its own cases that each group caught in v3.1, v3.2a and v3.2b are: "one-thing" 12, 14 and 13 of 18; "statement" 9, 8 and 10 of 10; "words" 8, 5 and 8 of 15; "one-place" 6, 5 and 4 of 10; "brevity" 1, 3 and 2 of 7. The two runs of v3.2 differ by up to three cases in a group, so a difference of that size between two versions is not evidence of a real difference.

Rules 7.14 and 7.6 (now 7.4.2 and 7.2.2), read in full, show that some findings that the agent had first judged false are real. Rule 7.14 says that a reason does not go through the text's paragraphs one by one, and the reason in control text 6.3 goes through the three properties that its text excludes from the check. Rule 7.6 says that more than one exception of the same kind is set out as a list, and control text 2.5 states two such exceptions in prose. The figures for v3.1 in the table were judged again on the same basis.

The false findings of v3.2 that recur have these causes:

- `refers-back` applied to a noun phrase that names the thing again, such as "the appendix" (4 findings);
- rule 7.5 applied to two subjects or two causes in one relation, such as "the entities common to them, and how they work together, are agreed" (3);
- `citation-words` applied to a citation that follows a term or the statement it supports (3);
- rule 7.2 applied to a sentence of the text that repeats the title (2);
- rule 7.7 (2);
- `term-explained` applied to a term that is cited at its first use (2).

Once section 7 has been restructured and its nodes changed, the work returns to node 12.3.6. Node 12.3.6, "Guaranteed fields" («Гарантированные поля»), waits for the owner's agreement. It is currently node 1.25, s-adhmw4gw6y82f44. The owner rewrote the first sentence of 12.3.6: «RecordType отмечает поле как гарантированное, если верно хотя бы одно из следующего:». The owner then rejected its closing sentence, «RecordType не отмечает как гарантированное никакое другое поле», as stated from the opposite. The script fix-newform-348.mjs removed that sentence and put «только» (only) into the first sentence, so that the list of conditions names the only cases in which a field is marked as guaranteed. The page that shows the base specification has been republished with the draft of 12.3.6 that fix-newform-348.mjs wrote (version 510). The sentence that said a field is guaranteed where it has a value in every data record has been removed, and so has the lead-in "each time a domain outputs or stores such a data record": each condition already implies that the field always has a value. The owner ruled on 2026-11-15 that a title is a short name for what its node is about, so the title of 12.3.6 stays "Guaranteed fields". The first condition of 12.3.6 reads "the domain sets the field's value when it creates a data record and never removes that value", because any value present in a data record met the earlier condition. On 2026-11-15 the owner named the rule "Guaranteed fields" and decided that the property of a field of a RecordType is to be renamed from "required" to "guaranteed" wherever that makes sense («да, переименовывай везде где это имеет смысл»). In the owner's words, the rule concerns the fields whose values a domain can guarantee, and whether to make a field required is a decision for the domain that reads the data rather than for the one that outputs it. The script fix-newform-345.mjs rewrote the draft of 12.3.6 around the property "guaranteed" of a field. The draft keeps the earlier changes: "outputs or stores", the closed list of conditions, the identifier as an example in the third condition, and the kinds of fields that are not guaranteed, which were moved into the examples. The third condition follows the decision of 2026-10-25 on 1.25, because the current wording, "as what identifies the record", can be read in two ways. The same script replaced the word "required" with "guaranteed" in the agreed drafts 12.3.2 and 12.3.4, which remain agreed, since a term renamed by the owner's decision is reported in one line and is not shown again. Once the owner agrees to the node, record the agreement with `agree.mjs`, and have an EngStickler reader read the English text of 12.3.6, 12.3.2 and 12.3.4 in the background. Then go through nodes 12.3.7 to 12.3.15 in order. Nodes 12.3.7 and 12.3.13 are to be changed to say that a RecordType variant is declared where the RecordType it refines is declared. Node 12.3.9 (currently 1.28, s-96df5fcfqsvex9d) and node 12.3.10 (currently 1.29, s-gzfq3x8hesj227e) let the domains add values to a field of type `one of` of a RecordType in the contracts section. The agent has recommended moving both of these nodes to the archive, together with the row of table 10.1 about the values that domains add. According to 12.3.1 and 12.3.5, the contracts section holds fragments of a format, which refer only to other fragments, so a fragment whose values several domains add would depend on those domains. The owner has not answered yet. Ask again when 12.3.9 is reached.

**Also waiting for the owner to answer:** the owner asked why the agreed rules on how a node is written are still broken, and what would ensure that every ruling is applied (2026-11-15). The agent found four causes. The owner's ruling of 2026-11-14 against description by negation was applied to node 12.3.5 only and was not recorded anywhere. The closing sentence of 12.3.6, which said what a RecordType does not mark as guaranteed, was introduced as a fix for the reviewer's finding that the list did not say that it was complete, and that fix was not checked against the other rulings. The reviewer marks a rule with a ✓ without quoting the words that justify it. The checklist that writing-rules.mjs prints contains only the agreed drafts of section 7 and of node 1.2.3 of the base specification, while other rulings are spread across the two rules files, this continuation file and the lists of changes in newform.json. The owner agreed to fix this before moving on to node 12.3.7 and proposed a design, which is being settled with the owner one question at a time (the skill `grilling`): a checker agent running on Haiku at low effort, which is given a text and returns JSON that either says the text passes or names each phrase and the rule of the guidelines that the phrase breaks; the author fixes the text and has it checked again, with at most five rechecks, and a text that still fails after the fifth recheck goes to the owner with the rules that the text broke after each fix, so that contradictions between the rules come to light; a violation of one of the owner's personal rules does not make a text fail; and, while the owner tests and adjusts the design (the owner calls this a debugging mode), every personal rule of the owner's that the author did not apply is reported to the owner alongside the text, in a block quote. The owner also wants the checker tested on the cases from earlier sessions in which a rule was broken and the owner caught the violation. Settled so far: the checker reads only nodes of the specification that are about to be shown to the owner, each node in full (its title, text, reason and examples), regardless of whether the node is new, a change to an agreed node, or a wording the owner dictated, and reads their English text, since the English text is the normative one and the rules were written for it (the owner, 2026-11-15: «ок»). The checker takes over the checklist from the reviewer `specsheriff-rule-reviewer`, which no longer gives a verdict on each rule of the checklist and keeps only the review of what a node says: the assessment against the five criteria, the check of the terms against the vocabularies of established practices such as requirements engineering, DDD and ArchiMate, and the check against the earlier decisions. A draft goes to the checker first, and goes back to it until it passes the check; then it goes to the reviewer; a draft changed in response to the reviewer's findings goes to the checker again (the owner, 2026-11-15: «ок»). The checker's model and effort are chosen so that it can do its job: the agent starts with Haiku at medium effort and raises the effort first, then switches to a stronger model, only as far as the tests on the cases from earlier sessions show is necessary (the owner, 2026-11-15). The mandatory checklist consists of the agreed rules of the SpecSheriff guidelines on how a node is written, section 7 and node 1.2.3, as writing-rules.mjs prints them. The optional rules are a separate short list that the agent compiles from the owner's personal rules on wording, in the two rules files and in the "Writing" section of the global CLAUDE.md, leaving out the rules on how work is done and those that repeat section 7. Each rule in that list is a statement that can be checked against the text, with its source and date. The list is shown to the owner before the tests are run, and each new remark of the owner's on wording is added to it in the same turn (the owner, 2026-11-15: «ок»). The checker returns only the violations it finds, as JSON of the form {"pass": false, "findings": [{"rule": "7.10", "optional": false, "field": "text", "phrase": "<an exact quotation from the node>", "why": "<a short reason>"}]}, where `field` names the title, the text, the reason or an example, and `optional` marks a rule from the owner's personal list. Only a violation of a mandatory rule makes a text fail. The checker proposes no fix: fixing is for the author or the reviewer (the owner, 2026-11-15: «да исправление должно быть за тобой или рецензентом»). The author never dismisses a finding under a mandatory rule. Where the author judges such a finding false, or sees that fixing it would break another rule, the text goes to the owner at once, with the author's explanation, without waiting for the fifth recheck. Only the rechecks that follow fixes for findings the author accepts count toward the limit of five. The author may decline to apply one of the owner's personal rules; while the owner tests and adjusts the design, the author then reports it to the owner in a block quote beside the text, naming the rule, the phrase and the reason (the owner, 2026-11-15: «ок»). The checker is tested on a set of cases that a subagent collects from the transcripts of earlier sessions: each wording that the owner rejected for how it was written rather than for what it said, with the text the owner was shown and the rule the owner invoked. The set also holds wordings that the owner accepted, as control texts that show whether the checker reports false findings. The checker is good enough when it flags the phrase recorded for every known case and when no more than one in five of its findings on the accepted wordings is false. Until both hold, the agent raises the effort level first, then switches to a stronger model; if even the strongest model falls short, the agent reports the results to the owner. The agent shows the owner how many cases the checker caught, which cases it missed, and which false findings it reported (the owner, 2026-11-15: «ок»). The optional list is .claude/vt/writing-optional.md (rules O1 to O12), which the owner accepted on 2026-11-15 («ок»). A subagent is collecting the cases into .ai_docs/t25-pass/checker-cases.json. Created on 2026-11-15: the checker's instructions in .claude/vt/wording-checker.md; the script .claude/vt/wording-check-input.mjs, which writes the task that the checker is given for one node (the instructions, the mandatory rules, the optional rules, the signals it counts, and the node) into .ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/; and the agent ~/.claude/agents/specsheriff-wording-checker.md (Haiku, medium effort), which Claude Code normally loads when a session starts; this session loaded it a few minutes after it was written. The control texts are twelve agreed drafts (1.1.1, 1.2.3, 2.5, 4.1, 6.3, 7.11, 8.3, 9.4, 10.6, 12.2.1, 12.3.4 and 12.4.2), with their tasks in .ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/control/. On Haiku at medium effort (replies in checks/control/replies-haiku-medium.json), the checker reported 25 findings on them, and the agent judged 10 of them false, which is more than one in five. Of its 9 findings under mandatory rules, 5 were false: two examples placed in the text (7.16 allows them there and does not require them to be anywhere else), a contrast describing one situation and a fact stated in the reason (both read as two thoughts), and "a field" read as a bare general word. Its real findings point to places where the wordings the owner has accepted break the rules as they are currently worded: the bare word "structure" in 1.1.1 (7.8), and negative statements in 1.2.3, 2.5, 6.3, 9.4 and 12.2.1 (O5). It caught the closing negative sentence of 12.3.6 but missed its convoluted first sentence, which the owner rewrote. Whether "X, so Y" is one thought or two is unclear from rules 7.5 and 7.6, and is to be put to the owner. On Haiku at high effort (replies in checks/control/replies-haiku-high.json), the checker reported 25 findings on the control texts. The agent judged 19 of them real. It judged 2 plainly wrong, both in 12.4.2: a relative clause read as a second thought, and a noun phrase read as a back-reference. It judged 4 others to follow from a literal reading of rules whose intent is unclear: rule 7.5 applied to an example that uses "but" to describe one situation (node 10.6); rule O1 applied to "this rule" meaning the node itself (node 6.3) and to "the requirement" in a reason (node 1.2.3); and rule O2 applied to a term in a title (node 9.4). At high effort the checker missed two real findings in node 4.1 that it reported at medium effort. The subagent wrote 157 cases to .ai_docs/t25-pass/checker-cases.json; 60 of them are clean: the owner was shown the English text, the case names a rule, and the subagent noted no doubt about it. The script .ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/run-cases.mjs runs the checker on those 60 cases in headless Claude Code sessions (`claude -p --model <model> --effort <level> --setting-sources local --allowed-tools Read`), saves each reply in checks/cases/<model>-<effort>/, and marks a case as caught if a phrase the checker quotes overlaps the phrase the owner objected to. On 2026-11-15 the checker was run on those cases with Haiku at medium and at high effort. Scores on the 60 clean cases described above: Haiku at medium effort caught 31 ($0.43 for the run), Haiku at high effort caught 36 ($0.59). Most of the violations the checker missed can be found only by understanding what the node means: a title that does not say what the node is about (7.4), a sentence that repeats another, an obvious statement (7.2), a sentence the owner found unclear. After that, runs on Haiku at max effort and on Sonnet at medium effort were started. Sonnet at medium effort caught only 23 of the 60 cases ($4.40 for the run), with fewer findings per case than Haiku. The owner then asked whether the checker's decisions can be studied in order to improve the checker's prompt or its algorithm (2026-11-15). Two facts emerged. First, the model's own reasoning is not kept in a readable form: the thinking blocks in a session's transcript are empty. Second, the runs so far were not isolated from the user's configuration: a headless session started in this repository loaded the global CLAUDE.md and the session-start hooks, and the checker spent its effort loading the `vt` skill. A session started in an empty folder (scratchpad/iso) with `--setting-sources local` loads neither of them. run-cases.mjs now runs each session in that folder, keeps the task files of each run separate, and, with --trace, asks the checker for its verdict and a one-clause reason for each rule (the option --trace of wording-check-input.mjs). The isolated runs on Haiku at high effort, with and without the trace, were started next. The isolated run without the trace caught 36 of the 60 cases ($0.47), as many as the non-isolated run on Haiku at high effort; the run with the trace caught 30 ($0.70). The traces of the 30 misses are in checks/misses-haiku-high-trace.json. In 28 of them the checker applied the right rule, often to the exact sentence that broke it, and judged the sentence "ok" at face value, with reasons such as these: a title "says what the node is about", a sentence "keeps its subject and verb", a term "may be known to the reader, so I do not report it". The rules that call for judgement (7.4, 7.2, 7.17, O2) are applied without any criterion of their own for deciding a case, and the instruction to report nothing it is unsure of keeps the checker from flagging violations of them. Two kinds of the owner's objections have no written rule: a phrase that can be read two ways, and a sentence that repeats another sentence of the node. A second version of the instructions, .claude/vt/wording-checker-v2.md, works in two steps: the checker first says what the node says (its main thought, what its title alone suggests, the thought of each sentence with marks such as "two readings", "repeats", "obvious" and "shorter", and the terms left unexplained), then applies the rules to that analysis; a finding it is unsure of is reported with `sure` set to false and does not make the text fail. After that, a run with this version of the instructions on Haiku at high effort (run-cases.mjs --v2) was started. With the second version of the instructions, the checker caught 50 of the 60 cases, 37 of them by findings marked as sure ($0.67 for the run), but it reported about 11 findings per case, against about 4 with the first version. On the twelve control texts (checks/control-cases.json, run with --cases) it reported 98 findings, 33 of them marked sure. The agent judged 20 of the 33 sure findings to be real, 8 plainly false, 2 doubtful and 3 dependent on the owner's reading of a rule. So more than one in five of the checker's sure findings are false. The false ones have these main causes: the checker flagging the specification's own terms (node, sid, section) under rule O2; rule 7.4 applied to titles that the owner had accepted; rule 7.16 read as requiring every example to be in `examples`; and the "But not:" form of an example read as a sentence fragment. Questions for the owner: whether a sentence of the form "X, so Y", or a contrast with "but" describing one situation, states one thought or two under rule 7.5; and whether rule 7.16 forbids an illustration inside the text, such as "as with the records a backup leaves out". Next: a third version of the instructions, which gives the checker the list of terms that the specification defines and the agreed form of an example; and, as a separate step, adding two optional rules taken from the owner's earlier remarks: a sentence must have only one reading, and no sentence may repeat another sentence of the node. The owner then asked whether the checker goes through the checklist rule by rule or judges all the rules at once (2026-11-15). It judges them at once: each check is a single call to the model, which receives every rule and the node together and writes its whole reply in one pass; "take the rules one at a time" is only an instruction, and nothing enforces it. The agent proposed an actual loop: a script calls the model once for each rule, or for each small group of related rules, with only that rule or group, its own test and the node; the script then merges the replies. The owner chose a loop over groups of related rules, to keep the cost down, and wants the approaches tried only on Haiku at high effort, for now (2026-11-15). The owner sees two directions, to be combined: narrowing what a single model call has to decide, down to a single rule, at a growing cost; and giving each call the context it needs, and testing that context on the cases where the checker keeps giving wrong answers. A group may need context of its own, such as the rules directly related to its rules and the history of their changes. The aim is a balance between too much context and too little: each group of rules is to have its own recipe, with which the checker gives correct results on that group of rules. The baseline to compare against is 37 of the 60 cases caught by findings marked as sure. The recipes are in .claude/vt/checker-recipes.json, which holds seven groups of rules (title: 7.4; thought: 7.5, 7.17, O4, O13, O14; structure: 7.2, 7.3, 7.6, 7.7, O10; words: 7.1, 7.8, 7.9, 7.13, O1, O2, O3, O7, O11; reason: 1.2.3, 7.14, O6; negation: 7.10, 7.11, O5; parts: 7.12, 7.16, 7.18, O8, O9, O12). Each recipe gives the checker a criterion of its own and, as context only, the rules related to the group's rules. Where a group needs more, its recipe adds the glossary of the terms that the specification defines, the test that pairs each sentence of the reason with the text of the node, or the decisions recorded for the group's rules. The decisions are left out of the first versions of the recipes. Rule 7.15, on revising a rule, governs a process and is not checked by any group. O13 (every sentence has only one possible reading) and O14 (no sentence repeats another sentence of the node) were added to the list of optional rules on the basis of the owner's earlier remarks. .claude/vt/wording-check-group.mjs writes the prompt for one group and one node; .ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/run-groups.mjs runs the checker with every group's recipe on every case, merges and scores the replies, and counts the cases each group caught. The first run (label groups-v1) caught 55 of the 60 cases, 42 of them by findings marked as sure ($2.07 for the run, 10 of the 420 calls failed), but the checker reported about 20 findings per case, 8 of them marked as sure. Counted strictly, by the findings of the group that the case's rule belongs to, it caught 27 cases, 15 of them by sure findings; the other cases were caught only by other groups, which flagged the same phrase for another reason. On the 12 control texts the checker reported about 16 findings per node, 6 of them marked as sure (73 sure findings in all), and the agent judged about half of the 73 to be real. Most of the false findings come from the "words" group (rule 7.8 applied to every general word, such as "something" in a rule stated in general terms, or "the whole system"; 14 sure findings, about 3 of them real) and from the "thought" group (rules 7.5, O4 and O14). Haiku at max effort, with the first version of the instructions and without isolation, caught 37 of 58 cases for $6.06, about as many as at high effort for ten times the cost, so raising the effort above high is not worthwhile. Next: tune the recipes of the "words" and "thought" groups, and score each group strictly, counting only the findings under its own rules. The owner ruled on rule 7.5 (2026-11-15): a sentence of the form "X, so Y" states one thought, since its point is to show how two things are related, and one relation makes one thought («Фраза вида «X, поэтому Y» — одна, тут смысл показать взаимосвязь - если взаимосвязь одна то и мысль одна»). This ruling goes into the `meta.decisions` of node 7.5 when the drafts are moved into spec.json. The literal text of 7.5 says otherwise ("Where one part of a sentence could be true while another is not, the parts are two thoughts"), so a change to 7.5 is to be proposed to the owner. The script .ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/group-report.mjs scores each group by its own findings only. For groups-v1, the "title" group caught 0 of its 15 cases, "thought" 5 of 9, "structure" 7 of 10, "words" 6 of 15, "reason" 4 of 5, "negation" 4 of 5 and "parts" 1 of 1. On the control texts the "words" group reported 26 findings marked as sure and the "thought" group 22. The title group judged every title "ok", including the long titles that read like a sentence, which the owner shortened; whether a title must be a short name or may state the rule is unclear, since the agreed titles of section 7 state their rules. The recipes of the "thought" and "words" groups now include rulings: the owner's ruling on 7.5, and a narrower reading of 7.8 and O1 (a general word breaks 7.8 only where a reader cannot tell what it applies to; "this rule" is not a back-reference, because it means the node itself). wording-check-group.mjs adds a recipe's rulings to the task. The second run of the "words" and "thought" groups (label groups-v2) was started on 2026-11-15. Asked whether a title must be a short name, the owner answered with a principle rather than a rule (2026-11-15): the more a text can be shortened without losing its meaning, the better, since every word costs the reader effort and is a place where an error can arise, as in code; but the text must stay easy for another person to read and understand. Rules are code at a higher level of abstraction, since code is written from them. The owner therefore suggested applying engineering principles such as KISS and SOLID to the way rules are written, and asked the agent to look for inconsistencies among the rules on writing a node. This is being discussed with the owner one question at a time (using the skill `grilling`). The second version of the recipes (groups-v2) sharply reduced the number of findings marked as sure on the control texts and still caught most of the cases that the groups had caught before. The "words" group caught 6 of its 15 cases (4 by findings marked as sure, compared with 2 in groups-v1) and reported 9 findings marked as sure on the control texts (compared with 26 in groups-v1). The "thought" group caught 4 of its 9 cases (1 by a finding marked as sure, compared with 3 in groups-v1) and reported 8 findings marked as sure on the control texts (compared with 22 in groups-v1). The agent then set out what it found when it read the rules on how a node is written as if they were code: a statement made in several places in different words ("short without loss of meaning" in 7.17, O4 and the owner's CLAUDE.md; "one thought" in 7.5, 7.7, O4 and O13; "a reason does not restate the text" in 7.14, O6 and O14); contradictions (the literal definition in 7.5 contradicts the owner's ruling, and 7.4 contradicts the titles of section 7); no stated priority between the rules that add words for precision (7.8, O1) and the rule that removes them (7.17); no defined reader, although 7.2, 7.4 and O2 each assume one; a signal (O4) mixed with rules; a process rule (7.15) mixed with rules about the text; and conventions left unwritten, such as the form "But not: …" of an example. The owner agreed to restructure the rules (2026-11-15: «ок  Переустраиваем правила»): a few principles, ranked, and the current rules becoming checks that implement them. The agent proposed four principles, in this order: the reader understands the text on first reading; one thing per unit (one thought per sentence, one requirement per node: SRP); one place for each statement (DRY); and brevity without loss of meaning (KISS). It also proposed setting apart the conventions on the form of a node (7.1, 7.13, 7.16, O7, O11, O12), most of which a script can check, the process rules (7.15 and the part of 7.2 on revising), which no check of a text can verify, and the signal O4. The owner answered that only the first is truly a principle; the other three are approaches to implementing it (2026-11-15). The owner agreed to the list of four principles as proposed. The owner then added that the first of the four is not a principle but the goal (2026-11-15: «первое — это не принцип, а цель»): the reader understands a rule correctly on first reading and with the least effort. The other three are approaches that serve the goal, so they need not be ranked: where two of them conflict, the wording the reader understands correctly with less effort wins. The owner agreed to define the reader in one place (2026-11-15: «ок»): whoever applies a rule, an agent or a developer who writes tests and code based on the rule; this reader knows the SpecSheriff guidelines and the terms of the specification, sees the node and the nodes it links to, and does not know the history of the discussion or what the author meant. The checker is to receive this description of the reader with every task. The owner then corrected the definition (2026-11-15): such a reader knows the SpecSheriff guidelines and the terms of the specification only partly, and full knowledge of them cannot be assumed. An agent would know them fully only with a context window large enough to hold the whole specification (and even then not reliably), or with a mechanism, such as a subagent or an MCP server, that gives it just the context that each question needs. The owner then asked, on 2026-11-15, which reader the agent meant, since the SpecSheriff guidelines and a specification that follows them are different texts. A specification that follows the guidelines is read by whoever uses SpecSheriff, that is, the guidelines together with the tools that check compliance with the guidelines. The guidelines are read primarily by an AI agent: they are its instructions on how to write a specification. The rules of the guidelines must themselves be written in accordance with the guidelines' rules. A mechanism is therefore needed that flags the agent's work whenever the agent breaks those rules, and the work is then corrected. In the owner's words, this is the essence of the task: if the guidelines can be written in accordance with their own rules, any specification can be written in accordance with them, and the same mechanism works for any specification. The agent proposed one definition for both cases: the reader of a node is whoever applies it, that is, whoever writes tests and code based on a node of a project, and the AI agent that writes a specification in accordance with a node of the guidelines and checks the specification against that node. On 2026-11-15 the owner then questioned the clause saying that the reader does not know the history of the discussion: a node keeps its decisions, todos and comments in its `meta`, so part of its history is available, and the owner asked which history the agent meant and which part of it matters. The agent answered that it meant the history kept outside the specification: the conversation, the intermediate drafts, the rejected wordings and what the author had in mind. A reader who applies a rule needs only its text and its reason; the decisions in its `meta` serve chiefly whoever changes the rule. Where a decision changes how the text reads, the text must be rewritten. The owner accepted this definition of the reader (2026-11-15: «ок»): the reader of a node is whoever applies it. This reader sees the whole node, its `meta` included, and the nodes it links to, and knows the rest of the specification and the guidelines only partly. The reader has no access to any conversation outside the specification, and no node may require one. The reader must be able to apply the rule from its text and its reason. The owner then agreed to a core vocabulary (2026-11-15): a few terms of the method, such as node, sid, rule, text, reason and link, which the reader always receives with a node; a node that uses any other term cites the node that defines the term at the place where the term is first used. The owner added that the rules on writing a node govern the text of a rule and its reason, and the agent treats the title as part of the wording. They do not govern the technical information in a node, such as citations, or its examples: an example is best written so that an AI agent understands it as precisely as possible. The owner accepted this structure of section 7, "How a rule is written" (2026-11-15: «ок»): a first node titled "The goal, the reader and the scope" (the goal; the reader; the scope, namely that the rules of the section govern a node's title, text and reason but not its citations or examples; the core vocabulary); then the subsections "Understandability" (7.3, 7.6, 7.8, 7.9, 7.10, 7.11 and the rule on citing a term), "One thing per unit" (one thought per sentence, one requirement per node: 1.2.3, 7.4, 7.5, 7.7), "One place for each statement" (7.12, 7.14), "Brevity without loss of meaning" (7.2, 7.17, 7.18) and "Conventions of form" (7.1, 7.13, 7.16). Rule 7.15 moves to the section on how the specification is kept. The nodes are initially only moved, with their wording unchanged; each change of wording is then proposed node by node. The agent then proposed moving the optional rules that serve the goal into the SpecSheriff guidelines, moving O7, O11 and O12 into the conventions of this project, and moving O4 into the script that counts the signals. The owner explained (2026-11-15) that the owner's personal rules are used in other projects too, and are optional, which lets a project rewrite them; they therefore must not be removed. Each project is to have its own set of rules on how texts are written, and a rule of that set takes precedence over the owner's personal rule on the same matter. The agent restated this in terms of layers, as in the settings of Claude Code or in CSS: the owner's personal rules are a global layer, kept in the skill `vt` and applied in every project; each project has a layer of its own; every rule has a permanent key, such as `refers-back` or `negation`, and a rule of the project with the same key replaces the owner's rule, or switches it off; the checker's prompt holds the project's rules and the owner's rules that the project has not replaced. The owner's global rules are O1 to O3, O5, O6, O8 to O10, O13 and O14; O7, O11 and O12 apply only to SpecSheriff and go into this project's layer; O4 stays a signal for the script. The owner confirmed this interpretation (2026-11-15: «правально»). The owner then agreed (2026-11-15: «ок») that a text breaking a rule in force fails the check, no matter which layer the rule comes from: a violation of one of the owner's rules that the project has neither replaced nor switched off makes the text fail, as a violation of the project's rule does. While the design is being tested, the agent still reports every case to the owner in a block quote where the checker found that a text broke one of the owner's rules. The owner agreed (2026-11-15: «ок») that the title of a node is a short name for what the node is about, not a statement of the rule: a reader sees the title first, often in a tree or a list, and a statement would repeat the main thought that the text opens with. The titles in section 7 that state a rule are changed when the work gets to those nodes, not all at once. For node 12.3.6, the reader sees the node inside the section on RecordTypes, so the owner's title "Guaranteed fields" can stay as it is, without the words "of a RecordType". The owner agreed to this plan (2026-11-15: «ок»): first the checker is rebuilt according to these decisions and tested again on the test cases and the control texts; then section 7 is restructured and its nodes changed, with the checker in the loop, each draft going to the checker, then to the reviewer, then to the owner, one node at a time; then the work returns to 12.3.6 and the rest of part 12, also with the checker in the loop. The rebuilt checker is to consist of the following parts: the layers of rules, each rule identified by a key (the owner's global rules in the skill `vt`, this project's rules, and the guidelines' rules on writing a node); the description of the reader and the core vocabulary in every prompt; a check that covers only the title, the text and the reason, not the examples or the citations; groups of recipes, one for each subsection of section 7 (understandability, one thing per unit, one place for each statement, brevity, conventions of form); and a script for the checks that a script can perform (the signals of O4, banned words such as "carry", capital letters, terms used without a citation). The following were built on 2026-11-15: the owner's global layer C:/Users/PC/.claude/skills/vt/writing-personal.md (with the keys refers-back, term-explained, citation-words, say-what-holds, reason-is-why, section-purpose, table-header, paragraphs, one-reading, no-repetition); this project's layer .claude/vt/writing-project.md (no-carry, git-names, and a line listing the keys switched off); the configuration .claude/vt/checker-v3.json (the goal, the reader, the scope, the core vocabulary, and five groups: one-thing, statement, words, one-place, brevity); the prompt builder .claude/vt/wording-check-v3.mjs, which merges the layers by key and removes the examples from a node; the script .claude/vt/wording-lint.mjs (capitals, "carry", "the tools"); and the runner .ai_docs/t25-pass/checks/run-v3.mjs, which runs every group on the 60 cases and the 12 control texts and reports each group's results for its own cases only. The old list .claude/vt/writing-optional.md is superseded by the two layers. The first run (labelled v3) on Haiku at high effort gave these results ($2.18 for the run, 4 of 360 calls failed): 55 of the 60 cases caught by at least one finding, 49 of them by findings marked as sure. If a case is counted as caught only when a finding of its own group catches it, the "one-thing" group caught 12 of its 18 cases (11 of them by findings marked as sure), "statement" 8 of 10 (7), "words" 4 of 15 (3), "one-place" 7 of 10 (6) and "brevity" 3 of 7 (2): 34 of the 60 in total, compared with 27 for groups-v1. Rule 7.4 now flags the long titles that the owner later shortened, which the "title" group of groups-v1 missed completely. On the 12 control texts the groups reported 81 findings marked as sure: 11 by "one-thing", 28 by "statement", 17 by "words", 10 by "one-place" and 15 by "brevity"; the script wording-lint.mjs found 3 more. The "words" group is still the weakest at catching its own cases. The agent judged the sure findings of the groups on the control texts: about 42 are real violations of the rules as they now stand, and about 38 are false. Rule 7.4 flagged five titles among the control texts that state the rule of their node, and the owner's ruling on titles turns these into real violations. Most of the false findings come from four sources. The rule `say-what-holds` was applied to every negative word: of its 21 findings, about 12 are false, made on the reasons of nodes, where a reason describes a problem, on descriptions of a thing such as "a node that was never approved", and on the word "unnoticed". Rule 7.17 produced nitpicks over single words: about 9 of its 14 findings are false. The rule `paragraphs` flagged a text already divided into paragraphs (4 false findings). The rule `term-explained` flagged unexplained terms in titles, where nothing can be cited (3 false findings). The owner ruled on 2026-11-15 that `say-what-holds` applies only to what the text of a rule requires, not to a rule's reason or to a negative word inside the description of a thing; writing-personal.md now says so. The question about the title of 12.3.6 is to be asked again once the design is settled.

The owner's decision of 2026-11-15 on guaranteed fields requires these changes in the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, with each test written before its code:

- in `specs/_schema/node.schema.json`, the property `required` of a RecordType's field becomes `guaranteed`, with the same values, and its comment says what a guaranteed field is;
- `sheriff reindex` renames the property `required` to `guaranteed` in every RecordType node of a project, as it renamed the kinds of node in commit 1e50209;
- `skills/tools/src/types.ts` reads `guaranteed`, while the generated code marks a guaranteed field in each target language's own way: `required` in JSON Schema, a property without `?` in TypeScript, and no `NotRequired` in Python;
- rules 18 (s-suv42g8r3ra2h6b) and 19 (s-nk8td3ez58gahc4) of the appendix of the tools, and every other rule of the appendix that says a field is required where its node says so, use the word "guaranteed";
- `skills/tools/src/review.ts` and `schema.ts`, which also mention the property, are changed too, and so are the guides and the saloon's RecordTypes.

When the drafts are moved into the specification files, the decision is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 12.3.6, and the rename of the property is to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of 12.3.2.

The owner agreed to node 12.4.3 again on 2026-11-14 («согласовано»), with the owner's own order of the domains in a row of the diagram of the domains (fix-newform-338.mjs through fix-newform-341.mjs): first, in the alphabetical order of their names, the domains that neither use nor are used by another domain of the row; then each other domain to the right of every domain of the row that it uses, as close to them as possible. The appendix of the tools is to say how the tools place a domain that uses several domains of the row, or that several domains of the row use; this is to be written in the step of T25 that changes the tools. An EngStickler reader read the English text of 12.4.3, and fix-newform-342.mjs applied its fixes to the English text only. Following the owner's comment on the page that shows the base specification, fix-newform-340.mjs removed the last item of the agreed node 12.4.2, since the order within a row is governed by 12.4.3 and the item was unclear. Node 12.4.2 stays agreed. Node 12.4.3 has been shown to the owner in full, in the wording that fix-newform-338.mjs and fix-newform-339.mjs wrote, together with the block «Оценка». On 2026-11-14 the owner set a new order of the domains. Within a row, the domains not linked to any other domain of the row come first, in alphabetical order of their names; then the linked domains, each placed as close as possible to the right of the domains it uses. The owner agreed to four clarifications: only `consumes` links between domains of the same row count; a domain is placed to the right of every domain of the row that it uses; where the links leave a choice, alphabetical order decides; the map orders its domains by the same rule.

The reviewer then found that the position "as close to them as possible" is ambiguous when a domain uses several domains of the row or several domains use it, and proposed spelling out a placement procedure. The agent kept the owner's simpler wording and left those cases to the appendix of the tools; the owner has been asked to confirm this. The sentence about the map was removed from 12.4.3, because the rule that governs the map is to cite node 12.4.3. The current node 4.1 (s-zu8ne3wq84zchc4), a rule of the SpecSheriff guidelines that describes the map's level branches, is to be moved into an extension, as 12.1.3 requires. The new order differs from the one the SpecSheriff tools currently compute, so `domainOrder` (`skills/tools/src/model.ts`) is to order each row separately, and the tests with the SpecTag s-uw9n84edf7cbe9n are to be changed in the step of T25 that changes the tools. The reviewer named two cases that tell the orders apart: an unlinked domain z comes before a pair in which domain a uses domain b, and a link from domain c to a domain of another level changes the current order c, b into b, c. The proposals that fix-newform-334.mjs through fix-newform-336.mjs wrote, which stated the procedure the tools currently apply, have been superseded by the proposal in fix-newform-338.mjs.

The owner agreed to node 12.3.5, "What a RecordType may refer to" (sid s-8ht2hn88cu2qwz9), on 2026-11-14 («пункт согласован»), after its examples were reduced to the owner's own example; fix-newform-333.mjs recorded the agreement. The wording is the one that fix-newform-332.mjs wrote, applying the reviewer's report on the wording that fix-newform-330.mjs wrote to the wording that fix-newform-331.mjs wrote. Its sentence restricting a fragment of a format to referring only to other fragments was the agent's proposal, made after the reviewer raised the concern that a fragment referring to a domain's RecordType would tie every RecordType that uses it to that domain; the owner agreed to it together with the node. An EngStickler reader is to read the English text of 12.3.5. On 2026-11-14 the owner agreed that a RecordType of one domain must not refer to a RecordType of another domain, but found the list of exceptions in the earlier draft unclear and its title poor: «Они просто не связаны» (they are simply not linked). In the rewrite, a RecordType does not refer to a RecordType of another domain, either in the types of its fields or through `refines`. The identifier of another domain's data record is held as an ordinary value, so it is no exception. RecordType variants then stay in the domain of the RecordType they refine (the reviewer's option (b)); nodes 12.3.7 (s-z3dnru6qqdf9m6j, currently 1.26) and 12.3.13 (s-xfwuvd3funutmsw, currently 1.32) and row 28 of table 10.1 are to be changed accordingly, and the reviewer is to confirm these changes.

On 2026-11-14 the owner ruled that a RecordType of one domain must not name a RecordType of another domain. A RecordType always describes the data a domain outputs, and no rule governs input: each domain takes the data it needs from what the other domains provide, and it may also use external data, to which no such link can be made. The owner answered the agent's two questions with «1. да 2. да»: node 1.39 (s-5yvpn4zhuqmv5bq) is to be moved to the archive, and a RecordType may refer to the data records of another domain by an identifier, as node 12.3.2 allows. When the drafts are moved into the specification files, node 1.39 is moved to the archive with the reason for its removal and with node 12.3.5 as its replacement, and the SpecSheriff tools stop checking the `consumes` links against RecordTypes. Node 12.3.5 takes the place of 1.39 in the planned structure of part 12 and states what is forbidden, so that a check can be built on the rule. The script fix-newform-328.mjs did not write `newform.json`; fix-newform-329.mjs wrote the node there. The owner then clarified (2026-11-14) that the rules of one domain may cite a RecordType of another domain, while a RecordType of one domain must not refer to a RecordType of another domain, except for the fragments of a format shared through the contracts section («общие миксины»). The owner gave an example: a consuming domain describes in its rules, especially in its technical appendix, what it does with the data, described by a RecordType, that it takes as input. The owner then rejected «не ссылается ... ни в типах своих полей, ни через refines» as a definition by negation. Fix-newform-331.mjs rewrote the node to say what a RecordType may refer to, in the types of its fields and through `refines`: only RecordTypes of its own domain and fragments of a format declared in the contracts section. It added a sentence saying that the rules of a domain may cite a RecordType of another domain, and added the owner's example as a second example.

Once the owner has agreed to 12.3.5, the following are to be changed, as the reviewer listed them: row 26 of table 10.1 (its text, its cost from "reported" to "refused", and its `rests` from 12.3.1 to the new sid); rule 7.1.14 of the appendix of the tools (s-ezq8csd433vj6fj), which is to say that the SpecSheriff tools refuse a RecordType that refers to another domain's RecordType instead of reporting it, stop checking the `consumes` links against RecordTypes, and also examine field types nested in other field types; the check itself, which the reviewer did not find in `validate.ts` and which is to be written with its test; the archive entry for 1.39; and the `meta.decisions` field of 12.3.5, where the decision of 2026-11-14 replaces the decision of 2026-11-04 on 1.39. The reviewer also found, in the wording that fix-newform-330.mjs wrote, that the following need to be changed: rows 26 and 28 of table 10.1; rules 7.1.14 and the third item of 7.1.16 of the appendix of the tools; and the reason in 12.3.2, whose statement that an identifier has the type of the identifying field now holds only for a field of type `identifier of` within one domain. Nodes 12.3.7 (s-z3dnru6qqdf9m6j) and 12.3.13 (s-xfwuvd3funutmsw) are to say that a RecordType variant is declared where the RecordType it refines is declared; until they do, they contradict 12.3.5. The `meta.decisions` field of 12.3.5 is to record that the node narrows the decision of 2026-10-31 on 1.24 (s-e877bnfa559tvk9), which established that an identifier has the type of the field that identifies the data record.

A reader found a gap in the agreed node 12.4.3: its list does not explain the order in its example. In the SpecSheriff tools (`domainOrder` in `skills/tools/src/model.ts`), the domains that no domain uses are placed in the order of their names, each preceded by the domains it uses, which are ordered in the same way. The list says only that names decide how two domains are ordered when the links leave a choice, and this does not explain why billing comes before accounts. Once 1.39 is settled, propose to the owner a correction to the list of 12.4.3 that states the rule the tools apply, and the fix to the English wording "not first by its name" → "not first, as the order of names alone would put it".

The owner agreed to node 12.4.3, "The order of the domains at the same level" («Порядок доменов одного уровня»), on 2026-11-14 («согласовано»); fix-newform-326.mjs recorded the agreement and applied the fixes that an EngStickler reader reported, to its English text only. Node 1.39 had lost its row on the page that shows the base specification when its merge into 12.3.1 was reversed; fix-newform-327.mjs placed it at 12.3.5 in the planned structure of part 12, right after the nodes on what a RecordType is, and renumbered nodes 12.3.5 to 12.3.14 of the structure as 12.3.6 to 12.3.15. Node 12.2.2 needs no change: a fragment of a format is declared in the contracts section, and 12.2.2 already requires the contracts section to be kept in a document of its own.

The owner agreed to node 12.4.2, "The diagram of the domains", on 2026-11-14 («согласовано»), together with moving the order of the domains into 12.4.3; fix-newform-323.mjs recorded the agreement. The owner had written every sentence of the earlier 12.4.2, so the reviewer's fixes went to the owner as a proposal. In the agreed wording, the SpecSheriff tools draw the diagram of the domains, and the diagram shows how the domains are connected, in the owner's words («как домены взаимосвязаны»). Node 12.2.3, which 12.4.2 cites, states a domain's links to the domains it uses, and so defines what "connected" means. A domain whose header names no level is placed in a row of its own, below the rows of the levels. The diagram is not the map of the specification: the map is a mind map, where a level is a branch and no `consumes` link is drawn. At present only the owner's personal viewer (`.claude/vt/viewer`) draws such a diagram, and neither plugin includes the viewer, so the result of T29 must ship in the same release as these nodes. The viewer now places a domain that has no level in the top row and orders the domains by name alone; T29 is to bring it into line with 12.4.2 and 12.4.3.

Node 12.4.3 keeps the sid of node 1.41, so 1.41 is not archived; this supersedes the note on archiving 1.41 in the paragraph on section 3. The map uses the order of the domains without the rows of the diagram, and the tools' tests check only that order. Node 1.39 cites 1.41 because of cycles; that citation is to point to 12.2.3 instead.

The owner agreed to node 12.4.1, "A domain's level in the hierarchy", on 2026-11-14 («согласовано»), together with moving the diagram into 12.4.2; fix-newform-320.mjs recorded the agreement. The opening sentence of 12.4.1 is the owner's definition of a level. Two of its sentences come from node 1.40 in its current wording, at the reviewer's suggestion: the list of levels runs from the top of the hierarchy down, and a level's number is its place in that list. The rule on a domain's level and the rule on the diagram are applied separately, which is why the diagram was moved out of 12.4.1. An EngStickler reader read the English text of 12.4.1, and fix-newform-322.mjs applied the fixes it reported to the English text only. When the drafts are moved into the specification files, the owner's decision of 2026-11-09 replaces the decision of 2026-10-27 on node 1.40 ("every domain stands at a level") in `meta.decisions`. The citations of node 1.41 in the map (s-zu8ne3wq84zchc4) and in 1.39 (s-5yvpn4zhuqmv5bq) are then changed to cite 12.4.1, 12.4.2 or 12.4.3.

The owner agreed to node 12.3.4 on 2026-11-14 («согласовано»); fix-newform-315.mjs recorded the agreement. An EngStickler reader is to read the English of 12.3.3 and 12.3.4.

The owner agreed to node 12.3.3 on 2026-11-14 («ок»); fix-newform-311.mjs recorded the agreement. The reviewer found that the proposal that fix-newform-306.mjs wrote for node 12.3.3 held two rules, each of which applies on its own, so fix-newform-309.mjs split that proposal. Node 12.3.3 keeps its sid and says that types and RuntimeChecks are generated from the RecordTypes. Its reason names a RecordType and a data format instead of "the description" and "the same structure".

The new node 12.3.4, "Data records conform to their RecordType" (sid s-wxcmpbvy9bzkfj9), implements the owner's ruling of 2026-11-14: a domain must not violate its own format, and a check is to be built on that rule. A domain outputs and writes to its store only data records that conform to their RecordType. The RuntimeCheck generated from that RecordType refuses a nonconforming data record before the domain outputs it or writes it to its store. The reviewer checked 12.3.4, and fix-newform-310.mjs applied the reviewer's fixes. The agent proposed a second sentence: a data record with a field that its RecordType does not describe does not conform to its RecordType. The owner called that sentence obvious on 2026-11-14 («очевидное утверждение»), and fix-newform-312.mjs removed it. The reason then said that the only sign of a renamed optional field is a field that its RecordType does not describe. The owner found that sentence unclear on 2026-11-14, so fix-newform-314.mjs removed it; the example still shows that case. JSON Schema accepts such a field by default, so rules 18 and 19 of the appendix of the tools are to require that the generated RuntimeCheck refuse it. The owner wrote the first sentence of the reason on 2026-11-14: the domains that rely on the format read another domain's data («Домены, которые читают данные другого домена»). Fix-newform-313.mjs put that sentence into the reason and brought the second sentence of the reason into line with it. The block «Оценка» shown with 12.3.4 also told the owner two things:

- an optional field that the code stops filling still passes the check;
- data records stored before their RecordType changed are refused when the domain outputs them, and no node says that the store must be migrated; in the round of questions on 2026-11-14, the owner said that the question of old data is not important now.

The owner's explanation that a domain reading another domain's data takes only the fields it needs was meant for the agent only and is not to be written into any rule. In the planned structure of part 12, nodes 12.3.4 to 12.3.13 were renumbered 12.3.5 to 12.3.14.

The owner renamed section 12.4 on 2026-11-14 from "Domains: levels" to "Domains: hierarchy" («Домены: иерархия»). The page that shows the base specification and the label of section 12.4 in gen-table-10-1.mjs carry the new title; the page of table 10.1 is to be regenerated and republished with it, and the section gets the new title when the drafts of part 12 are moved into the specification files. The owner renamed section 12.2 the same day from "Domains: division" to "Domains: decomposition" («Домены: декомпозиция»), on the page that shows the base specification and in the label of section 12.2 in gen-table-10-1.mjs. On the owner's instruction («да, приведи»), fix-newform-308.mjs changed the title of node 12.2.1 to "Decomposition of the specification" («Декомпозиция спецификации»); nothing else in the node changed. The owner also wants node 12.4.1, agreed to on 2026-11-09, to be reviewed again in light of the new title of section 12.4. It is to be proposed once nodes 12.3.3 and 12.3.4 have been agreed to, after the reviewer has checked it, together with the block «Оценка».

The owner agreed to node 12.3.2 on 2026-11-14 (the word the owner used: «Принято»), in the wording written by the scripts fix-newform-300.mjs through fix-newform-304.mjs; fix-newform-305.mjs recorded the agreement. An EngStickler reader read the English text of node 12.3.2, and fix-newform-307.mjs applied the fixes it reported to the English text only. The node, titled "What a RecordType contains" («Состав RecordType»), cites 12.3.1 for what a RecordType is and lists what a RecordType contains: its name, a description of what it is for, each of its fields, and the field that identifies its data records when another RecordType refers to it. A condition that involves several fields is written as a rule of its own, linked to the RecordType. The item about a data record holding a field that its RecordType does not describe has been removed from the node.

The owner agreed to node 12.3.1 on 2026-11-14 (the word the owner used: «согласовано»), in the wording written by the scripts fix-newform-282.mjs through fix-newform-298.mjs; fix-newform-299.mjs recorded the agreement. An EngStickler reader read the English text of node 12.3.1, and fix-newform-301.mjs applied the fixes it reported to the English text only. The node opens with the owner's paragraph: a RecordType is a data format that forms part of a domain's API, describing the data the domain outputs and keeps in its store, which other domains may use in whole or in part. Data in one format has one RecordType, declared in the only domain where data in that format first appears. The exception is a fragment of a format, such as an address (the owner's term «фрагмент формата»): fragments are declared in the contracts section, and any domain may reuse them. RecordType names are unique across the specification. The paragraph on the `consumes` links has been moved back into node 1.39 (s-5yvpn4zhuqmv5bq), which stays an approved node of its own, reversing the owner's earlier ruling to merge it into 12.3.1 (rule 7.7). A fragment that only one domain needs is also declared in the contracts section, while node 3.1 keeps in that section only what more than one section needs, except for terms; so 12.3.1 is to declare an `overrides` link to 3.1 when the drafts are moved into the specification files, and its text already cites 3.1, as 12.1.2 requires. The decision of 2026-11-13 on the data models and the answers given in the round of questions on 2026-11-14 are to be recorded in the `meta.decisions` field of node 12.3.1 when the drafts are moved into the specification files.

Nodes that are to change after 12.3.1: node 12.3.2, which is to cite 12.3.1 as the definition of a RecordType and to say that domains offer data rather than pass it; node 1.39, which goes to the archive (the owner, 2026-11-14), replaced by the new node 12.3.5; the approved nodes 1.28 and 1.29, which let domains add values to a field of a shared format, and the row of table 10.1 that concerns those values, all of which are to be removed or reworked; and node 1.32, to be narrowed so that other domains cannot extend one domain's format. The reviewer also found that the rows of table 10.1 about `consumes` and `identifier of` refer to 12.3.1 and should refer to 1.39 and 12.3.2 respectively, and that the comment on a RecordType in `node.schema.json` still says that domains pass data. In the step of T25 that changes the SpecSheriff tools, once the owner has agreed to node 12.3.5, write the test and then the code for the check it makes possible: the SpecSheriff tools detect a RecordType that refers to a RecordType of another domain in the types of its fields or through `refines`. The reviewer recommends refusing such a RecordType, as row 26 of table 10.1 is to say. Node 1.39 is to be moved to the archive, so the SpecSheriff tools are to stop issuing the report that node 1.39 requires. In the same step, remove the required property `unknown` of a RecordType from `node.schema.json`. Rules 18 (s-suv42g8r3ra2h6b) and 19 (s-nk8td3ez58gahc4) of the appendix of the tools let a RecordType node choose what happens to a field that nobody declared: rewrite them to require that the generated RuntimeCheck refuse such a field. `skills/tools/src/review.ts` passes `unknown` to the reviewer and must stop doing so.

What the owner settled in the round of questions:
- Node 12.2.1 keeps the wording the owner agreed to on 2026-11-08 (the owner, 2026-11-14). The proposal to add the data models to it has been withdrawn (fix-newform-281.mjs). A RecordType connects the parts of the system, as a cable connects blocks without being a block itself. So a specification divided into domains consists only of the domains and the contracts section they share. Node 12.2.1 mentions a RecordType only in its reason, which says that a domain describes its API in the form of RecordTypes.
- The data models of a domain are essentially part of its interface, a connector that other domains can plug into. A cable always joins a pair of connectors. Each connector may have a shape of its own, but the two must be compatible.
- The flow of data that the RecordTypes serve (the owner, 2026-11-14): a domain takes the data it needs from the store, from several domains if need be, does its work, and usually creates data of a new kind. Each domain writes its data into a table or an index of its own, never into a shared one, in a format that one of its RecordTypes defines. "RecordType" is a general term for the type of a data record that a domain outputs.
- Domains do not send data to one another. They offer it, as craftsmen offer their goods at a market, in a strictly defined format, and data in a changed format is different data (the owner's answer to question 3).
- So a domain defines the format of only the data it creates, and reads the data of other domains in the formats that the domains which created it defined (the owner's answer to question 2). A RecordType is the name given to the API of a domain's outgoing data. The data a domain saves in its store is its connector, so storage is not a separate purpose of a RecordType.
- Service messages, such as a notification or a report on the state of a domain, travel through a separate signalling channel. They are not business data, just as information on a machine tool's state travels separately from the workpieces passed from one machine tool to the next. How a domain learns of new data, through that channel or through triggers in the store, is a matter for the technical appendix.
- A change of format agreed with the domains that read the data is part of normal operation, and a change that has not been agreed breaks the contract. What happens to data already stored in the old format, migration or deletion, need not be decided now.

The owner asked whether the flow has an established name. The agent answered that it combines several patterns: Pipes and Filters (Pattern-Oriented Software Architecture, 1996; Enterprise Integration Patterns, 2003) for the chain of processing; data as a product, with a data contract, from Data Mesh (Dehghani, 2019) for a domain offering its data in a fixed format; Control Bus (Enterprise Integration Patterns), or the control plane apart from the data plane, for the signalling channel; and Shared Database (Enterprise Integration Patterns) for reading another domain's tables directly. Data Mesh comes closest to the owner's market analogy.
- Data in the same format has one name, so two domains that produce data in the same format use one RecordType (the owner's answer to question 4, 2026-11-14: «зачем то же самое должно иметь два имени»). In the owner's example, a domain that sanitizes data by removing or replacing secrets and personal data changes the content and keeps the format. That is the case that paragraph 2 of node 12.3.1 covers: a domain that passes data on with only its values changed keeps the RecordType and its name.
- Two domains that produce data in the same format are, to the owner, a sign of poor architecture (the owner's answer to question 6, 2026-11-14). A domain may take its incoming data from several sources, so the two import domains in the agent's example, one for files and one for an external API, should be a single domain. So every RecordType whose data a domain produces has exactly one domain that produces it, and that RecordType is declared in that domain. No part of the specification that belongs to no domain is needed to declare the RecordTypes of the data that domains produce. When answering question 5, the owner had chosen a part that belongs to no domain for data that two domains produce in the same format, and had suggested an intermediary domain for the example that consolidates such data. The owner then described both as hypothetical answers to a hypothetical question.
- A structure that the RecordTypes of several domains contain, such as an address or a sum of money, is declared in the data models (the owner's answer to question 7, 2026-11-14). The data models hold only such shared structures and no data that a domain produces, as a shared kernel does in domain-driven design. A RecordType contains such a structure through a field of type `record`.
- The shared data structures are part of the contracts section, kept in a document of their own (the owner's answer to question 8, 2026-11-14). The contracts section holds what the domains share: the terms and the shared data structures. They are kept in separate documents because a term and a data structure are written differently, as the owner decided on 2026-11-13. So nodes 12.2.1 and 3.1 keep the wordings the owner agreed to. The agent proposed the name «общие структуры данных» (shared data structures) in place of «модели данных», since «модель» is empty unless the thing it is a model of is named. The owner answered the question put in those words without objecting to the name. The name is to be confirmed when node 12.3.1 is shown.
- No separate check is to be added to ensure that a domain reading only part of another domain's data stays compatible with it (the owner's answer to question 9, 2026-11-14). Nodes 12.2.3, 12.2.4, 12.2.5 and 12.3.3 already cover this compatibility: the `consumes` links name the sections a domain reads, the index of the producing domain names the domains that read each section, a rule relying on a field of another domain's RecordType links to that RecordType, and the types in the code are generated from the RecordTypes.
- The guidelines describe no signalling channel (the owner's answer to question 10, 2026-11-14): it is one of several possible architectural choices, which comes up when examples are discussed, and not a topic for the guidelines. What a user is notified of, and when, is set by a rule of the domain that sends the notifications, and how a domain learns of new data is a matter for the technical appendix.

The round of questions is over: no question from it is still open.

The answers decide what changes in 12.3.1 (the definition of a RecordType, where a RecordType is declared, and an `overrides` link to 3.1 if a RecordType leaves the contracts section), in 12.3.2, in 12.2.2 and in the nodes listed below. The owner's term "data models" is recorded in the `vt` skill's `terms-ru.md`. Its meaning there follows decision 5 and is to be revised once the questioning ends.

The rule "Every wording is assessed against the whole specification before it is shown" in `.claude/vt/rules.md` (the owner, 2026-11-14) applies to every node shown from now on. The reviewer, `specsheriff-rule-reviewer`, reports on the five criteria as its check 8, and the agent itself assesses every wording that the reviewer does not check.

The agent's search found these nodes that the decision on the data models may affect, depending on the outcome of the questioning:
- 12.2.2, which lists the documents the specification is kept in;
- 12.3.1, whose third paragraph and reason declare a RecordType that no domain first provides in the contracts section. Its `meta.decisions` is to record the decision;
- 3.1, under which the rules binding more than one top-level section are agreed in the contracts section. A decision recorded on the current node 1.3 treats a RecordType node as a rule of its section;
- the row of table 10.1 on a list of values a domain adds, which names "a RecordType described in contracts";
- the current nodes 1.28 and 1.29, still to be moved into part 12.3, which name a RecordType described in the contracts section;
- where the rules linked to a RecordType are kept: the rules binding one field to another (12.3.2) and the lists of values the domains add (the current 1.28);
- the nodes of the appendix of the tools that say where the tools look for RecordTypes, and the node that describes the map of the specification;
- the English text of 12.3.1, where "is a data format" is to be replaced with "describes a data structure", as 12.3.2 says. Readers keep reading "first provides" as "provides at first"; consider "is the first to provide";
- the English text of 3.1, to be reworded after the grilling session: "The contracts section agrees on" is a calque, since a section cannot agree on anything. What the sections share is agreed in the contracts section;
- the SpecSheriff tools, at the step of T25 where they are changed: `isDomain` in `skills/tools/src/model.ts` would treat a folder `models` as a domain, and `validate.ts`, `values.ts` and the code in `generated.ts` that draws the map of the specification recognise only the contracts section (the reviewer, 2026-11-14).

The owner agreed to node 12.2.5 on 2026-11-14 in the wording written by fix-newform-271.mjs and fix-newform-273.mjs; fix-newform-274.mjs recorded the agreement. A node that relies on what another domain states has a link to the node stating it. The link may be of any type and may go to a node in any section of that domain. The `meta.todos` item naming the node that links to the new rule has been dropped. An EngStickler reader has read its English text, and fix-newform-276.mjs applied the fixes to the English alone.

The owner agreed to node 9.1 on 2026-11-14 in the wording written by fix-newform-268.mjs and fix-newform-269.mjs (fix-newform-270.mjs). An EngStickler reader then read its English text, and fix-newform-272.mjs applied the fixes to the English alone. The owner proposed «реализующий спецификацию, созданную по guidelines», and the agent recommended against it: a project's own guidelines are also written according to the SpecSheriff guidelines, so the code of a project's own checks would count as the system's code. The owner also proposed a separate rule allowing a person to write code by hand, but only as an exception. The agent recommended against such a rule, since the checks apply to every change in the same way, whoever made it, and no check can tell who wrote a line. Code written before its rule is allowed only under extension 12.6, "Adoption of an existing project". The owner accepted the wording without that rule. The owner's preference of 2026-11-14 that a translation render every nuance is recorded in the `vt` skill's rule "The Russian rendering of a node covers the whole node, as fully as the original".

The owner agreed to node 12.1.2 on 2026-11-14, with the proposal written by fix-newform-265.mjs included (fix-newform-266.mjs). That proposal adds the requirement that the text of a node declaring an `overrides` link cite the rule it changes. The owner agreed to node 2.1 on 2026-11-13 in the wording that fix-newform-245.mjs wrote: the appendix of the tools is a separate document and is not part of the guidelines. **Decision 5 of the owner, 2026-11-13, on the data models:** the RecordTypes leave the contracts section and form a separate part, the data models, kept in `specs/models/` alongside the base specification. On 2026-11-13 the owner asked whether RecordTypes should be moved out of the contracts: a term is a definition in prose, while a RecordType describes the structure of data that domains pass to one another, which is closer to a schema than to a term. The agent recommended moving the RecordTypes into a separate part, the data models, with a folder of its own, `specs/models/`, and leaving only the terms in the contracts section. The owner agreed that the description of RecordTypes belongs to the extension of the guidelines for domains, whose nodes 12.3.1–12.3.3, still drafts, describe the RecordType kind of node. The owner then doubted whether a RecordType should be described in prose rather than by a JSON Schema, since the data is ultimately a JSON object and a model that no machine can check would be a gap in the specification. The agent answered that a RecordType is already machine-readable: each field has a name, a type from a fixed list of neutral types (`$defs/neutralType` in node.schema.json), a flag showing whether it is required, and a description. The types in the code and the JSON Schema that checks the data at run time are both generated from a RecordType (12.3.3). The base specification names no technology (1.1.4), and the system's data need not be JSON. The owner agreed on 2026-11-13 to keep RecordTypes in that form. The owner then suggested keeping the data models in a folder next to the technical appendix, since data is specific to a particular implementation. The agent recommended keeping them alongside the base specification: the rules of the base specification refer to the data's fields, and the base specification must not depend on the technical appendix, which describes how it is implemented. How the data is represented, such as its serialization or its storage, belongs in the technical appendix and in the generated code.  Then propose new wordings for nodes 2.3 and 12.1.1, one at a time. It describes one implementation of the guidelines and changes as often as a technical appendix does, while the principle "Three documents, changing at three rates" says that the guidelines hardly change. What is checked, when, and what a failure costs stays in the rules of the guidelines, as node 2.1 says. The part of the appendix of the tools that concerns an extension goes into a section for that extension. (2) Each extension of the guidelines goes into a file of its own, `specs/_guidelines/extensions/<name>.json`, so that the agent working in a project reads only the extensions the project chose; node 12.1.1 gives this reason. The owner left this choice to the agent («сам реши»). When the drafts are moved into the files of the specification, record the decision in the `meta.decisions` field of node 12.1.1. The term appears in nodes 6.1, 12.1.2 and 12.1.3 and in checks 10.1.20 and 10.1.20a. No occurrence has been replaced yet. **The rule on shortening** was proposed by the owner on 2026-11-13: «Предложение нужно формулировать максимально коротко насколько это возможно, не теряя его основной мысли и правильной литературной формы». The owner was then asked about it one question at a time (the skill `grilling`) and settled the following:
- the rule is not a separate rule but the new wording of step 1, "Shorten", in the "Writing" section of the global CLAUDE.md («уточненая формулировка для Сократи»);
- shortening must keep the whole meaning, not only the main thought: no condition, exception or grammatical subject is cut («сохраняет весь смысл»);
- «правильная литературная форма» means that shortening produces none of the following («подходит»):
  - a sentence without a subject or a verb;
  - a string of nouns in place of a verb;
  - a function word left out where a careful native speaker would use it;
  - a label or an abbreviation in place of the words it stands for;
- the rule goes into the owner's global CLAUDE.md and also into the method's section "How a rule is written". That is section 6 of the current spec.json and section 7 of the new form of the base. The owner first said «только в моем CLAUDE.md» and then changed their mind: «хотя нет правило 6 тоже нужно скорректировать»;
- in that section, the rule goes in a new node right after node 6.3 of the current spec.json («новый узел после 6.3»). Adding it to 6.3 would contradict node 6.4. In the new form of the base, 6.3 is split into 7.5 and 7.6, so the new node comes after 7.6. Its key 7.17 is temporary, and the node is renumbered to follow 7.6 when the drafts of the new form are moved into the specification files;
- the rule applies only to what is written from check 10.1.26 on. Nodes the owner has already agreed to are brought into line with it when they are next changed («и применяем только дальше»).

The owner agreed to the new wording of step 1 (2026-11-13, «ок»). It is written into the global CLAUDE.md, together with two sentences giving the reason at the end of the section's *Why*. The copy of "Writing" in the vt skill's rules.md matches it. EngStickler's readers read the English text of the new wording before the owner saw it. Their fixes changed only the English, not the meaning.
