**The current item is table 10.1, as a node kind of its own for a table (the owner, 2026-11-11).** The design is agreed ("1"): `t25-pass/table-kind-design.html`, https://claude.ai/artifact/YJyt3PJchjuAVDsBsSassn (v1); the earlier drawing `rows-design.html` (KHq3sJu2EtZAsJX1HXgbZ7) is superseded by it.
- A table node: its head is the node itself, with its own sid and approval — title, text (what the table holds; it may require what holds of every row), reason, columns (a column may name where the code declares its values, and the tools then check them).
- Rows, chosen per table by need (the owner: optional, as examples; the rule is in .claude/vt/rules.md): simple — cells inside the node, no sid, the table one rule approved, implemented and tested whole; or nodes — own sid, cells by the head's columns, no title (the owner: a table's heading is its header row), own links, tests and approval.
- Kept from the same day's rulings: a row node is approved alone, and the table of checks may be approved whole by one command on its sid, writing an approval for every draft row (the owner chose the table of checks only, against any section; at the kind's turn, ask whether that covers every table with node rows). A row links to the base rules it checks by a new link type `checks`, set by hand, the rule's incoming link built (the rules are in table-10-1.json `rests`, all 44 rows). A test names the same node as the code it tests (from agreed 9.2 and the SpecTag node's reason): here the tools' code carries no SpecTag (§s-hheus6f395ynqvc) but implements the tools node, so each of the 52 tests names the tools/7.x node of its check, and a row shows its tests through `implements`.
- Dropped: the kind CheckRule (its terms-ru row is replaced at the naming); 10.1 as a section; moving "Each check reads…" to 10.2 — it stays in the head of 10.1 as a requirement of every row.
- The survey of tables (scratchpad survey-tables.mjs): the table of checks (node rows); tools 20, field types (simple rows); 2.2 link types (a list plus a bullet per meaning, written twice); `TextRule.list` in 1.5, 1.27, tools 10, the saloon's levels and generated files (one-column tables declared in code); the 10.2 draft "When the checks run" (moment → checks); RecordType fields left to their kind.
- The kinds are named `TableRule` (the table, beside TextRule) and `TableRow` (a row made a node), Latin in Russian (the owner, 2026-11-11, "1"; in terms-ru.md, replacing CheckRule).
- `TextRule.list` stays beside TableRule; a node chooses either (the owner, 2026-11-11, "2", over replacing `list` and over a one-column border). Whether 2.2, the link types, becomes a table is asked at its turn, by need.
- A table may still be written in a node's text; a TableRule is proposed by need, where a column is to be checked against the code, the head requires of every row, or the rows need to be nodes (the owner, 2026-11-11, "1"; the rule in .claude/vt/rules.md widened to it).
- **A cell, not a row, is where a node stands** (the owner, 2026-11-11, "2", over the row as the node: more flexible, and tables are built so in practice; a column may hold a variant of a rule with its own implementation, and binding to the row would not allow that): each cell of a TableRule is a small container holding text or a node; the owner needs no other content. So the kind TableRow is withdrawn; a node in a cell is of an existing kind (a rule, a term) and has no title, its column and row naming it; approval is per node in a cell, or the table whole by one command; the `checks` link and the tests go from the node in the cell. The design page shows cells and every ruling so far (v2).
- The text cells of a row and the heading of a column belong to the wording of every node in that row or column: a change to either drafts those nodes (the owner, 2026-11-11, "1"). So a check and its cost stay one requirement, approved together and implemented by one tools node.
- 10.1 is one TableRule whose rows fall into two groups, each opened by a heading row across the table, "The specification on its own" (25 checks) and "The specification and the code" (19); a group heading is text of the rows under it, so it belongs to the wording of their nodes (the owner, 2026-11-11, "1", over two TableRules and over a column repeating the group). A TableRule may group its rows.

**How the review goes now (the owner, 2026-11-10: "нам нужно уже продвигаться дальше").** A section's reviewer fixes go in at once, only the nodes are shown, and a question is asked only where the owner must decide. A question names outright the node, its section, the present wording and the proposed wording. A question on wording is not combined with asking for agreement. A node may be split within its section without asking (`.claude/vt/rules.md`). `insert-node.mjs` and `remove-node.mjs` also shift numbers inside `changes`, so a number of a present node can go wrong; sections 6 and 8 used scripts of their own. A new section goes to a fresh `specsheriff-rule-reviewer`. Its report lies in `C:/Users/PC/.claude/projects/D--SpecSheriff/<session>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`, and where the agent sent an addition the report is the longest text message, not the last. The page is checked in the browser by `evaluate_script`, since `take_screenshot` on a background tab hangs.

**Owed to section 10 and part 12: an extension for an existing project taking up the method (the owner, 2026-11-11, «чтоб не забыть»), named by the owner the same day "Adoption of an existing project" / «Адоптация существующего проекта» (over Migration, Import, Conversion and «Внедрение…»; in terms-ru.md).** The specification is used in two contexts: a new project, which writes its specification first and implements it after, and an existing project moving to the method. The main rules should hold the first only; what holds only while an existing project migrates goes to an extension of the guidelines of its own, with the rules that matter then. To be weighed when its turn comes, from the agent's reading: migration-only today are the leniency for code and tests a commit leaves alone without a SpecTag (11.2, current 5.2, s-kkbqcb9xrpaepwn), the third paragraph of the reason of the agreed 1.3.2 (a migrating project holds code and tests written earlier), the clause of 1.3.2's first item citing s-kkbqcb9xrpaepwn, and the skill `spec-adopt`; the draft status of the specification as a whole serves a new project as well (approved rules without code yet, no tests at git push: 11.3, current 5.3, s-xhcmchcngykvn2k), so it stays a rule of the SpecSheriff guidelines; moving between releases of SpecSheriff (`sheriff reindex` rewriting what an earlier release wrote) is another migration, the tools' own, which belongs to the appendix of the tools, not to this extension. Agreed 1.3.2 is then changed and checked whole, as the owner's ruling of 2026-11-11 says. At the move, a todo on 12.1.1 (s-mbmnsnxenmavu3z) if not settled before.

RU: «Отдельного домена, который первым предоставляет RecordType, нет, например, если каждый из нескольких доменов сам формирует данные, которые описывает этот RecordType.» Its ground: "first provides" can be read as "the domain written first". **A separate part for what only some projects need, being grilled at the owner's request (2026-11-09).** Their proposal: a part of the guidelines, "optional capabilities" or an appendix, for what is declared optional, such as domains and the visualization of domains by levels: extensions a project may take or not. Keeping them beside the basic rules inflates the guidelines and makes them harder to agree; sections 2 and 3 could move there. Measured: of those sections' 13 drafts, 2.3, 2.4, 2.5, 2.6 and 3.3 do not depend on domains; outside them 16 of 55 drafts name a domain, agreed ones of section 1 among them; 17 of 57 nodes of tools.json; agreed 2.1 says a specification not divided into domains counts as one domain; agreed 2.5 calls the SpecSheriff guidelines the rules every project shares and 2.6's reason says they come whole; `sheriff guide` prints all of them at every load of the skill `spec`. The owner's answer on what a project not using the part sees (2026-11-09): the guidelines hold a separate part, named for example «Дополнительные возможности», with the division of the base into domains, the exchange between domains in RecordTypes, and everything that has no meaning without domains. RecordType gets a subsection of its own there, and 3.4 is divided into smaller rules, one thought each. The plugin is decomposed too: the main skill is always loaded and holds the basic part only; another skill, such as one for domains, with the tools tied to it, is loaded only where the main skill sees the specification divided into domains. Branches left, one question at a time, after which the design is drawn on a page before anything moves: (2) which rules go. The owner on a RecordType without domains, 2026-11-09, thinking aloud: it was conceived to unify the data domains exchange; a type abstract enough for any project is a god object; the guidelines, as rules for keeping a specification, begin to dictate architectural patterns, which is not their aim; and a specification without domains is not really one domain, its division is by sections grouping rules by meaning. The owner accepted, 2026-11-09, as the root of this branch, the boundary that the main part holds only how a specification is kept and implemented, and whatever prescribes how the system is organised (domains, contracts, levels, RecordTypes) is an additional capability. The agent's earlier recommendation of a RecordType apart from domains is withdrawn. So storage leaves the definition of a RecordType, `sheriff types` applies only with RecordTypes, and 2.1's "counts as one domain" goes. Domains, RecordTypes and levels form one additional capability with subsections, since the owner ties them all to domains and 2.1's agreed reason has a domain describe its API in RecordTypes. Contracts stay in the main part (the owner, 2026-11-09, over the agent's recommendation to name no place): any specification may need to agree common terms, and they want a strictly defined place for it. Contracts adapt to the specification's size: a section of its own in a specification made only of sections, a separate document shared by all the domains where there are several. So a term is defined once, in contracts; what 3.1 says of a domain's API conventions goes to the part on domains; validate.ts:86 and `partOf` must count a contracts section inside a file. Whether the boundary also goes into `docs/principles.md` is asked later. Measured: `sheriff guide` prints 283 KB here at every load of the skill `spec`; nodes about domains or RecordTypes hold about 38% of spec.json's text and 29% of tools.json's; (3) how the rules left behind name a domain; (4) whether the part is agreed later, and 2.5 and 2.6; (5) settled, the owner 2026-11-09: both are combined. `sheriff guide` prints the rules on domains where the specification has domains, deterministically, and prints an index of the additional capabilities, each with when it is needed and the skill to use. The index covers what the script cannot see, a project without domains that wants to start using them. A skill of a capability holds a command printing its rules and any procedure of its own, such as dividing a specification into domains, never the rules themselves. The agent's decision: the program `sheriff` is not divided, since an agent reads only what the guide prints and the checks on domains act only where domains exist; the rules in tools.json on domains go to the additional part. The owner, 2026-11-09: the boundary is written down, one sentence each, in 1.1.3 "What the guidelines govern" (s-efe2cmw9asrbt2g) and in the principle on the three documents. The reviewer's verdict: not ready. To apply, in the wording shown once the name is chosen: one sentence each, "Whatever prescribes how the system is organised, such as domains and the RecordTypes they exchange, belongs to the [name], which a project uses only by its own choice" (the example changed from "a division into domains", since 2.1 makes a domain a part of the specification); 1.1.3's reason gains "The guidelines are rules for keeping a specification. How a system is organised is the project's architectural decision."; the name gets a defining node, cited from 1.1.3. The part is inside the SpecSheriff guidelines by the owner's words, so 2.5 ("the rules every project shares") and 2.6 ("come whole") change at their turn. A project chooses by having domains; at the tools step `isDomain` (model.ts:244) must not treat every other folder as a domain. The name, the owner 2026-11-09: "extension", «расширение», over "additional capabilities" and the reviewer's "optional module": it extends the base rules, a project picks what it needs, others than domains may come, and it expresses what 1.1.3 says of extending the guidelines. Always "extension of the guidelines" (in `terms-ru.md`); "the extension of the schema" a project's own guidelines add keeps its phrase, and bare "the extension" in 1.2, tools/1 and tools/7.1.2 takes its object at their review. The wording passed the reviewer and is shown to the owner, not yet written anywhere: "Whatever prescribes how the system is organised, such as domains exchanging data, belongs to the extensions of the guidelines, which a project uses only by its own choice." (RU «Всё, что предписывает, как устроена система, например домены, которые обмениваются данными, относится к расширениям guidelines, и проект использует их только по своему выбору.»), in 1.1.3 and in the principle; 1.1.3's reason gains "The guidelines are rules for keeping a specification. How a system is organised is the project's architectural decision." The owner's answer, 2026-11-09: base rules should know nothing of extensions and not mention them, since an extension may vanish or be a draft and a reference to it is fragile. So 1.1.3 stays as agreed, that sentence and the reason's addition are dropped. The owner's refinement on the page: the ban covers a base rule's text and reason; the tool builds incoming links from the extensions into its metadata, as it does across the specification, so the rule knows who uses it. The dependency runs from an extension to the base rules, the index is built from what the extensions declare, and the base rules name no domain either (1.1.2 and the 16 drafts). The principle may still state the boundary, naming no particular extension, after the reviewer. Who writes an extension, the owner: internal extensions, shipped with the plugin, may change the base logic where needed, since the tools shipped with them follow the change; a project may not, since it cannot be sure of working tools (the ground of 1.1.3's reason). How a reader of a base rule learns an extension changed it, the owner 2026-11-09: by a link of its own type from the extension's node, shown among the rule's incoming links as `exempts` is (2.2), refused in a project's own guidelines. The design is drawn on the page https://claude.ai/artifact/6EDjYMZPS1Yf2Ud8QocGGH (`t25-pass/extensions-design.html`, built by `gen-extensions.mjs` from the drafts; the classification of nodes is in that script) and shown to the owner, who is asked whether the distribution of the nodes is right. Nothing has moved. After their word: the plan with an estimate, the main rules agreed first and the extension after them (the agent's decision on the order), the defining node, the link type's name, the principle's sentence, each through the reviewer. First real case: contracts as a section in the base, a separate document with domains; (6) the name; (7) the task: T25 grows, or the decomposition of the plugin becomes a task of its own.

**The reason of 3.4 is being grilled at the owner's request (2026-11-09).** Their view: the reason is too large and should answer why the entity RecordType exists and what problem it solves. A RecordType is an attempt to unify the data domains exchange. The main idea of a domain is that its logic is encapsulated, so each domain has a fixed API through which it both receives and gives data. So that one domain knows exactly what will arrive, the other provides it in a strictly agreed format, and both keep a contract. A RecordType is not the whole API, only the part for the data processed. The proposal on the reason further below waits on this. Branches, one question at a time: (1) the node split into four, since the view explains only the definition, asked; (2) storage: the node's first paragraph and 8.1 say a RecordType is also for storage, the view speaks of exchange only; (3) the word "contract" against the part called contracts, since a RecordType is declared in a domain; (4) "not the whole API" against the agreed sentence of 2.1's reason, "A domain describes its API in the form of RecordTypes", and where the rest of an API is described.

**Where 3.4 stands:** node 3.4 "RecordType" (sid s-7vm323mhry2enx3) is to be shown again after the reviewer's findings (`fix-newform-44.mjs`, on the page). The owner's ruling, 2026-11-09: the possibility of a RecordType in contracts stays, since cutting possibilities is not the right way; a RecordType may have a common part, shared by all the domains using it, and that part stands in contracts. Only the example of a store was replaced: "A RecordType with no such domain is declared in contracts" stays, since without it the common part reads as holding for every RecordType. It is followed by "That RecordType is the format of the part common to the data of all the domains using it", and the reason's second paragraph gained a sentence. On their agreement: `agree.mjs`. Next, section 3 is checked for consistency and completeness, as sections 1 and 2 were. Then the review goes on in the page's order, sections 4–12, with 7.4 and 7.5. At the move into spec.json this ruling goes into the node's meta.decisions.

**The reviewer of a drafted rule** is the personal agent `specsheriff-rule-reviewer` (`~/.claude/agents/`, the owner, 2026-11-09), which loads the skill `specsheriff-terminology-review` from the owner's account and so checks the terms of the new text against the accepted vocabularies, ArchiMate 4 among them. The skill `archimate` made that morning was deleted at their word. The licence on ArchiMate's publication page forbids using its text with generative AI, so it is never read. Node 3.4 goes to this reviewer first. A review of the names of the whole revision, by the same skill, is sensible before the base moves into spec.json.

**Comments at a session's start:** a watch started by hand on the page does not arm the automatic replies, a publish of the page does. `ArtifactComments` with `action: watch` and no url shows whether they are armed.

**Comments:** automatic replies stay on, since only they bring the owner's comments here (the
owner, 2026-11-07; `vt` rules.md). At each notification: the thread whole, the automatic reply
checked against the selection and corrected at once where wrong, the change made, answered with
the version, the thread read again, resolved.

an approved specification, since 1.3.2 now says that while the specification is a draft an approved
rule need not have code (the owner, 2026-11-08); `sheriff binding` only reports an unimplemented rule, in both modes.

**For 4.1, at its review:** 1.1.2 now says the appendix inherits the structure of the base where
the two overlap, and `sheriff validate` holds that at the level of domains only (it refuses an
appendix section named after no domain). In the owner's larger project an appendix section within a domain takes
the title of a base section, and only those with something technical to say exist (13 of 13 such
sections match, and only 5 domains have an appendix section). 4.1 says only "a section for each
domain it implements". Raise whether it should say so.

Its paragraph on tests repeats the sentence the owner added to 1.4.1
("The test must contain a reference to the rule"). Raise how the two divide.

**Section 3, state and notes for the move:** the order of the domains (current 1.41, used only for display) was merged into 3.7; 1.41 goes to the archive, with s-nkyty4dy9x2v2a4 as its replacement, joined to it by a supersedes link, and the citations of 1.41 in current 4.1 and 1.39 are moved to s-nkyty4dy9x2v2a4. The sentence on the API is theirs, "A domain uses it in its API" (`fix-newform-41.mjs`). The sentence on contracts rewritten at their comment that its meaning escaped them, reviewed (`fix-newform-42.mjs`): "A RecordType with no such domain is declared in contracts". To be agreed again. The owner then asked why a RecordType can stand in contracts at all, since a store only keeps data formed earlier: the store example is wrong (no ground recorded for it). The real case put to them: several domains each form one format, none receiving it from another (a common envelope of events), which the rule on values the domains add, s-96df5fcfqsvex9d, relies on. If they keep the case, the example is replaced through the reviewer. Then a pass over section 3, which ends at 3.7. The node on showing a level was taken out of the base (a detail that belongs in the appendix, T29), so section 3 ends at 3.7; 3.5, 3.6 and 3.7 are agreed to; **the owner: a domain's level is optional, used when the domains are visualized**, so at 3.7 "Every domain stands at a level" must be changed to match, and at the tools step validate.ts:247-248 and the row of the table of checks must accept a domain's header with no level, the field in node.schema.json (with its comments) must stop being required, and the rule on the map (s-zu8ne3wq84zchc4) must say where such a domain is placed on the map (the map already has a branch "No level listed"); 3.4 "RecordType" (the owner's title) is agreed to (`fix-newform-35.mjs`): the owner found the node on entities (current 1.37, sid s-ce9wbaut6edfuy8) a duplicate of 3.2 and 3.3; its two statements and its reason were moved here. At the move into spec.json: 1.37 goes to the archive, with s-7vm323mhry2enx3 as its replacement, joined to it by a supersedes link, and the implements link of the table row moves to s-7vm323mhry2enx3. 3.2 and 3.3 are agreed. Section 3 was renumbered on 2026-11-08 by inserting 3.3: and 1.37 then merged away, so section 3 ends at 3.10: notes below written before that say 3.5–3.10 for the same nodes, and 3.3 for 1.37. Node `text` comment in node.schema.json says "What must hold", which no longer fits a term: to change at the tools step. 3.1 is agreed, any term counting as shared. For the tools step, the reviewer recommends keeping contracts in specs/contracts/ in every case, so that the tools do not change. Section 2 is agreed as a whole; 2.6 titled "A project's interaction with the SpecSheriff guidelines", text theirs. 2.5 "A project's own guidelines" is agreed: its own rules are a possibility, only those beyond the owners, the approval rule and the levels are optional, and they refine or extend. **At the tools step:** the owner holds that a project's guidelines never implement the SpecSheriff guidelines; every such rule today uses `refines`, so drop `implements` from validate.ts:463 and the row of the table. 2.4 is agreed in the reviewer's wording, so 2.3 stays as agreed. The doubt on 1.1.6 is settled: the SpecSheriff guidelines are a part with owners of its own, where they are written. 2.3 "The structure of the guidelines" is agreed, its first paragraph theirs. **The owner, 2026-11-08: no "base of the guidelines"; in that context they are simply the guidelines** (it confuses them with the base specification). Done in 2.3, 2.4 and 2.5; to carry out in the row of the table in 12.1, in the root text of tools.json and in the row of terms-ru on the SpecSheriff tools. 2.1 and 2.2 are agreed; 2.2's second paragraph is a permission, since a recommendation cannot stand in a rule, and the sentence on size left the reason of 1.1.2.

**Contracts, the owner's ruling of 2026-11-08, for 3.1 and 3.2:** contracts exist where needed. With one domain they are a section within the specification; for several domains, a separate document. A specification so small that its rules do not overlap needs none. So a term stands in contracts wherever those are; at the tools step `partOf` (model.ts) reads contracts only as the directory of that name, and validate.ts:86 refuses a term elsewhere, so a contracts section inside a domain's file has to count.

**Earlier state, kept for its notes:** the owner's word on 2.1 "The division of the specification" (shown whole, as it stands, sid s-h6h25ctd3yryudh): the agreed 1.1.2 says the base may be divided where needed, 2.1 says it is made of contracts and one domain or several. Its reason now holds the owner's "A domain describes its API in the form of RecordTypes" (`fix-newform-30.mjs`); the next sentence still says "interface", put to them. Its condition and a paragraph on contracts are in, reviewed. Section 1 is agreed as a whole; 1.6.1 last. 1.5.2 is agreed, titled "Every code symbol has a SpecTag" (the owner: this is the section on code, tests are described above). - **How the review is done** (`vt` rules.md, all the owner's of 2026-11-07/08): an exact wording of
  the owner's goes in word for word, only spelling and punctuation put right; a comment on a
  selection acts on that selection only (the tool shows the selected words without their place:
  where they occur twice, ask); only what was asked is changed, a following change elsewhere is
  proposed and waits; an agreed wording is not reworded, not even by a proposal, unless asked;
  every automatic reply is checked against the selection and corrected in the thread.
- **Agreed** (`agree.mjs`, a badge on the page; each approved by `npx sheriff approve` once it
  stands in spec.json with that wording): 1.1.1–1.1.5 (2026-11-07; 1.1.2 again on 2026-11-08, with
  the paragraph on dividing the base), 1.1.6 "The owners" (2026-11-08), 1.2.1–1.2.6, 1.3.1, 1.3.2 and 1.3.4 (2026-11-08). The
  owner rewrote much of them: 1.1.2 "how the specification is kept and implemented"; 1.1.3 the
  guidelines' own scope; 1.1.4 three short sentences and the example-in-brackets paragraph; 1.1.5
  "The technical appendix describes how the rules are implemented"; 1.2.1 "One node, one rule";
  1.2.2 "A node, and so the rule, is cited by its sid"; 1.2.3 the text what is required, the reason
  why; 1.2.5 "The one source of truth is the specification, not the code"; 1.2.6 "Only what the
  rules require is done". The drafts in `newform.json` and their `changes` hold every wording.
- **Waiting on the owner besides 1.3.1:** 7.4 "A rule is dropped where it governs another process
  or states the obvious" (from 1.13, its reason now also holding 6.12's two sentences on
  prohibitions); 7.5 "An exception is written into the rule it narrows" (from 6.12).
- **Into 1.1.2's decisions at the move** (the owner, 2026-11-08): the base may be divided; the
  appendix inherits its structure where the two overlap, worded so over "on the same principle",
  which left open which principle, and over a bare "inherits", which read as every section repeated.
- **Into 1.1.6's decisions at the move** (the owner, 2026-11-08): the owners are a node of their
  own, introduced before their first use (the former draft 9.4, `fix-newform-24.mjs`). "Accountable"
  was weighed and set aside: RACI has one Accountable per task while a part may have several

- **The owner's rulings of the review so far:** 1.1.2 names the three parts and 1.1.3–1.1.5 unfold

## The new structure of the base

The owner, 2026-11-07, at the showing of 1.1: the first rule keeps one declaration only — the
specification is the set of its nodes, and the nodes are linked. The text on generation is secondary,
not superfluous, so it goes towards the end. The guidelines go from the general to the particular: the
first rules as short as commandments, every refinement and exception after them. The owner asked for
the structure to be drawn before the review goes on ("Рисуй").

The drawing, as proposed:

- The order follows the flow of a change, the owner's ruling of 2026-11-07: node, rule, approval, test,
  code; the checks hold the whole flow and stand last. Rules are grouped by what they bind together.
- 1 The foundations: sixteen short commandments in subsections 1.1 The specification, 1.2 The rule,
  1.3 Approval, 1.4 The test, 1.5 The code, 1.6 The checks. Three are new: the declaration of 1.1;
  the parts of the specification and the question each answers (the owner's comment: a new reader
  must first learn that the base says what and the appendix how); "Everything that bears on a rule
  stands in its node". 1.16 is said from the side of the appendix: technology is named there. The
  others are existing nodes moved whole (1.13, 6.12, 1.14) or the main thought of a split node (1.3,
  1.4, 1.12, 1.7, 1.8, 3.2, 3.4, 3.5, 5.1). The owner's comments on the page placed the rule on
  owners after "Everything that bears…", and "Code changes only…" after "The test comes before…".
- 2 The parts of the specification; 3 Contracts and the domains; 4 The technical appendix; 5 Nodes;
  6 Links; 7 How a rule is written; 8 Data structures; 9 Status and approval; 10 Tests and code
  (was Code); 11 What is generated from the nodes (was The map); 12 The checks and the procedures.
- 1.2.3 reads "A rule's text and its reason are held apart in one node", in Russian «Описание
  правила и его обоснование хранятся раздельно, но в одном узле» (the owner's comment).
- The parts commandment names the guidelines as a third part; the owner named two. Said so in the
  conversation; dropped if the owner wants two.
- The owner, 2026-11-07: wordings never change with the structure. The page shows every present node
  under its present title, and each commandment with its present text rendered faithfully. A split is
  marked on the node that divides, and its parts stand where they will go, named "part of …". Their
  wordings, and whether 1.1 adds that the nodes are linked, are settled at each node's review. The
  two new nodes (the parts of the specification, everything in its node) keep their gist. The rule is
  in the `vt` skill's `rules.md`: "A change of order changes no wording".
- The owner, 2026-11-07: they approve the new form, not the present one, so the page shows each node
  as it will stand. A split redistributes the present sentences verbatim; new words only where
  unavoidable, each declared in the node's list of changes. Drafts: `t25-pass/newform.json` (the
  commandments and the parts leaving them, 32 nodes); `apply-newform.mjs` puts them on the page;
  `fix-newform-1.mjs` applied the reviewer's 32 findings (exceptions kept with the rules they narrow:
  1.3.1 names 9.5, 1.5.2 names 10.6, 11.1 names 11.2). Sids: 1.1 → 11.1, 1.3 → 5.4, 1.4 → 5.5,
  1.7 → 1.3.1, 1.8 → 1.3.2, 1.12 → 1.2.3, 3.2 → 10.8, 3.4 → 1.4.1, 3.5 → 1.5.2, 5.1 → 12.1.
  Links to retarget at the move (the reviewer): 10.4, 11.4 (5.6) and tools/6 on waiting → 11.2; 1.38
  → 9.4; 5.12's reason + 9.5; citations of 3.4 in 5.7, 5.12, 1.23 → 10.4, in 5.2, tools 7.2.4,
  7.2.9 → 10.7; 6.4's refines link → 1.2.1; 12.1's implements link is also to point to 1.2.2; the current 5.5 must also cite 1.6.1.
  Open for the node reviews: whether 1.5.2 and 10.7 join symbols and tests apart (6.4); "main" in
  the owner's title of 1.1.2 (every part is one of the three). 1.1.1 by the owner's comment
  (page v13): the text adds that the nodes are linked by a structure and by links of several
  types; the reason, a graph not a list, says nothing of rules (they are 1.2's); the link to
  6.3 added; the alternative offered, the links in the reason only. The owner then cut two of its
  reason's sentences (graph not a list; meaning by section and links), page v14. 1.1.2 by the
  owner's comment: titled "The basic structure of the specification" («Базовая структура
  спецификации»), its former title the first sentence of its text (`fix-newform-4.mjs`).
  Batch two reviewed (14 findings) and fixed by `fix-newform-5.mjs`, page v15: 9.2 excludes
  sections and headers; the archive exempt in both 6.1 and 6.2; 3.1 names 3.2 and 3.4; 3.6 says
  "carries" through 3.5 and grounds its index; titles of 12.12 and 4.2; the retargets completed
  (check table implements → 3.2, 2.5, 6.1, 8.1, 2.4; docs/README.md → 2.5, 12.11; tools/17's
  reason → 4.2, its text "as the rule on the generated types requires"; tools/11 implements 9.2,

  Declined: 6.1's title stays the present title's half (no rewording). Doubts for the node
  reviews: 2.6's reason states a requirement (approved as where written); 4.1's "detail an
  instance needs" lost its ground to 4.2; 7.3 reads "Such a sentence" one way; 8.1's last sentence
  has no ground. 1.1.1's reason is now the owner's sentence alone (page v18; the former first

  no ground of its own, as a definition; the owner was told and did not object.
- The second batch, the splits outside the commandments, 27 drafts by `fix-newform-3.mjs` (English
  taken paragraph by paragraph from spec.json, every change an explicit replacement; `coverage.mjs`

  1.23 and tools/1 on the extension → 5.2; tools/7.1.2 + 5.2; tools/11's reason on withdrawal → 9.2;
  1.18 and 1.31 on the instance → 4.3; 5.1's implements of 1.21 → 2.4; to 2.5 the citations of
  1.22 meaning what a project's own guidelines hold (1.40's text and decision, 3.6's reason, the
  decisions of 1.17 and 1.28); 1.25, 1.26, 1.27's refines → 8.1; 1.37 → 3.2; the two index tests
  and 1.39's reason → 3.6; to 6.1 the citations of the rule on citing (2.2's text, tools/7.1.7,

  Open: 8.1's last sentence (rules binding fields) had no ground in the present node either.
- Under each commandment the page held its present node whole, folded (replaced by the new form): the English from spec.json,
  the Russian from `t25-pass/full-ru.json` (faithful, reusable at the node's review). Titles on the

  specification is made of three main parts: its guidelines, its base and its technical appendix";
  1.1.3 "The base specification states what the system does and names no technology".
- The pass's split recommendations are built in. The old 1.1's sid goes with the text on generation

- To settle at each node's showing: which part of a split keeps the sid; the English wording, after the

- After the agreement: the restructure is drafted whole and deduplicated, then shown one node at a time

  a rule is written" is the last section of `spec.json`: that line is corrected. tools.json and

- The Russian of "set" in 1.1 is «совокупность», the owner's word (in `terms-ru.md`). The rule "A

## Where the working files are

`.ai_docs/t25-pass/` (private, ignored by git), copied from the first session's scratchpad, every
script repointed there:

- `pass/applied.json` — every rewritten node: sid → location, `old` and `new` (title, text, reason).
- `pass/recs.json` — the recommendations: `splits` (47: 36 split, 11 keep, each with its new nodes and
  the tests to re-tag) and `doubts` (65: 16 `ambiguity`, 21 `confirm`, 28 `informational`).
- `pass/gen-page.mjs` — builds `pass-page.html`, the page "Проход по guidelines".
- `pass/RULES.md`, `REVIEW.md`, `extract.mjs`, `verify.mjs`, `fix.mjs`, `fixes-*.json`, `apply.mjs`,
  `g*.in/out/review.json` — how the pass was made.
- `coverage.mjs` — counts the nodes changed against HEAD (117 of 131 rules in wording, 2 new, every
  rule's `kind` renamed).
- `sync-tests.mjs`, `decide.mjs` — `meta.tests` synced from the SpecTags; a decision added to a node.
- `queue-outline.md`, `queue-map.html`, `names-of-the-method.html` — the outline of the queue map, the map built from it and the `vt` template, and the vocabulary page.

The pages are artifacts of the first session. A new session republishes them by their URL, reading
each first with the Artifact tool:

- the pass page https://claude.ai/artifact/D9CPTMCxG7AHbtVZokTYrg;
- the queue map https://claude.ai/artifact/2sAk1z1MZTsTnRqGSkqGky;
- the vocabulary page https://claude.ai/artifact/Mojs1KJXp1mTAPbPZnajw2 (version 10, settled).

## The owner's review of every rule

The owner, 2026-11-07: the whole document needs agreeing in substance, so every rule is reviewed by
them, one at a time, from the start. The approvals given in their name by the pass stand meanwhile.

- **Order.** `spec.json` from 1.1 to its end, then `tools.json`, then `project.json`, each part done
  whole before the next (the rule "One part at a time" in `.claude/vt/rules.md`: the message moving to
  another part opens with a line in capitals). Sections are shown with their nodes, never for an
  approval. 6.3 and 6.4 the owner approved in this task; they are named in their place in one line
  and passed over unless the owner asks.
- **Showing a rewritten node** (rules.md "How a node is shown"): the title as the heading, the
  location and sid; the original, as HEAD holds it, in English, then in Russian; the new wording as
  it stands in the file, in English, then in Russian; then a list in Russian of what changed, each
  place, what it said and what it says now. The original and the new are in `pass/applied.json`.
  A node whose wording did not change (14) is shown once, saying it is unchanged.
- **With the node** go its own findings from `pass/recs.json`: a split recommended by 6.4, an
  ambiguity or a reading to confirm, with the recommendation. One question per message. Where a node
  has a doubt, that question comes first, since the wording waits on it. Where a split is
  recommended, the split is asked first, with the new nodes; each new node is then shown for
  approval. Otherwise the question is whether the wording is approved.
- **On the owner's "ок"** the approval stands as it is. The node is marked reviewed in
  `pass/reviewed.json` (sid → date and result), to be created at the first answer.
- **On a correction** the new wording is written with status `draft`, shown again, and approved by
  `sheriff approve <sid>` on the owner's "ок".
- **A split** keeps the node's sid for its main requirement and gives new sids to the parts carried
  out apart, so no node is removed. The tests of those parts move to the new sids: their SpecTags are
  rewritten, then `sync-tests.mjs`. Where a split would remove a node after all, the archive rule
  (§s-f2b5zvc565s242f) is read first: a node an approval was ever given for goes to `specs/_archive/`.
- **The pass page is kept current** as the review goes: `gen-page.mjs` reads `pass/reviewed.json`,
  marks each node reviewed or corrected, and shows a counter of reviewed nodes at the top. Republish
  it after each part, or sooner when the owner asks. The owner looks at it again at the end, for the
  whole at a glance.
- **When a part is done**, it is gone over whole (rules.md "A part is gone over whole"): numbering,
  agreement between rules, terms, and every decision and todo of its nodes settled.
- The decision the pass added to each node says the approval was given in the owner's name. Once the
  owner has reviewed every node, the ruling of that decision is rewritten on the nodes the owner
  approved, saying the owner approved the wording after reading it on the date of their review: a
  change of metadata, the status kept.

## How the work is done

- Never use working labels (A, B, C) in messages: name each rule by its title.
- A draft goes to the agent `specsheriff-rule-reviewer` before the owner sees it, started anew for each node so that it takes the current version of the skill `specsheriff-terminology-review`, which the owner updates (a resumed agent keeps the version it started with).
- The owner answers in the main chat. Threads on a page only point at a place; read the WHOLE thread
  at each notification, act, resolve.
- Until the hooks are confirmed running: after each edit of `specs/` run by hand `S="node
  skills/claude-code/tools/dist/cli.js"; $S reindex && $S export >/dev/null && $S render >/dev/null;
  $S validate; $S binding; $S citations`, and never leave an approved node approved over a changed
  wording. Binding reports 7.2.10's tests lagging behind its text; that report stood before this task.

## Settled with the owner

- 6.3 "One thought to a sentence, the main one first", s-juucyammgq4shq8, approved with its decision.
- 6.4 "A node holds what is applied together, and nothing applied apart", s-6j8ystpj2yzcchq, refines
  1.3, cites 6.3, approved with its decision. The old 6.3–6.10 are now 6.5–6.12.
- No base rule on paragraphs or on names. The rules of naming are a section "Naming" of the skill
  `spec-review`, approved and written, with a new step 2 there: the signals of the base rule
  "Writing" (sentence over 25 words, semicolon, parenthesis holding a second thought, paragraph over
  three sentences, node over three requirements).
- The vocabulary:
  - names: TextRule (the kind `rule`), NodeKind, RuntimeCheck, RecordType, SpecTag;
  - properties through their object, in code font: `TextRule.list`, `RecordType.fields`,
    `meta.comments`, `meta.decisions`, `meta.todos`, `meta.code`, `meta.tests`;
  - values as the schema holds them, in code font: link types, field types, `draft`/`approved`;
  - `kind` values equal the names: `section`, `term`, `header` (was `domain`), `TextRule` (was
    `rule`), `RecordType` (was `recordType`);
  - everything else an ordinary word: node, rule, section, term, domain, header, contracts,
    guidelines, tools, owner, check, test, list, field…
  - the criterion (the owner): a name only for a real term — a procedure, entity, constant, variable —
    that can be confused with the same ordinary word; as concrete as possible; a property of a named
    object written Object.property; unnecessary entities are harmful.
- The pass: every node of the guidelines brought to 6.3, 6.4 and the names before the release.

## Found and owed

- Rules 1.3.3 and 10.3 of the base say that an approval "stands", while the agreed drafts of the appendix say that it "is in force". The base is to be brought into line with the appendix the next time either rule changes (found by the review of group 6, 2026-11-24).
- Under rule 1.3.4, approving a `TableRule` also approves the nodes in its cells, while under rule 1.3.7 withdrawing its approval withdraws only the table's own approval. Whether the two rules should match is to be put to the owner once the appendix of the tools is done (found by the review of group 6, 2026-11-24).
- Rule 1.3.5 does not say whether a node's kind is part of its wording. So a rule changed into a term, which needs no code, keeps its approval, since the fingerprint is computed as rule 1.3.5 says and leaves the kind out. Whether the kind belongs to the wording is to be put to the owner once the appendix of the tools is done (found by the review of group 6, 2026-11-24).
- No rule of the base specification requires a way to remove the method from a repository, so `sheriff remove` implements no rule of its own. It is kept in the same node as `sheriff init`, "Setting a repository up and taking SpecSheriff out of it" (s-7unxytshhc4md54). A rule of the base on removing the method is to be proposed to the owner once the appendix of the tools is done (found by the review of group 6, 2026-11-24).
- The agreed drafts s-ztseyetus7np6br and s-agc6fb3vx46pc23 write "a field that has an `enum`", while the rows of table 12.12 and the drafts of group 5 write "a field with `enum`" for the same thing. The wording of those two drafts is to be brought into line with the rows of table 12.12 the next time either of those two drafts is changed (found by the review of group 5 on 2026-11-24).
- Rule 1.1.2 (s-qmmzf9nksfsa3qp) says that where the base specification is divided into sections, the technical appendix inherits its structure. Where a project has chosen the Domains extension, row 11.3.5 checks this. Where it has not, no row of table 10.1 checks that the technical appendix inherits that structure. The current code refuses a wrongly named file under `specs/tech/` even in a project without the extension (validate.ts, around `isDomain('base')`), and the draft of item 42 drops that refusal in stage 3. Before that code is changed in stage 3, the owner is to be asked whether a project without the Domains extension should get a check that enforces rule 1.1.2. The reviewers of group 5 found this gap on 2026-11-24.
- 10.5 (s-94k7b6r5kf65udf), which the owner has agreed to: its text lets a commit through whenever the reviewer gives an answer that the SpecSheriff tools can read, while its last example, "The answer passes the commit but reports a finding", describes a readable answer that leads to the commit being refused. Its title and the sentence about the refusal refer to a review that could not be carried out, and that example does not describe such a review. EngStickler's reader found this on 2026-11-20, after the owner had agreed to 10.5. It will be fixed the next time 10.5 is changed for another reason.
- 10.1 (s-jau82aq9juv9zzh) has two versions that differ: the draft in newform.json and the head in
  table-10-1.json. The owner agreed to the head on 2026-11-11. The two versions will be reconciled
  when the drafts are moved into the specification files, and the result will be shown to the owner.
- tools/6's reason is narrower than its rule: the project's generator code also triggers the derived
  files. Fixed when tools/6 is reviewed.
- tools/3's "both agents" counts what a code declaration holds (AGENTS in `init.ts`). Fixed when
  tools/3 is reviewed.

## Left, in order

1. The owner's review of every rule, above, with the splits and the doubts of each node.
2. The guidance on sections in `spec-review`, as the owner ruled on 2026-11-19 (under "Waiting for the owner" below).
3. Skills, `skills/claude-code/README.md` (with the paragraph on moving to this release, since the
   kinds are renamed), `docs/README.md`, `docs/research/landscape.md`, `_promo/` texts naming kinds:
   brought to the names. Rare other senses to rewrite there (from the vocabulary page): Node →
   Node.js; "rules of its own" of a shell → "its own syntax"; "in neutral terms" → "in neutral
   words"; "A SpecTag links a symbol" → "binds"; "an exit status" → "an exit code"; "a plain approval,
   such as ok" → "a plain ok"; "contract" in docs/README reworded.
4. Task T31 (testing the skills before each release) is carried out before the release, so that T25's release is the first one whose skills are tested. Then `npm test` is run, and the release is published according to `internal/release.md`, after the rehearsal that the same file describes. The saloon
   (`D:\specsheriff-saloon`) is then migrated by running the `sheriff reindex` of the published release.

## Waiting for the owner

The owner agreed to the whole new wording of the principle "Deterministic checks hold every participant to the contract": to its list of the reviewer's judgements on 2026-11-19 («ок»), and to its first paragraph, turned into a list, on 2026-11-20 («ок»). The owner also agreed to the new wording of the principle "Nothing unapproved is implemented" on 2026-11-20 («ок»). That wording adds the case that follows from the sixth and eighth questions of the grilling session. In it, the clauses joined by semicolons are split into separate sentences. After the owner agreed, the agent made three changes that do not alter the meaning:
- "where:" became "in each of these cases:";
- the principle now says that a rule's owners are those named by the nearest section or domain above it that names owners;
- "default owners, one or more" became "one or more default owners".

The agent wrote all of these into docs/principles.md. The commit made at the end of the session states the owner's decisions in its message, since a decision on a principle is kept only in the commit message.

The owner agreed to the new wording of node 1.3.6 (s-t5uw8zfxzhfdg86) on 2026-11-20 («ок»). It adds a third case to those in which an agent approves a node on an owner's behalf: a node of the technical appendix that the agent wrote while implementing an approved rule of the base specification, and that has an `implements` link to that rule. accept-proposal.mjs wrote the agreed wording into the draft of 1.3.6, and agree-1-3-6.mjs added the owner's rulings to the draft's `decisions` and the nodes that its text cites to the draft's `cites`. An EngStickler reader agent read the agreed English text of 1.3.6. propose-10-4.mjs then corrected, in the text of 1.3.6, the phrases the reader had found, without changing the meaning.

The owner agreed to the new wording of node 10.4 (s-nyh9yk22ba6g77q) on 2026-11-20 («принимаю»). For any commit it reviews, the reviewer is now given everything that each of its judgements requires. For a node of the technical appendix approved in a commit, the reviewer is given the rules of the base specification that the node implements, the rules linked to them in either direction, and the rules in the same top-level section. The owner chose this set of rules over the whole base specification, since giving the reviewer the whole base specification would cost the person who makes the commit more tokens. accept-proposal.mjs wrote the agreed wording into the draft of 10.4, and agree-10-4.mjs added the owner's rulings to its `decisions` and the nodes that its text cites to its `cites`. An EngStickler reader agent read the agreed English text of 10.4 before the session closed, and fix-10-4-english.mjs corrected the one phrase it found.

The owner agreed to the new wording of node 1.6.1 (s-m4h279nbaqkpa3t) on 2026-11-20 («ок»). In that wording, the paragraph on the reviewer lists the reviewer's three judgements, just as the agreed principle "Deterministic checks hold every participant to the contract" and node 10.4 do. accept-proposal.mjs wrote the agreed wording into the draft of 1.6.1, and agree-1-6-1.mjs added the owner's rulings to the draft's `decisions`, and added to the draft's `cites` the nodes that its text and reason cite. An EngStickler reader agent read the agreed English text of 1.6.1, kept in english-1-6-1.md, and agree-10-2.mjs corrected the phrases that the reader had found, without changing the meaning.

The owner agreed to the new wording of node 10.2 (s-epaw9sgyaajzn8g) on 2026-11-20 («ок»). In that wording, the review runs on every commit for which the list of the reviewer's judgements in 1.6.1 names at least one judgement, and 10.2 refers to that list rather than repeating it. accept-proposal.mjs wrote the agreed wording into the draft of 10.2, and agree-10-2.mjs added the owner's ruling to the draft's `decisions`, and added to the draft's `cites` the nodes that its text cites. An EngStickler reader agent is reading the agreed English text of 10.2, kept in english-10-2.md.

The agent is proposing, one at a time, the wordings that follow from the grilling session and are not yet agreed:
- the main table of checks, which had no row that set the cost of a failure for either of the reviewer's two new judgements: the judgement on a statement that a rule's implementation needs no change, and the judgement on an approved node of the technical appendix. The agent decided to propose two new rows, one for each new judgement, rather than broadening row 10.1.43, since each judgement can be made without the other judgements (node 7.3.3, s-6j8ystpj2yzcchq); the rule reviewer, the agent specsheriff-rule-reviewer, agreed. The owner agreed to row 10.1.43a on 2026-11-20 («ок»). It is the check in which the reviewer judges a statement that a rule's implementation needs no change, and its cost on failure is the same as that of row 10.1.40, since the statement replaces the changes that row 10.1.40 requires. An EngStickler reader agent read the English text of row 10.1.43a, kept in english-10-1-43a.md, and add-10-1-25a.mjs corrected the phrases that the reader had found, without changing the meaning. The check in the other row reads no code, so the row goes into the group "The specification alone" as row 10.1.25a. Its draft, which already includes the rule reviewer's fixes, is in draft-10-1-43ab.json. Row 10.1.25a was proposed after the third item in the principle's list of the reviewer's judgements, which is the next item of this list, was settled, since the row's scope depends on that item. The owner agreed to row 10.1.25a on 2026-11-20 («ок»); an EngStickler reader agent is reading its English text, kept in english-10-1-25a.md. The rule reviewer also found that node 10.5 requires a commit to be refused when the reviewer gives no readable answer only where the commit changes code; the item on node 10.5 below covers this finding;
- the third item in the list of the reviewer's judgements in the principle "Deterministic checks hold every participant to the contract": the owner agreed on 2026-11-20 («ок») that it applies only to a node of the technical appendix that implements a rule of the base specification, as nodes 1.3.6, 1.6.1 and 10.4 do. The agent wrote the new wording into docs/principles.md. The commit made at the end of the session states the owner's decision in its message, since a decision on a principle is kept only in the commit message;
- node 10.5, under which a commit that approves a node of the technical appendix and changes no code would not be refused when the reviewer gives no readable answer. The owner agreed to its new wording on 2026-11-20 («ок»): the SpecSheriff tools refuse a commit for which the reviewer must make at least one judgement unless the reviewer gives an answer that the tools can read. accept-proposal.mjs wrote the agreed wording into the draft of 10.5, and propose-1-3-3.mjs added the owner's ruling to the draft's `decisions`, added to the draft's `cites` the nodes that its text cites, and corrected the English of its text. An EngStickler reader agent read its English text, kept in english-10-5.md, and propose-1-3-4.mjs corrected the phrases that the reader had found, without changing the meaning;
- the reasons in 1.3.3 and 1.3.4, which say that an approval means that an owner has agreed to the rule, but no longer hold for a node approved under the third case of 1.3.6. The owner agreed to the new reason for 1.3.3 on 2026-11-20 («ок»): the status `approved` claims that the rule's present wording was approved by one of its owners or, as 1.3.6 allows, by an agent on an owner's behalf. An EngStickler reader agent is reading its English text, kept in english-1-3-3.md. The owner agreed to the new reason for 1.3.4 on 2026-11-20 («ок»): an approval means that someone accountable for a rule has agreed to what it states or, in the cases that 1.3.6 lists, that an agent has approved the rule on that person's behalf and reported it to them. An EngStickler reader agent read the English text of 1.3.3, and propose-10-1-41-reason.mjs corrected the phrases that the reader had found, without changing the meaning; another reader is reading the English text of 1.3.4, kept in english-1-3-4.md;
- row 10.1.41. On 2026-11-20 the owner rejected the proposed reason, the sentence moved word for word from the former reason for 10.1, which says that an approved rule that is not implemented is reported rather than refused. The owner pointed out that the reason speaks of approval while the row's text speaks of binding a rule to code, and that the intended meaning is the opposite: while the specification is approved, a commit that includes an approved rule without code and tests is refused; while the specification is a draft, it is normal for such a rule to have no code or tests, and nothing is reported. The agent agreed and withdrew the reason. The same defect is also present in the agreed row itself: its cost on failure is "reported under the rule's sid while the specification is approved", and it covers every approved rule rather than the rules that the commit includes, which contradicts the owner's ruling of 2026-11-19 that a check reads only what the commit contains. The sentence also cited 1.4.1 (s-rah8b37kytb682y), which says only that a rule's test is written before its code. The agent restated the meaning to the owner for confirmation: while the specification is approved, a commit that includes an approved rule that the system's code must implement is refused, and the refusal names the rule, unless the rule is bound to non-test code of the system and to a test that is not marked as skipped; a commit includes a rule when it changes the rule's wording, adds or withdraws the rule's approval, or changes the rule's tests or the code bound to the rule; while the specification is a draft, the check reports nothing; rules outside the commit are not read;
- node s-txbwsthdqhvyzd2 of the appendix of the tools.

Five texts outside the specification restate what the two principles said before their new wording. They are brought into line with the new wording in the stage that updates the skills and the guides:
- docs/README.md, in the paragraph on the checks;
- the article _promo/articles/no-code-without-a-rule.md, in its section "One judgement, by an independent reviewer";
- picture 2, _promo/images/html/2-what-stops-bad-code.html, and its entry in the table in _promo/images/README.md;
- CLAUDE.md, in the "Status" item of the "Editing the specification" section, which names a purely technical replacement as the only kind of approval the agent makes on the owner's behalf;
- the spec-review skill, wherever it says which approvals the agent makes on the owner's behalf.

## When the task closes

The closing steps of `vt` and `.claude/vt/project.md`: `elapsed.mjs --since 2026-11-06T21:12:29Z`;
the commit "SpecSheriff guidelines and tools: …", authored by the owner, no assistant attribution,
then push; the Done row in `tasks.md`; this file and `.ai_docs/t25-pass/` deleted; memory updated.
